- **Data.** The development runtime keeps its state under `<checkout>/.ohtia-dev/`, so nothing it creates reaches the researcher's `<home>/ohtia` store, and its credentials under the checkout's own credential-store service, `Ohtia Development` followed by the checkout identifier (STORY-003-001), so the development command never reads or overwrites the installed application's `Ohtia` entries or another checkout's. The data service accepts the development actions only when it, too, runs in the `development` mode, which it determines independently under ADR-0043.
- **Actions,** each added by the named story:
  - `request` (STORY-003-003), with `--ohtia-dev-model=<slug>`: sends the fixed request "Describe the usual parts of a thesis in about 300 words." through the stored ChatGPT session, with a development attempt identity;
  - `interrupt` (STORY-003-003), with `--ohtia-dev-attempt=<id>`: interrupts that request;
  - `create-development-project` (STORY-003-005): creates the development project described below, through the command `development.createProject`;
  - `start-conversation` (STORY-003-005), with `--ohtia-dev-project=<id>` and `--ohtia-dev-text=<text>`: starts a conversation with that first researcher message through `conversation.start`;
  - `send` (STORY-003-005), with `--ohtia-dev-conversation=<id>` and `--ohtia-dev-text=<text>`: appends a researcher message through `message.append`, which creates the task its project's routing assigns;
  - `resume-requests` (STORY-003-006), with `--ohtia-dev-connection=chatgpt`: closes that connection's usage pause through `usagePause.resume`;
  - `stop` and `start-again` (STORY-003-007), with `--ohtia-dev-task=<id>`, through `task.stop` and `task.startAgain`; `start-again` records the accepted warning;
  - `continue-all` (STORY-003-008), through `task.continueAll`;
  - `open-conversation` (STORY-003-010), with `--ohtia-dev-conversation=<id>`: shows that conversation in the focused window;
  - `create-project` (STORY-003-014): creates an ordinary thesis in setup through `project.create`, whose messages create `project_setup` tasks;
  - `confirm-setup` and `reject-setup` (STORY-003-014), with `--ohtia-dev-proposal=<id>`, through `project.confirmSetup` and `project.rejectSetup`;
  - `send-to-josefa` (STORY-003-020), with `--ohtia-dev-conversation=<id>` and `--ohtia-dev-text=<text>`: in a conversation of a confirmed ordinary project, appends a researcher message and creates a `search_strategy_planning` task instead of a `direct_guidance` one, through the command `development.sendToJosefa`;
  - `run-plan` (STORY-003-026), with `--ohtia-dev-project=<id>`: runs the fixed plan of SPEC-003-031 in that confirmed ordinary project, through the command `development.runPlan`.
- **Development project.** From STORY-003-005 on, `create-development-project` creates a confirmed project with `is_development` set (STORY-003-004), the title "Development project", the document language `en` and one empty conversation; more conversations come from `start-conversation`. In a development project every researcher message creates a `development_test` task answered by Virgilio, before and after STORY-003-014, so the chat stories keep using it while ordinary projects get `project_setup` and the later operations. The operation catalog accepts `development_test` only in the `development` mode.
- **Absence from packages.** The development command lives in its own module, `packages/main/src/development-command.ts`, which only the `development` mode loads. No package contains it: an artifact inspection test fails if any package built in the 12 CI cells contains that module, and a launch of each built package with `--ohtia-dev-command=request` is refused at startup.
- `development_test`, policy and instruction version 1, with the input rule that every operation of this EPIC follows (STORY-003-005): the newest whole messages up to the input limits, sent oldest first, with the English note "Earlier messages in this conversation are not included." when older ones are left out. In this story the `request` action's input is its one fixed message. Values: `max_pending_tasks` 20; `max_input_bytes` 16 KiB and `max_input_items` 20; `max_output_bytes` 16 KiB; `max_work_units` 1; `max_attempt_duration_ms` 10 minutes; `max_inactivity_ms` 120 seconds; `stop_grace_ms` 5 seconds; `max_attempts` 2, `max_auto_retries` 0 and `retry_backoff_ms` 0. STORY-003-005 enforces the task fields and STORY-003-007 the time fields. Its instructions, sent in `instructions` with every `development_test` request:

  ```text
  You are Virgilio, the research guide in Ohtia, a desktop application where researchers write research documents with the help of AI agents.

  This conversation is a development test of Ohtia's connection to the language-model service. It belongs to no research project, and nothing in it is research data.

  Reply in English to the last message. Keep replies short unless the message asks for a particular length.
  ```

**Documentation amendments:** `docs/desktop-runtime.md`, "Runtime modes": "It takes no arguments." becomes "It takes no arguments except the development command switches `--ohtia-dev-command=<action>` and `--ohtia-dev-<argument>=<value>`, which main admits only in this mode; while a window from the checkout is open, they reach that instance."

**Decisions and rationale:** The request data of this story is what `development_test` needs; STORY-003-005 adds the task fields and STORY-003-014 the tools, the first story whose operation uses them. Resume is declared unsupported rather than emulated. Provider requests leave from main so that the data service, which decides them, never holds the credential they need. The development command is a switch of the unpackaged development runtime, with its own data root, so it can never reach an installed application or its data.[^adr-0043]

**Verification:** The `request` action sends the fixed request with a chosen model, using the researcher's stored session, and receives the real streamed reply; `interrupt` stops another request mid-reply and gets `cancelled`. With the fake provider imitating OpenAI's documented responses, every case of "Errors and outcomes" yields its result and reason, and `chatpass_v2_scope_not_authorized` leaves the connection in `error` with the reason `sign_in_rejected` after a restart; a rejected renewal sends nothing and returns `connection_expired` with the connection `expired` or `error`, and a temporary renewal failure sends nothing and returns `provider_unavailable` with the connection still `connected`; a permit older than 2 seconds at the first check, and one that a slow renewal ages past 2 seconds before the second check, are refused with `permit_expired` and send nothing; a connection that is no longer connected at its check sends nothing and returns `connection_expired`; an unparsable event in the stream fails with `invalid_output` and keeps the text received before it; a reused permit and a dispatch from an ended generation are discarded; ending the data service closes its requests; and every `development_test` request carries the instructions above. The artifact inspection test proves the development command is absent from every package. The extended conformance suite passes, including the check that no request carries `previous_response_id`, `conversation`, `prompt` or a tool other than Ohtia's own. Automated tests run in the 12 CI cells and the real-account check on the x86-64 virtual machines.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-009: Project and conversation records

Add the first research tables and the data-service operations that use them, without a window yet.[^data-model][^adr-0008][^adr-0010]

Tables:

- `projects`:
  - `id`: UUIDv7 stored as a 16-byte BLOB;
  - `project_type`: only `thesis`;
  - `title`: 1 to 200 Unicode code points without leading or trailing whitespace;
  - `document_language`: a BCP 47 tag of the form `language[-script][-region]` and nothing more: a language subtag of 2 or 3 letters, an optional script subtag of 4 letters and an optional region subtag of 2 letters or 3 digits, with no variant, extension, private-use, extended-language or grandfathered part.[^rfc5646] It is stored in canonical case (language lowercase, script with an initial capital, region uppercase, as in `sr-Latn-RS` or `es-419`), which the database checks; BCP 47 tags carry no meaning in case, so the data service converts a received tag to that case before every check.[^rfc5646] The data service checks each subtag against the bundled IANA Language Subtag Registry: it must exist with the matching `Type` (`language`, `script` or `region`) and carry no `Deprecated` field;
  - `is_development`: 1 only for the development project that the development command creates from STORY-003-005 on, and 0 for every project this story's operations create; set at creation and immutable;
  - `setup_confirmed_at`;
  - `created_at` and `updated_at`: UTC with milliseconds, such as `2026-11-08T14:03:12.345Z`.

  `title`, `document_language` and `setup_confirmed_at` are either all empty, while the project is in setup, or all set; they are written together on confirmation.

- `conversations`:
  - `id`;
  - `project_id`: required and immutable;
  - `title`: empty until named, then 1 to 200 Unicode code points;
  - `created_at`;
  - `updated_at`: equal to `created_at` until the first rename, then changed only by renames; each rename writes a value later than the previous one, adding a millisecond when the clock gives the same or an earlier time;
  - `revision`: starts at 1 and advances by one in every transaction that renames the conversation or saves a message in it, and, from STORY-003-005 on, in every transaction that changes one of its tasks. The research subscription and the conversation reads carry it (STORY-003-010).

  A project in setup has exactly one conversation. A rename carries the `updated_at` it was based on as its expected version, as `architecture.md` requires for a change to existing data; when another rename was saved first, the rename is rejected with `stale_revision`, whose `currentRevision` is the conversation's current `revision`, and nothing changes. A message saved meanwhile changes `revision` but not `updated_at`, so it never makes a rename conflict.[^architecture][^workspace]

- `messages`:
  - `id` and `conversation_id`;
  - `position`: the message's place in the conversation, a positive integer, unique and increasing within the conversation but not necessarily consecutive. A researcher message takes the position after the highest one used or reserved in its conversation; from STORY-003-005 on, an agent reply takes the position its task reserved when it started, so some positions stay unused;
  - `author_kind`: `researcher` or `agent`;
  - `agent_id`: one of the seven catalog agents (`virgilio`, `josefa`, `jorge-luis`, `erasmus`, `boris`, `maria`, `thomas`) for agent messages, empty for researcher messages;
  - `content` and `created_at`.

  Messages are immutable. The database rejects empty content and sets no length limit; the composer limits researcher messages (STORY-003-012) and each operation limits agent output.

- `command_receipts`:
  - `operation_id`: generated by the caller and unique: the window, main for the command it sends (STORY-003-009), or the development command;
  - `command`: the operation name, one of the data-changing commands that the data service accepts: in this story `project.create`, `project.confirmSetup`, `conversation.start`, `conversation.rename` and `message.append`, and in each later story the commands its block names (SPEC-003-002);
  - `payload_sha256`: the 32-byte SHA-256 of the command's payload, the port request's `payload` object without the envelope, serialized as RFC 8785 canonical JSON;[^rfc8785]
  - `result_id`: the row created or changed;
  - `created_at`.

  Repeating an operation returns its first result; reusing an identifier with different content is rejected. `data-model.md` and ADR-0011 require this. Only accepted commands are stored: a command rejected by validation, a precondition or a conflict writes no receipt, so repeating it checks it again against the current data.

The repository ships no copy of the IANA Language Subtag Registry yet. This story adds it as `config/localization/iana-language-subtag-registry.txt`: IANA's file copied byte for byte when the story is implemented, in its record format (`Type`, `Subtag`, `Deprecated` and the other fields, with records separated by `%%`). Its `File-Date` line identifies the copy, and the pull request records that date and the file's SHA-256; on 2026-11-10 the published file has `File-Date: 2026-09-17`.[^iana-subtags] Every operation that receives a tag, such as confirming setup, applies the same case conversion and registry checks.

That copy measured 731,819 bytes, within the 1024 KB that `check-added-large-files` allows, a hook that `.pre-commit-config.yaml` runs with `--enforce-all`, so that hook needs no exclusion.[^pre-commit-hooks] It ends with a single line feed and has no trailing whitespace and no carriage return, so `end-of-file-fixer`, `trailing-whitespace` and `mixed-line-ending` leave its bytes unchanged and need no exclusion either; `.gitattributes` keeps its line feeds (`* text=auto eol=lf`).

If the copy taken at implementation does not end with exactly one line feed, or has trailing whitespace, the story gives `end-of-file-fixer` or `trailing-whitespace`, whichever would change it, an `exclude` of that one path with the comment "# IANA's Language Subtag Registry, copied byte for byte; its File-Date line identifies the copy.", indented eight spaces, so its bytes stay IANA's.

Identifiers are generated with Node's `crypto.randomUUIDv7()`, present in Electron 45's Node 24.22.0 and the development Node 26.12.1, so no library is added.

Data-changing operations:

- `project.create`: create a project in setup together with its empty setup conversation, in one transaction;
- `project.confirmSetup`: confirm setup, writing title, document language and confirmation time; fails with `project_not_in_setup` if the project is already confirmed;
- `conversation.start`: create a conversation in a confirmed project together with its first researcher message, in one transaction with one operation identifier, so that no conversation the researcher starts is ever empty and a failure creates nothing; fails with `project_in_setup` in a project in setup;
- `conversation.rename`: rename a conversation, with the expected `updated_at`; a name outside the `title` rules fails with `name_invalid`;
- `message.append`: append a researcher message after the highest position used or reserved in the conversation.

`conversation.start` and `message.append` reject an empty researcher message with `message_invalid`. A command whose identifier names no project, conversation or other record it needs fails with `record_not_found`. Each of these rejections saves nothing. This story adds these codes to the port's closed `errorActions` in `packages/contracts/src/port.ts`, with the next actions that SPEC-003-015 lists, including the new `refresh_record`, because the data service already returns port errors; a rename conflict uses the existing `stale_revision`.

Read operations: `project.list`, the projects, including those in setup; `conversation.list`, a project's conversations ordered by their latest message, the one at the highest position; `message.list`, a conversation's messages from a given position.

Migration `0007` creates the four tables through the existing ADR-0010 path: Ohtia takes the checkpoint before migrating; a failed migration rolls back, keeps the source database and leaves the data gate closed until the existing explicit retry or restore.[^adr-0010]

**Documentation amendments:**

- `docs/data-model.md`: in the diagram, `text analysis_language` becomes `text document_language`; the project fields sentence becomes "`title`, document language and observed creation/update metadata describe the investigation; the document language is the BCP 47 language of the document's text, confirmed by the researcher during setup, in which agents work and answer."; "Interface locale and manuscript language remain separate from project analysis language." becomes "Interface locale remains separate from the project's document language, which is also the manuscript's language."; "not an inferred equality with analysis language" becomes "which starts as the project's document language"; and "Changing the project analysis language or interface locale never relabels previous or current manuscript text." becomes "Changing the project's document language or the interface locale never relabels previous or current manuscript text."
- `docs/product-definition.md`: "Interface translation, analysis language, source language and manuscript language are separate settings." becomes "Interface translation, the project's document language and source language are separate settings; the document language is also the manuscript's language."
- `docs/thesis-workflow.md`: the sentence requiring an explicit analysis-language selection becomes "During setup, Virgilio proposes the project's document language, the language of the thesis text in which agents work and answer, and the researcher confirms it. Keep it distinct from the OS-derived or user-overridden interface locale."; the "Analysis language" item becomes "**Document language:** the language of the thesis text, confirmed at setup; agents work and answer in it; it is distinct from the application locale."
- `docs/frontend-architecture.md`, `docs/design-system.md`, `docs/agents/agent-collaboration.md`, `docs/agents/agent-virgilio.md`, `docs/agents/agent-thomas.md` and `docs/product-roadmap.md`: every remaining "analysis language" becomes "document language", with the same meaning, except the two `docs/product-roadmap.md` passages of the next item.
- `docs/product-roadmap.md`, "Settings grows with the product": the row "Analysis language and research profile | Research project" becomes "Research profile | Research project", keeping its third cell, and above it the new row "Document language | Research project | EPIC-003" is added, both aligned with the table's columns; and in the onboarding entry that follows the EPIC-003 entry, "explicitly selected analysis language before assistance" becomes "the project's document language, confirmed at setup".
- `docs/epics/EPIC-002-basic-interface/epic.md`, SPEC-002-010: "no dead Connections, Diagnostics, Storage, analysis-language or concurrency controls are supplied now" becomes "no dead Connections, Diagnostics, Storage, document-language or concurrency controls are supplied now".
- `docs/localization.md`: "Help text distinguishes interface language from document and analysis language." becomes "Help text distinguishes interface language from the language of documents and analyses, which is the project's document language."
- ADR-0024, an editorial correction that keeps its identity under `docs/architecture-decisions.md`: "Source/manuscript language and project analysis language remain separate inputs; changing UI locale does not translate existing research." becomes "Source language and the project's document language, which is also the manuscript's language, remain separate inputs; changing UI locale does not translate existing research."; and "The interface locale never supplies an implicit manuscript or analysis language." becomes "The interface locale never supplies an implicit manuscript or document language."[^architecture-decisions]
- `docs/epics/EPIC-002-basic-interface/epic.md`, SPEC-002-019: "source pixels, analysis language or export settings" becomes "source pixels, document language or export settings". After these changes no "analysis language" or "analysis-language" remains in `docs/`, in any letter case, apart from this EPIC's own concept files, which quote the replaced texts.
- ADR-0008: Ohtia generates UUIDv7 values with Node's `crypto.randomUUIDv7()`.

**Decisions and rationale:** Command receipts make every creating or changing operation idempotent, which double clicks and lost replies need. The database enforces closed lists fixed by an ADR; the data service validates lists that grow with each story. `document_language` replaces `data-model.md`'s `analysis_language`: the researcher decided that agents work in the language of the document's text, and the name stays generic over project types. The tag grammar admits scripts and numeric regions, such as `sr-Latn` and `es-419`, because a document's writing system and regional variety can matter; deprecated subtags are rejected so each language has one stored form. Rejected commands are not stored, because a repeat should be judged against the current data. A conflicting rename keeps the typed name, as `workspace-layout.md` requires.[^workspace]

**Verification:** Automated tests on a real SQLite database check that each rule rejects invalid data and each operation works; a repeated operation returns the same result and a reused identifier with other content is rejected; the payload hash is the same for two payloads that differ only in key order; a rejected command leaves no receipt; `en`, `ast`, `sr-Latn`, `es-419` and `pt-BR` are accepted, `ES-ar` is stored as `es-AR`, and `iw`, `en-US-x-test`, `zh-min-nan` and an unknown subtag are rejected; a rename with a stale `updated_at` is rejected with `stale_revision` and changes nothing, while a message saved between two renames advances `revision` without a conflict; `conversation.start` in a project in setup is rejected with `project_in_setup`, confirming a confirmed project with `project_not_in_setup`, an empty message with `message_invalid` and an unknown conversation with `record_not_found`, and a start that fails leaves neither a conversation nor a message; researcher messages take increasing positions; an existing version-0006 database upgrades and keeps its preferences; a failing migration rolls back, keeps the source database and closes the data gate. The packaged application opens and upgrades an existing database. Automated tests run in the 12 CI cells.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-010: Durable AI tasks

A task is one accepted request for an operation with fixed inputs; each execution is an attempt.[^adr-0011]

`tasks`:

- `id`, `project_id` and `conversation_id`, all immutable;
- `input_through_position`: the task covers the conversation's messages up to this position, the position of the last message it answers. That set is final when the task starts: only the conversation's running reply or consultation task (STORY-003-022), or a plan's contribution or synthesis task (STORY-003-023 and STORY-003-025), can still save a message below a later position, and a reply already running does not receive it. Saving a reply replaces any waiting task (Coalescing); the blocks of those stories state when their saved results do. The operation's input rule selects what is sent, and `attempts.transfer_manifest` records it;
- `reply_position`: empty until the task first moves to `running`; then the position reserved for its reply, the one after the highest position used or reserved in the conversation. It stays with the task across its attempts and is unique among the conversation's messages and reservations;
- `operation`, validated against the operation catalog, `policy_version` and `instruction_version`;
- `connection`, validated against the connections;
- `document_language`: the project's `document_language` when the task was accepted, or empty when the project was still in setup; immutable. An agent message takes its language from the task that produced it (STORY-003-010);
- `accepted_by_operation_id`, pointing to `command_receipts`: the researcher command that caused the task; a task that replaces a waiting one because a reply was saved inherits the replaced task's value;
- `replaces_task_id`: the task this one replaces, set when starting an uncertain task again and when coalescing cancels a not-started task;
- `state`: the eight ADR-0011 task states (`queued`, `blocked`, `running`, `cancelling`, `completed`, `failed`, `cancelled`, `uncertain`), enforced by the database;
- `blocking_reason`: set only while `blocked`; enforced by the database with the values `connection_disabled`, `permission_required`, `input_changed`, `configuration_unavailable`, `restart_paused` and `usage_limit_reached`, of which STORY-003-006 and STORY-003-008 set the last two; ADR-0011's `shutdown_paused` is never used (STORY-003-009);
- `state_revision`: starts at 1, advances by one in every transaction that changes the task, and orders concurrent changes;
- `created_at`, which sets queue order with `id` as tie-break, and `updated_at`.

`attempts`:

- `id`, `task_id` and `attempt_number`, unique within the task;
- `service_generation`: the data-service start that owns the attempt;
- `state`: the eight ADR-0011 attempt states (`prepared`, `running`, `waiting_user`, `stopping`, `completed`, `failed`, `cancelled`, `uncertain`);
- `outcome_reason`, set only when failed, cancelled or uncertain: `usage_limit_reached`, `not_eligible`, `connection_expired`, `provider_unavailable`, `request_rejected`, `provider_error`, `invalid_output`, `output_limit_exceeded`, `connection_lost`, `empty_reply` and `permit_expired` in this story; `inactivity_timeout`, `deadline_exceeded` and `cancelled_by_researcher` in STORY-003-007; `service_restarted` in STORY-003-008;
- `requested_model` and `observed_model`;
- `dispatch_permitted_at`: the time the data service issued the attempt's dispatch permit, written in the dispatch transaction that issues it, which commits before the data service posts `provider_dispatch` to main (STORY-003-003), and cleared only in the transaction that records a not-dispatched result from main (`permit_expired`, `connection_expired` or `provider_unavailable`). An empty value therefore proves that the request never left; a set value means that it may have left, so a crash between that commit and the send leaves the attempt to the rules for a dispatched attempt (STORY-003-008) and never to a second attempt;
- `transfer_manifest`: references to messages and versions, never text;
- `provider_request_id`;
- `input_tokens`, `cached_input_tokens`, `output_tokens` and `reasoning_tokens`, where empty means unknown;
- `created_at`, `started_at` and `finished_at`.

An attempt has no revision of its own because it always changes in the same transaction as its task. `messages.task_id` links an agent message to the task that produced it.

**Receipts.** ADR-0011's "Preserve immutable attempt outcomes, input identities and transition receipts" is met by these records, and no separate log of every task state change is kept: the `command_receipts` row of each accepted command; each task's immutable fields, `input_through_position`, `accepted_by_operation_id` and `replaces_task_id`, which identify its input and what caused it; each attempt row, whose `state`, `outcome_reason`, `requested_model`, `observed_model`, `dispatch_permitted_at`, `transfer_manifest`, `provider_request_id`, `started_at` and `finished_at` never change once the attempt reaches `completed`, `failed`, `cancelled` or `uncertain`, except that its token fields show the latest accepted usage report, whose reports STORY-003-034 keeps as their own immutable rows; a terminal task, which never changes again; and `state_revision`, which orders each task's transitions. A new attempt is a new row, never an earlier attempt rewritten.

Rules:

- **Acceptance.** A task is accepted in the same transaction that stores what caused it, such as the researcher's message.
- **Concurrency.** Several tasks run at once within two limits: the general inference limit, whose default becomes 10 (the migration changes the stored value from 1 to 10 and advances its revision only while the record is still the seeded revision 1, so a saved choice is preserved; local processing stays 1), and the connection's declared limit (ChatGPT declares 3, checked with the researcher's account). Queue order is acceptance order.
- **One per conversation.** The database allows at most one running or cancelling task and at most one not-started task per conversation.
- **Coalescing.** A new researcher message in a conversation that already has a not-started task cancels that task and creates one task covering all messages up to the new one, with `replaces_task_id` pointing to the cancelled task. When a reply is saved while a not-started task waits, that task is replaced in the same transaction so that its input includes the reply: the new task keeps the waiting task's `input_through_position` and `accepted_by_operation_id`, and `replaces_task_id` points to it. The replacement needs no new researcher command because the only added input is the reply to the researcher's own messages; the new ADR of this story records this exception to ADR-0011's rule that changed input needs a new accepted request.
- **Reply position.** The transaction that first moves a task to `running` reserves its `reply_position`. Researcher messages written while it runs take later positions, so they appear below the reply. The reply, complete or partial, is saved at the reserved position. If the task ends without text, no message takes that position and nothing is shown there except the task's notice, when it has one (STORY-003-013).
- **Saving the reply.** The reply becomes an agent message with its `task_id` at the task's `reply_position`, in the same transaction that completes the task; reported tokens are recorded on the attempt.
- **Conversation revision.** Every transaction that changes a task, including the one that saves its reply, also advances its conversation's `revision` (STORY-003-004) by one.
- **Empty reply.** A provider result that completes without any text fails the attempt with `empty_reply`: no message is saved, because the database rejects empty content, and the task's notice takes its reply position (STORY-003-013). STORY-003-014 extends the rule to replies that carry tool calls.
- **Input.** Every operation of this EPIC builds its input this way unless its block states otherwise. Its instructions go in `instructions`. The input holds the conversation's messages up to the task's `input_through_position`, taken whole from the newest backward until the next one would not fit within `max_input_bytes`, counted as the UTF-8 bytes of their text, or within `max_input_items`, one item per message; they are sent oldest first. When older messages are left out, the first item is the note "Earlier messages in this conversation are not included." Every note that Ohtia adds for the model is an English input item with the `user` role, placed where its block states, and counts toward neither limit. A message is never cut. `attempts.transfer_manifest` records the messages sent, by identifier. Notes are built when each attempt's input is built, from the records as they are at that moment, and the attempt's `transfer_manifest` records each note it carried by its kind and the identifier and revision of the record it describes; the manifest is written in the dispatch transaction and never changes afterward, so every dispatched input is recorded and immutable, which is how this EPIC meets ADR-0011's immutable input manifest. A later attempt of the same task builds its notes again and records them in its own manifest.
- **Message too large.** A researcher message whose UTF-8 bytes alone exceed the `max_input_bytes` of the operation whose task it would create could never be sent whole, so `conversation.start` and `message.append` reject it with the controlled error `input_too_large` and save nothing.
- **Output limit.** `max_output_bytes` is one budget for the whole output of an attempt: the UTF-8 bytes of its text and, from STORY-003-014 on, the UTF-8 bytes of its tool-call arguments, counted together as they stream. The data service counts the reply's UTF-8 bytes as its text arrives. When a part would take the reply past `max_output_bytes`, it sends `provider_interrupt`, keeps the longest beginning of the text that fits within `max_output_bytes` and ends at a whole code point, and fails the attempt with `output_limit_exceeded`; the kept text is saved as the reply, which STORY-003-007 marks `partial`. From STORY-003-014 on, main also enforces the whole budget, text and tool-call arguments together, with one count per attempt that never decreases, emits an attempt's tool-call events only after the provider completes the response within that budget, and returns the attempt failed with `output_limit_exceeded`, emitting none of its tool-call events, when the budget is passed (STORY-003-014); the data service handles that result in the same way, and a failed attempt applies none of its tool calls.
- **Work units.** One per dispatched attempt (SPEC-003-004). The data service never issues a dispatch permit for a task whose dispatched attempts already equal its `max_work_units`.
- **Pending limit.** An operation's queued and blocked tasks, in every project, count toward its `max_pending_tasks`. A command that would add one more is rejected with the controlled error `pending_limit_reached` and saves nothing, neither its message nor its task, as ADR-0011 requires a request over its admission cap to be rejected before acceptance.[^adr-0011] In this story those commands are `conversation.start` and `message.append`; each later command that creates a task follows the same rule. A task that replaces a not-started task in the same transaction adds none, so a message that coalesces (Coalescing) is accepted at the limit, and so is the replacement made when a reply is saved. This story adds `input_too_large`, next action `check_request`, and `pending_limit_reached`, next action `retry_later`, to the port's closed `errorActions` (SPEC-003-015).
- **Refused permit.** An attempt that main returns not dispatched with `permit_expired` (STORY-003-003) becomes `cancelled` with that reason, and the same transaction clears its `dispatch_permitted_at`; its task returns to `queued` in its original order, keeps its `reply_position` and runs its second attempt. A task with no attempt left fails with `permit_expired`.
- **Returning to not started.** A running task whose attempt never dispatched can return to not started: here after a refused permit, in STORY-003-006 after a rejected renewal and in STORY-003-008 after an orderly stop or a crash. If its conversation already has a not-started task, that task covers the same messages and later ones, so the returning task is cancelled instead, as by coalescing: it shows no notice, its reservation stays unused, and the one-not-started rule (One per conversation) holds.
- **Model.** OpenAI names no default model and asks clients to keep the order of the list returned for the account.[^siwc-models] Until STORY-003-035, every task requests the first model of its connection's model list. Main reads that list when the connection becomes `connected` and again whenever the list it holds is older than one day, and reports each list to the data service with the connection's state (STORY-003-001). The dispatch transaction writes to `attempts.requested_model` the slug of the first entry whose `visibility` is `list`, in the order of the latest reported list, and the request data carries it (STORY-003-003); `observed_model` records the model the provider reports, or stays empty when it reports none. While the latest list main reported for a task's connection has no such entry, the data service issues that task no dispatch permit: the transaction that would issue it fails the attempt with `provider_unavailable` and no `dispatch_permitted_at`, so it uses no work unit, and fails the task with that reason, whose notice offers "Try again" (STORY-003-013). This story adds the versioned, runtime-validated service-channel message `model_list_refresh` in `packages/contracts`, from the data service, with the connection identifier: main reads that connection's model list again, as on connection, and reports it with the connection's state, or reports the list it holds when the read fails. STORY-003-013's "Try again" sends it before its new task can receive a permit.
- **Development project.** This story adds the `create-development-project`, `start-conversation` and `send` actions of the development command, the development-only command `development.createProject` and the `development_test` routing of development projects (STORY-003-003). From this story on, `conversation.start` and `message.append` accept the task their message causes in the same transaction.
- **Session renewal.** One renewal serves all running tasks; no task loses its session.
- **Uncertain.** An uncertain attempt quarantines its slot under ADR-0011 and is never repeated automatically; the other tasks continue. STORY-003-007 adds the cessation rule and the replacement.

**Documentation amendments:**

- Add `docs/adr/adr-NNNN-research-agent-task-execution.md`, titled "ADR-NNNN — Research-agent task execution", with the description "Replace the ADR-0011, ADR-0028 and ADR-0037 rules on concurrency, cessation, readmission, restart, exit, planning and evaluation that Ohtia's provider-backed tasks change." Following `docs/architecture-decisions.md`, it is a new record for materially different choices and links to each passage it replaces, while ADR-0011, ADR-0028 and ADR-0037 keep their earlier rationale with a replacement link.[^architecture-decisions] Its context states that this EPIC's providers offer no background execution or cancellation endpoint, that Ohtia is a desktop application the researcher closes and reopens, and that the researcher chose each value below. Its decision records these choices, each with the passage it replaces:
  - `max_concurrent_inference` defaults to 10 and accepts whole numbers from 1 to 50, the only concurrency control in Settings; `max_concurrent_local_processing` stays a stored preference with the value 1 until a feature uses local processing; a connection's declared limit still caps admission. It replaces ADR-0011's sentence on the two Settings limits and "Both default to **1** and accept positive integers."
  - The required policy field `max_inactivity_ms`, the longest time without any provider event before an attempt stops, added to ADR-0011's list of policy fields.
  - For an adapter without background execution or a cancellation endpoint, closing the connection proves that nothing more can arrive: a stopped attempt becomes `cancelled`, an exceeded limit `failed`, a lost connection `uncertain` without holding its slot, and usage stays unknown. It replaces, for those adapters only, ADR-0011's requirement of an adapter-supported cancellation or inspection result and its quarantine of an uncertain attempt's slot.
  - The blocking reason `usage_limit_reached`, opened by a connection's usage pause and cleared only by the researcher's "Resume {service} requests", and the transition from `queued` to `blocked` when a disconnection or a usage pause blocks queued work, added to ADR-0011's blocking codes and transitions.
  - When an agent message is saved while a not-started reply task of the same conversation waits, whether a reply or the result of a consultation or plan contribution that the researcher approved, that task is replaced by one with the same researcher acceptance whose input adds that message. This is the one exception to ADR-0011's "Changed input needs a new explicitly accepted request."
  - After an orderly exit, accepted not-started tasks continue by themselves in order at the next start; after a crash, power loss or forced quit they acquire `restart_paused`, and one "Continue all" resumes them after their prerequisites and policy are revalidated. It replaces ADR-0011's "Accepted not-started tasks acquire `restart_paused`; the user explicitly continues them after their prerequisites/policy are revalidated."
  - An attempt proved never to have dispatched, with no `dispatch_permitted_at`, is recorded `cancelled`, and its task runs its second attempt without a new researcher request: after an orderly stop it is requeued with `service_restarted`, after main refuses an expired dispatch permit it is requeued with `permit_expired`, and after a rejected renewal it is blocked and requeued on reconnection; after a crash it waits for "Continue all". `max_attempts` bounds these attempts, which do not count against `max_auto_retries`; that stays 0, so nothing that was sent is ever repeated by itself. It replaces, for these cases, ADR-0011's "If zero dispatch and local cessation are proved, record the interrupted attempt and leave its task blocked for explicit continuation within the remaining original attempt/work limits." and its counting of such a run as an automatic retry, and ADR-0028's "Proven zero dispatch permits a complete readmission within the operation's single retry budget; uncertain dispatch does not." with "Proven zero dispatch permits one complete readmission within the operation's `max_attempts`, which does not count against its `max_auto_retries`; uncertain dispatch does not." The data service writes `dispatch_permitted_at` in the transaction that issues the permit and commits it before main can send, so only an empty value proves zero dispatch.
  - Transition receipts are the records that STORY-003-005's "Receipts" rule names: command receipts, the immutable task fields, attempt rows that never change once final apart from their latest usage report, and each task's `state_revision`; no separate log of every task state change is kept. It states how ADR-0011's "Preserve immutable attempt outcomes, input identities and transition receipts." is met, without replacing it.
  - Ohtia's notes to the model, about omitted messages, proposals, plans and memory, are built when each attempt's input is built, from the records at that moment, and recorded in that attempt's `transfer_manifest`, which is immutable once the attempt is dispatched. Every input sent is therefore an immutable, recorded input manifest, as ADR-0011's "A **task** is one accepted request for an operation on an immutable input manifest under a versioned policy." requires; it states how that rule is met, without replacing it.
  - Application exit uses no `shutdown_paused`: while Ohtia waits to quit, not-started tasks stay queued for the next start; "Quit with unresolved remote work" does not exist; uncertain tasks do not open the exit dialog; and an operating-system shutdown or logout exits without a dialog, so the next start applies the crash rules. It replaces the matching parts of ADR-0011's "Application exit".
  - Serialized execution remains correct when inference concurrency is set to one. It replaces ADR-0037's "Serialized execution remains useful under the default concurrency of one."
  - Ohtia does not grade the models researchers connect, so collaboration is not evaluated against a single-agent baseline. It replaces ADR-0037's requirement of that comparison and its trigger to revisit the choice on measured research benefit.
  - Specialist work and synthesis run as separately bounded tasks under ADR-0011; planning happens inside the coordinator's direct-guidance turn, and the researcher approves the concrete plan before any work runs. It replaces ADR-0037's "Run planning, specialist work and synthesis as separately bounded tasks under ADR-0011."

  Its consequences: up to ten replies share a provider's limits at once; a closed connection may still be billed, so its usage stays unknown; queued work resumes after an orderly exit without a new request, which the exit dialog announces; and the benefit of coordination over a single agent is not measured. Each later story of this EPIC implements the choices that concern it: STORY-003-006, STORY-003-007, STORY-003-008, STORY-003-009, STORY-003-014, STORY-003-019 and STORY-003-027.

- ADR-0011: at the start of "Task states and transitions", "Attempt lifecycle and completion evidence", "Scheduling and required limits", "Application exit", "Restart, suspension and unresolved work" and "Consequences", add "ADR-NNNN replaces parts of this section and names each one. The text below keeps the earlier choice and its rationale.", with "ADR-NNNN" linked to `adr-NNNN-research-agent-task-execution.md`
- ADR-0037: add the same sentence at the start of "Context", "Decision" and "Consequences".
- ADR-0028: add the same sentence at the start of "Decision", with "ADR-NNNN" linked to `adr-NNNN-research-agent-task-execution.md`.
- `docs/adr/index.md`: add the new ADR's entry with its title and description.
- `docs/frontend-architecture.md`, "Connections, tasks and consumption": "Application Settings provides separate concurrency controls for inference and local processing, both defaulting to 1." becomes "Application Settings provides separate concurrency limits for inference, defaulting to 10, and for local processing, defaulting to 1."
- `docs/architecture.md`: "configurable concurrency defaulting to one inference and one local-processing attempt" becomes "configurable concurrency defaulting to ten inference attempts and one local-processing attempt".
- `docs/product-roadmap.md`, shared acceptance rules: "Global inference/local-processing concurrency defaults to one each and respects adapter limits" becomes "Global inference concurrency defaults to ten and local-processing concurrency to one, and both respect adapter limits".

The migration keeps `docs/architecture.md`'s rule "Preserve an existing valid limit record rather than resetting it": it changes only the seeded value.

**Decisions and rationale:** One running task per conversation, coalescing and closed failure codes were adopted after comparing Paperclip, Multica and OpenClaw. Lists fixed by an ADR are database constraints; growing lists are validated by the data service so that adding an operation does not rebuild a table.[^paperclip][^multica][^openclaw] A reply stays where it appeared, right below the last message it answers, and messages written while it ran go below it: the screen does not jump, nobody reads the reply as an answer to the later message, and the next request ends with the researcher's message, which current Anthropic models require, because they reject a request whose last message is the assistant's.[^anthropic-prefill] One new ADR replaces the changed parts of ADR-0011, ADR-0028 and ADR-0037 because `docs/architecture-decisions.md` gives a materially different choice a new record; it is written here, with the task engine, because every later rule builds on that engine.[^architecture-decisions][^adr-0011][^adr-0037]

**Verification:** With the development command and the researcher's account, three conversations of a development project run at once and a fourth waits; two messages sent while a reply streams produce one reply that sees the previous one. With the fake provider, one renewal with three running tasks happens once; each documented OpenAI error maps to its code; an uncertain task holds its slot while others continue; a message sent while a reply runs takes a position after the reply's reserved one, and the reply is saved at its reservation; a task that ends without text leaves its reservation unused; a completed result without text fails with `empty_reply` and saves no message; the replacement made when a reply is saved keeps the waiting task's acceptance; a conversation over the input limits sends the newest whole messages, oldest first, after the omission note, and the transfer manifest lists them; a researcher message over the operation's `max_input_bytes` is rejected with `input_too_large` and saves nothing; a reply that passes `max_output_bytes` is interrupted, keeps its text up to the limit at a whole code point and fails with `output_limit_exceeded`; at `max_pending_tasks` a new message is rejected with `pending_limit_reached` and saves nothing, while a message that coalesces is accepted; a permit refused with `permit_expired` requeues the task for its second attempt, which then runs, and a second refusal fails it; `dispatch_permitted_at` is committed before `provider_dispatch` is posted and cleared by every not-dispatched result, and a crash between that commit and the send leaves the attempt with its value; each task requests the slug of the first entry whose `visibility` is `list` in the list main reported, and records it in `requested_model`, also when an earlier entry is hidden; while the list has no such entry, a task fails with `provider_unavailable` without a dispatch permit or a work unit; and `model_list_refresh` makes main read the list again and report it, or report the list it holds when the read fails; a final attempt row never changes except for its token fields; every task change advances the task's `state_revision` and its conversation's `revision` by one; and a task records its project's document language at acceptance, and none in a project in setup. Automated tests cover every state change and database rule in the 12 CI cells.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-011: Blocked work and plan-limit pause

Handle a connection that becomes unavailable and a ChatGPT plan that runs out.[^adr-0011][^siwc-errors]

- **Connection unavailable.** When ChatGPT becomes disconnected, connecting, expired or errored, every state but `connected` (STORY-003-001), queued tasks move from `queued` to `blocked` with `connection_disabled`, and new tasks are accepted already blocked. Running tasks continue; if the session no longer works after their request left, they fail with `connection_expired`. If OpenAI rejects the renewal just before dispatch, or main finds the connection no longer connected at its dispatch check (STORY-003-003), nothing was sent: the attempt ends `cancelled` with `connection_expired` and no `dispatch_permitted_at`, and its task becomes `blocked` with `connection_disabled`, as ADR-0011 requires when a prerequisite disappears before dispatch, unless its conversation already has a not-started task, in which case it is cancelled instead (STORY-003-005); on reconnection it is requeued and runs its second attempt.[^adr-0011] If that renewal fails temporarily (no network or a 5xx response), the connection stays `connected` and nothing was sent: the attempt fails with `provider_unavailable` and no `dispatch_permitted_at`, and its task fails with that reason, whose notice offers "Try again" (STORY-003-013); no other task is blocked. When ChatGPT is connected again, each task is revalidated and requeued in its original order; a task that fails revalidation stays blocked with `connection_disabled`, or with `usage_limit_reached` while a plan pause is open.
- **Plan exhausted.** When an attempt fails with `usage_limit_reached`, the same transaction opens a ChatGPT pause and blocks waiting tasks with `usage_limit_reached`; new tasks are accepted already blocked. Running tasks continue; if they fail with the same code they fail and are not retried. OpenAI asks clients to pause new requests and not to infer a reset time.[^siwc-errors]
- **Resume requests.** The command `usagePause.resume`, with the connection and its operation identifier, closes that connection's open pause and requeues, in order, every task it blocked, in every project. With no open pause for that connection, as when another window resumed it first, the command succeeds without change and records its receipt. STORY-003-013 shows it as the button "Resume {service} requests ({n})", where `{n}` is the number of tasks the pause holds, and until then the development command's `resume-requests` action runs it (STORY-003-003). If the plan is still exhausted, the first failure opens a new pause; tasks already dispatched fail with the same code and are not retried.
- **Both at once.** A disconnection during a pause blocks tasks with `connection_disabled`; on reconnection they requeue only if no pause is open, otherwise they stay blocked by the plan.
- **Coalescing** also applies to blocked tasks, because they have not started.
- **`usage_limit_pauses`:** `id`; `connection` (in this story only `chatgpt`, validated by the data service); `source_attempt_id`; `paused_at`; `resumed_at`, empty while paused; `resumed_by_operation_id`, pointing to `command_receipts`. The database allows at most one open pause per connection.
- **`blocking_reason` values after this story:** `connection_disabled`, `permission_required`, `input_changed`, `configuration_unavailable`, `restart_paused` and `usage_limit_reached`.

The notice and its "Resume {service} requests ({n})" button belong to STORY-003-013; for ChatGPT the notice links to `https://chatgpt.com/settings/usage`, the usage address the connection declares, which OpenAI gives.[^siwc-errors]

**Documentation amendments:** none. The ADR written in STORY-003-005 already records the blocking reason `usage_limit_reached` and the transition from `queued` to `blocked`.

**Decisions and rationale:** Ohtia never retries a plan exhaustion by itself; Paperclip holds work until a reported reset time, but OpenAI documents none for Sign in with ChatGPT, so an explicit `usagePause.resume` is used instead.[^paperclip]

**Verification:** With the fake provider, a plan-exhaustion error opens the pause and blocks the queue and new messages; `usagePause.resume` requeues in order, including blocked tasks of other projects; a repeated `usagePause.resume` returns its first result; `usagePause.resume` with a new operation identifier after the pause was closed succeeds without change and records its receipt; resuming with the plan still exhausted opens a new pause on the first failure, and tasks already dispatched fail with the same code; a disconnection during a pause keeps tasks blocked by the plan after reconnection; coalescing replaces a blocked task and keeps its reason; a rejected renewal before dispatch cancels the attempt without sending, blocks the task with `connection_disabled`, and reconnection runs its second attempt; a temporary renewal failure before dispatch sends nothing, keeps the connection `connected` and fails the task with `provider_unavailable`; while a sign-in is `connecting`, queued and new tasks are blocked with `connection_disabled`. With the researcher's account, signing out with queued tasks blocks them and signing in again resumes them. Automated tests cover each state change and the one-open-pause rule in the 12 CI cells.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-012: Stop, time limits and uncertain replacement

Stop a task, enforce time limits and let the researcher replace an uncertain task.[^adr-0011]

- **Stop.** The command `task.stop`, with the task and its operation identifier. A queued or blocked task becomes `cancelled` without an attempt. A running task becomes `cancelling` and its attempt `stopping`; the data service sends `provider_interrupt` and main closes the connection (STORY-003-003), and once it is closed the attempt is `cancelled` with `cancelled_by_researcher`, the task is `cancelled` and the slot is freed. If the reply finishes at the same moment, the change that saw the current `state_revision` wins; if the reply won, Stop returns the completed task.
- **Closed connection.** With Sign in with ChatGPT, closing the connection proves that nothing more can arrive, so the slot is freed: a stopped attempt is `cancelled`, an exceeded limit is `failed`, a lost connection is `uncertain` without holding the slot, and usage is unknown with a note that the service does not confirm whether generation stopped.
- **Time limits.** Each operation declares `max_attempt_duration_ms` and the new `max_inactivity_ms`. When either expires, Ohtia closes the connection and the attempt fails with `deadline_exceeded` or `inactivity_timeout`. Values come with each operation's policy (SPEC-003-004); `development_test` uses 10 minutes and 120 seconds, the inactivity value taken from OpenClaw's practice.[^openclaw]
- **Start reply again.** For an uncertain task, the command `task.startAgain` creates a new task linked by `replaces_task_id` that receives the conversation up to its last message, except a partial reply that the uncertain task saved: the new task takes the uncertain task's `input_through_position`, so that reply stays out of its input, which ends with a researcher message or another agent's message in the `user` role (STORY-003-005). The command is accepted only for the conversation's latest reply task, the one no other reply task of the conversation was accepted after, so an old reply never lands after newer ones; otherwise it is rejected with `newer_task_exists` and changes nothing, and STORY-003-013 shows its notice "A newer reply already answers these messages." A task that is not `uncertain` is rejected with `task_not_retryable`, and, like every command that creates a task, `task.startAgain` is rejected with `pending_limit_reached` at its operation's pending limit (STORY-003-005); neither changes anything. This story adds `newer_task_exists`, next action `refresh_record`, and `task_not_retryable`, next action `check_request`, to the port's closed `errorActions` (SPEC-003-015). The latest reply task is terminal and no task was accepted after it, so the conversation has no not-started task and coalescing never applies. The command records that the researcher accepted the warning that the reply may be charged twice, as ADR-0011 requires for repeating uncertain work, in the new column `tasks.charge_warning_accepted_at`: a UTC time with milliseconds, set only on a task that `task.startAgain` created, or that a later story's "Start reply again" created through its own command (STORY-003-022 and STORY-003-026), at its creation, and empty on every other task.[^adr-0011] Until STORY-003-012 and STORY-003-013 add their buttons, the development command's `stop` and `start-again` actions run Stop and "Start reply again" (STORY-003-003).
- **Partial text.** Text received before a cut is saved as an agent message marked `partial`. `messages.ending` is `complete` or `partial` for agent messages and empty for researcher messages, enforced by the database. The migration that adds the column sets it for the agent messages saved since STORY-003-005: `complete` when the message's task is `completed`, and `partial` for every other agent message. The reason stays on the attempt: stopped, attempt deadline, inactivity, output limit, an error in the stream, an event Ohtia cannot parse (`invalid_output`) or lost connection. A stop before any text leaves no message.
- **Cut notes.** Every later task whose input includes a partial message, starting with the next task in that conversation, receives it as part of the conversation and, as the input item right after it, an English note with the `user` role (STORY-003-005), where `{agent}` is the name of the agent that wrote the partial message and the ending follows the attempt's `outcome_reason`:
  - `cancelled_by_researcher`: "Note from Ohtia: {agent}'s reply above is incomplete. The researcher stopped it before it finished."
  - `deadline_exceeded`: "Note from Ohtia: {agent}'s reply above is incomplete. It was cut off because it took longer than Ohtia allows for one reply."
  - `inactivity_timeout`: "Note from Ohtia: {agent}'s reply above is incomplete. It was cut off because the service sent nothing for too long."
  - `output_limit_exceeded`: "Note from Ohtia: {agent}'s reply above is incomplete. It was cut off because it was longer than Ohtia allows for one reply."
  - `provider_error` and `provider_unavailable`: "Note from Ohtia: {agent}'s reply above is incomplete. It was cut off because the service reported an error."
  - `invalid_output`: "Note from Ohtia: {agent}'s reply above is incomplete. It was cut off because Ohtia couldn't read the rest of the service's reply."
  - `connection_lost`: "Note from Ohtia: {agent}'s reply above is incomplete. It was cut off because the connection to the service was lost."
  - Any reason that a later story adds to partial replies uses "Note from Ohtia: {agent}'s reply above is incomplete." alone, unless its block gives its own sentence.

**Documentation amendments:** The ADR written in STORY-003-005 already records the required policy field `max_inactivity_ms` and the closed-connection rule for adapters without background execution or a cancellation endpoint. The documents that still keep every uncertain attempt's slot change with it; in each, "ADR-NNNN" links to `adr-NNNN-research-agent-task-execution.md`:

- `docs/architecture.md`: "Uncertain attempts retain quarantined capacity and cannot publish research effects." becomes "Uncertain attempts cannot publish research effects. They retain quarantined capacity, except that an attempt of an adapter without background execution or a cancellation endpoint frees its slot once its connection closes, as ADR-NNNN records."
- `docs/agents/agent-collaboration.md`, handoff: "It cannot move an in-flight attempt, give the recipient the sender's capability or bypass an uncertain attempt's quarantine." becomes "It cannot move an in-flight attempt, give the recipient the sender's capability or bypass the quarantine that an uncertain attempt keeps; under ADR-NNNN, an uncertain attempt of an adapter without background execution or a cancellation endpoint keeps none once its connection closes."
- `docs/agents/agent-collaboration.md`, coordination sequence: "An uncertain attempt retains its quarantine." becomes "An uncertain attempt retains its quarantine, except that an adapter without background execution or a cancellation endpoint frees the slot once its connection closes (ADR-NNNN)."
- `docs/product-roadmap.md`, "Bounded execution": "lower limits preserve admitted work and uncertainty quarantines persist" becomes "lower limits preserve admitted work and uncertainty quarantines persist, except where ADR-NNNN frees the slot of an adapter without background execution or a cancellation endpoint once its connection closes".
- `docs/agents/agent-collaboration-evaluation.md`, scenario 7: "unresolved cessation remains uncertain and quarantined" becomes "unresolved cessation remains uncertain, and quarantined unless its adapter has no background execution or cancellation endpoint, whose closed connection frees the slot (ADR-NNNN)".
- `docs/agents/agent-collaboration-evaluation.md`, scenario 10: "including quarantined slots" becomes "including quarantined slots and the slots that closed connections free under ADR-NNNN".

**Decisions and rationale:** Keeping partial text matches what ChatGPT does when stopped and lets the researcher ask to continue. Releasing the slot after the connection closes follows from Sign in with ChatGPT's lack of background mode and cancellation endpoint.[^siwc-limitations]

**Verification:** With the development command and the researcher's account, stopping a real reply midway cancels the task, frees the slot and saves the partial text, which the next message sees. With the fake provider, the attempt deadline, the inactivity limit and the output limit each leave their code; a lost connection leaves the task uncertain and frees the slot; "Start reply again" creates the linked task with `charge_warning_accepted_at` and is rejected, changing nothing, with `newer_task_exists` when the uncertain task is no longer the latest reply task, with `task_not_retryable` for a task that is not uncertain and with `pending_limit_reached` at the pending limit; each cut reason gives the next task its note right after the partial message; the migration marks existing agent messages `complete` or `partial` by their task's state; Stop racing completion leaves one result. Automated tests run in the 12 CI cells.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-013: Restart and suspension

Recover tasks after Ohtia or its data service starts again.[^adr-0011]

- **When.** Every time the data service starts, Ohtia dispatches nothing until it has reviewed what remained from the previous run. This happens when Ohtia opens and when the data service crashes and is restarted while Ohtia stays open; each start already has its own `generation`. Before main starts the new data service, it has closed every provider request of the ended generation and discards their later events (STORY-003-003), so no attempt the review meets is still streaming.
- **`service_runs`:** `generation`, the value stored in `attempts.service_generation`; `started_at`; `clean_stop_at`, empty until the service stops in order. One row per start; rows are never deleted. The existing orderly shutdown writes `clean_stop_at`, as its last write before it closes the store. The shutdown message that main sends the data service, today exactly `{"kind":"shutdown","generation":…}` in `packages/utility/src/entry.ts`, gains the required boolean `orderly`: the data service accepts only `{"kind":"shutdown","generation":…,"orderly":true}` and the same message with `false`, drains and closes the store for both, and writes `clean_stop_at` only for `true`. In this story main always sends `true`; STORY-003-009 sends `false` for an operating-system shutdown or logout.
- **After an orderly stop.** Queued tasks run by themselves in order; blocked tasks keep their reason.
- **After a crash, power loss or forced quit.** Every not-started task, queued or blocked, becomes `blocked` with `restart_paused`. An attempt that never dispatched becomes `cancelled` with `service_restarted`, and its task also becomes `restart_paused`, or is cancelled when its conversation already has a not-started task (STORY-003-005). One command, `task.continueAll`, which STORY-003-013 shows as "Continue all ({n})", with `{n}` the number of paused tasks, resumes them all, in every project, in order after revalidating each: a missing connection leaves `connection_disabled`; an open plan pause leaves `usage_limit_reached`. A task with no attempt left fails with `service_restarted`. With no restart-paused task, as when another window continued them first, the command succeeds without change and records its receipt.
- **Dispatched attempt, in both cases.** An unfinished attempt with `dispatch_permitted_at` set counts as dispatched, even when main never sent it (STORY-003-005). The task becomes `uncertain` and its slot is free; any partial text from that attempt is lost.
- **Suspension.** Main detects sleep and wake with Electron's `powerMonitor` `suspend` and `resume` events, and on `resume` sends the data service the new versioned, runtime-validated message `service_wake`, defined in `packages/contracts` beside the provider messages of STORY-003-003: its `generation`, `suspendedAt` and `resumedAt` (UTC with milliseconds, from the wall clock at each event) and `asleepMs`, the wall-clock time between them, or 0 when the wall clock went backwards.[^electron-power-monitor] On that message, the data service reviews each running attempt, counting `asleepMs` against its `max_attempt_duration_ms` and `max_inactivity_ms`, and main refuses any dispatch permit issued before `resumedAt`, as ADR-0028 requires, with the result `permit_expired` of STORY-003-003, which requeues the task for its second attempt (STORY-003-005). A `service_wake` of another generation is discarded.[^adr-0028]
- **Edge cases.** A task that was `cancelling` when the service ended is `cancelled` with `cancelled_by_researcher`, because its connection is closed. After an orderly stop, an attempt that never dispatched becomes `cancelled` with `service_restarted` and its task is requeued, or cancelled when its conversation already has a not-started task (STORY-003-005). Coalescing a `restart_paused` task creates a task that keeps `restart_paused`.
- **Task policy.** `max_attempts` 2, `max_auto_retries` 0 and `retry_backoff_ms` 0. A second attempt runs only for a task whose first attempt ended before dispatch: when "Continue all ({n})" resumes it after a crash, when it is requeued after an orderly stop, when main refused its dispatch permit (STORY-003-005), or when reconnection requeues it after a rejected renewal (STORY-003-006).

Until STORY-003-013 adds its notice and button, the development command's `continue-all` action runs "Continue all ({n})" (STORY-003-003).

**Documentation amendments:**

- `docs/frontend-architecture.md`, "Connections, tasks and consumption": "On restart, show paused accepted tasks and offer explicit continuation after revalidation; restoring tabs or enabling a connection does not start them." becomes "After an orderly exit, accepted not-started tasks continue by themselves in order at the next start. After a crash, power loss or forced quit, show the paused accepted tasks and offer one "Continue all" after revalidation; restoring tabs or enabling a connection does not start them." The ADR written in STORY-003-005 already records the same rule in place of ADR-0011's.
- `docs/agents/agent-collaboration.md`, "Completion, cancellation and recovery": "Accepted not-started tasks remain paused;" becomes "After an orderly exit, accepted not-started tasks continue by themselves; after a crash, power loss or forced quit they remain paused until "Continue all";".
- `docs/agents/agent-collaboration-evaluation.md`, scenario 9: "Restart with queued tasks and unactivated intent." becomes "Restart with queued tasks and unactivated intent, after an orderly exit and after a crash."; the rest stays and applies after a crash.
- `docs/product-roadmap.md`: in the EPIC-003 "Done when", "explicit restart continuation" becomes "restart continuation (automatic after an orderly exit, explicit after a crash)", and "Verify all 20 deterministic collaboration scenarios at the shared module boundary" becomes "Verify deterministic collaboration scenarios 1 to 12 and 15 to 20 at the shared module boundary; scenarios 13 and 14 need the manuscript editor", the rest of that sentence unchanged; in the shared acceptance rules, "Exit/restart/suspension follow the canonical state machine and explicit continuation" becomes "Exit/restart/suspension follow the canonical state machine, with explicit continuation after a crash".

**Decisions and rationale:** The researcher chose automatic resumption, as Paperclip, Multica and OpenClaw do. Because Ohtia is a desktop application rather than a server, the researcher also chose to separate the two cases as OpenClaw does: an orderly exit is the researcher's own action, and the exit dialog says that queued work continues at the next start; after a crash, power loss or forced quit nothing has told the researcher, so the work waits for "Continue all ({n})". Real failures are never retried automatically.[^openclaw]

**Verification:** With the researcher's account on the x86-64 virtual machines and the development command: killing Ohtia mid-reply leaves the task uncertain with its slot free on reopening; killing Ohtia with queued tasks pauses them and "Continue all ({n})" runs them in order; an orderly close and reopen runs them by themselves. With the fake provider: a cut before dispatch pauses the task and "Continue all ({n})" launches attempt 2; a second cut fails it; `task.continueAll` with no restart-paused task succeeds without change and records its receipt; a data-service crash with Ohtia open is treated as a crash, and main has closed that generation's requests before the review starts. Suspension is tested by simulating `suspend` and `resume`: main sends `service_wake` with the time asleep, a running attempt counts it against its limits, a permit issued before the sleep is refused with `permit_expired` and the task's second attempt runs, and a `service_wake` of an ended generation is discarded. A crash between the commit of a dispatch permit and the send leaves the task `uncertain` with its slot free and runs no second attempt. The data service writes `clean_stop_at` for a shutdown message with `orderly` `true` only, and rejects one without `orderly`. Automated tests run in the 12 CI cells.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-014: Application exit

Apply ADR-0011's application-exit rules to Sign in with ChatGPT.[^adr-0011]

- **When.** Closing the last window, "Quit" in the menu, and on macOS Cmd+Q or "Quit" in the Dock. Closing a window while others remain open does not touch tasks.
- **No running tasks.** Ohtia exits without asking. Queued tasks stay stored and run by themselves on the next start; blocked tasks keep their reason (SPEC-003-013).
- **Running tasks.** Ohtia stops starting new tasks and shows a dialog with three choices, rendered in the window whose close was intercepted or, for "Quit" in the menu, Cmd+Q or "Quit" in the Dock, in the most recently focused window, which main tracks through `browser-window-focus` because a Dock quit can come while no window has focus, so it can show the live count. Its title is "Quit Ohtia while replies are running?" and its body "{running} replies are still running. Replies that haven't started are kept for the next time you open Ohtia, unless you cancel the work.", with plural forms, where `{running}` is the number of running tasks in every project. Initial focus goes to "Close dialog":
  - "Close dialog", also Esc: closes the dialog and everything continues;
  - "Wait for replies, then quit": the body becomes "Ohtia will quit when the running replies finish. {running} still running.", with plural forms, announced politely as the count changes; the wait happens before the broker shutdown (`stopBrokerServices`) starts, so the shutdown's own time limit applies only after the last reply is saved; running tasks finish within their limits; queued tasks do not start and wait for the next start, which the dialog states; the dialog shows how many remain and keeps the other two choices available; Ohtia exits after the last reply is saved;
  - "Cancel work and quit": main sends the data service the command `task.cancelAll`, with an operation identifier main generates, which cancels queued and blocked tasks and stops running tasks as with Stop (SPEC-003-012), keeping partial text; Ohtia exits once everything is saved.
- **Dialog close label.** This is the first story of this EPIC with a confirmation dialog, so it changes the existing `dialog.close` catalog text from "Close" to "Close dialog", in every supported locale catalog, so that the header button and the safe option read the same (SPEC-003-002); "Close dialog" is already the close icon's name in `design-system.md`. That text names the header close button of every dialog and the footer close buttons of the About dialog (`packages/renderer/src/command-surfaces.tsx`, its `footer`) and of the Help dialog (the same file), which all close their dialog. The one other use, the button that closes the "layout could not be remembered" notice in Settings (`packages/renderer/src/settings-surfaces.tsx`), closes only that notice, so it moves to the new catalog key `settings.closeLayoutNotice`, "Close notice", in every supported locale catalog. The `dialogs.close` shortcut entry in `packages/contracts/src/commands.ts` keeps `dialog.close`, so the shortcut list reads "Close dialog".
- **No `shutdown_paused`.** While waiting to exit, Ohtia holds queued tasks in memory and never uses ADR-0011's `shutdown_paused`; if it crashes during the wait, the crash rules apply on the next start.
- **Uncertain tasks** do not trigger the dialog: their connection is already closed, so there is nothing to stop.
- **Operating-system shutdown or logout** exits without a dialog, so no orderly-stop note is written and the next start applies the crash rules. Once main detects it, it skips the dialog and lets the exit proceed: it still runs `stopBrokerServices`, as for any quit, but sends the data service the shutdown message with `orderly` set to `false` (STORY-003-008), so the data service drains and closes the store without writing `clean_stop_at`. Electron 45 offers a different signal on each platform:[^electron-power-monitor][^electron-browser-window][^electron-shutdown-source][^apple-will-power-off]
  - Windows: the window events `query-session-end` and `session-end`, which Electron emits for a shutdown, a restart or a user log-off.
  - macOS: the `powerMonitor` `shutdown` event. Electron emits it on `NSWorkspaceWillPowerOffNotification`, which macOS posts when the user requests a logout or powers off the device.
  - Linux: the `powerMonitor` `shutdown` event covers reboot and shutdown only, because Electron emits it on logind's `PrepareForShutdown`; Electron reports no logout, and it turns a `SIGTERM` into an ordinary quit. Main therefore installs its own `SIGTERM` listener with `process.on("SIGTERM")` and treats that signal as an operating-system shutdown or logout.

**Documentation amendments:** `docs/frontend-architecture.md`, "Connections, tasks and consumption": "Quit with unresolved remote work requires its distinct acknowledgement after local cessation is proved." becomes "Uncertain tasks do not open the exit dialog, and an operating-system shutdown or logout exits without it, so the next start applies the crash rules." The ADR written in STORY-003-005 already records the exit choices in place of ADR-0011's, including the removal of `shutdown_paused` and "Quit with unresolved remote work".

**Decisions and rationale:** A dialog during shutdown could leave the computer waiting for an absent researcher. Paperclip marks runs interrupted and OpenClaw aborts replies with `aborted_for_restart`; neither waits, so the waiting option comes from ADR-0011.[^paperclip][^openclaw]

**Verification:** With the fake provider in the 12 CI cells: each of the three choices with the dialog's texts and live count, the header close button of every existing dialog and the footer close buttons of About and Help named "Close dialog", the Settings layout notice's button named "Close notice", the `dialogs.close` entry of the shortcut list reading "Close dialog", exiting with no running task, closing one of two windows, a crash during the wait, a menu quit with no focused window showing the dialog in the most recently focused one, and, with simulated events, `session-end` on Windows, `powerMonitor` `shutdown` on macOS and Linux, and a `SIGTERM` to main on Linux each exiting without a dialog or `clean_stop_at`, after a shutdown message with `orderly` `false`. With the researcher's account on Windows, Ubuntu 26.04, Fedora 44 and Debian 13: quitting with each choice mid-reply, logging out and powering off the virtual machine during a reply.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-015: Conversation view

Show one conversation in the chat panel with attributed messages and live replies, and carry the research operations and long texts through the application port.[^workspace][^port]

- **What it shows.** The chat panel shows one conversation: its name at the top, as the panel's heading, or, when unnamed, its creation date and time in the interface locale with a medium date and a short time, such as "Nov 10, 2026, 2:03 PM" in `en-US`, and its messages in order below. With no conversation open, the panel stays as it is today. In this story the development command's `create-development-project` action creates the development project and its new `open-conversation` action opens a conversation in the focused window (STORY-003-003); the window remembers the open conversation only while it is open.
- **Authors.** An agent message shows its agent's static role symbol (`ContributorSymbol`), name and role, marked as AI, through the existing `VoiceLabel`. A researcher message has no symbol or name, as `research-presentation.md` requires, and screen readers announce it as "You". Contributors have no portraits and no animated gestures in this EPIC. The message shows no `ContributorAttribution` details; reply details are STORY-003-034.
