A researcher message is a bubble aligned to the inline end of the timeline, at most 85% of its width, on `surface.emphasis` with `radius.medium`; an agent message spans the timeline's width below its `VoiceLabel`. Message text uses `type.ui.body-small`.
- **Message actions.** Below each saved message, a row of always-visible quiet icon buttons, never shown only on hover: each is 32 × 32 px with a 16 px icon in `content.secondary`, its name as accessible name and tooltip, and `space.1` from the next. A researcher message's row is "Copy message", "Reuse message" and "Add memory entry…"; an agent message's row is "Copy message", "Show details" and "Add memory entry…", in that order. Each button arrives with its story: "Copy message" with STORY-003-011, "Reuse message" with STORY-003-012, "Add memory entry…" with STORY-003-033, which leaves it out of the setup conversation, and "Show details" with STORY-003-034; this story draws the row's place and measurements.
- **Message text.** In this story each message shows its text as plain text, exactly as stored: line breaks are kept, and long words and addresses wrap, so the panel never scrolls horizontally, even at 280 CSS px. Text never becomes HTML, as ADR-0014 requires.[^adr-0014] STORY-003-011 renders it as restricted Markdown.
- **Timeline contract.** Every item the chat shows (messages, task notices, proposal cards, plan cards, handoff dividers and synthesis messages) has a stable identity, a revision and a position in the conversation. A repeated item does not duplicate, and an older revision is ignored. Items are ordered by position, then by their rank within a position, then by identifier: a message ranks 1, a card that a task proposed 2 and a task's notice 3, and each later item type names its rank in its block. A task's notice takes the task's `reply_position` once the task has started and its `input_through_position` before, so it appears below the last message it answers (STORY-003-013). An item's revision is 1 for a message, which never changes, and the task's `state_revision` for a task's notice (STORY-003-005); each later item type names its revision in its block, as STORY-003-014 does for proposal cards. A card that a reply proposes takes the reply position of the task that proposed it (STORY-003-005): right below the reply's text, or in its place when the reply carried only the proposal and therefore saved no message (STORY-003-014). Every later story that adds an item type uses this contract and the timeline reads below.
- **Live replies.** The data service sends new text to the window as it arrives, with sequence numbers, over the research subscription (Port). If the window misses a number, it asks again for all the text received so far. A streaming reply is redrawn at most ten times per second. While a reply streams, its message is marked `aria-busy` and a line below its text shows Lucide `pen-line` and "{agent} is writing…", where `{agent}` is the name of the agent that writes it, in `type.ui.caption` and `content.secondary` with a `space.1` gap; the line is not announced, because streamed parts are not. The message shows no actions until the reply is saved, because "Copy message" needs the saved message; then the line disappears and the actions row appears.
- **Reading.** At the end of the conversation, the view follows new text. After scrolling up, the view stays put and a "Jump to latest message" button appears: a round 32 px icon button with a 20 px Lucide `arrow-down` icon, a 1 px `border.control` border, `surface.page` and `shadow.overlay`, centered `space.2` above the composer, with "Jump to latest message" as its accessible name and tooltip. Returning to the conversation restores the reading position. `pen-line` and `arrow-down` are vendored from the `lucide-static` 1.66.0 package pinned in `packages/renderer/icons/manifest.json`, with their hashes in the manifest and the generated `design-icons.ts` and icon mappings updated through the existing `scripts/design_assets.py` procedure, whose `--write-icon-module` regenerates `design-icons.ts`.
- **Long conversations.** Opening a conversation loads its latest 50 timeline items. While earlier items exist, the top of the timeline shows the secondary button "Load earlier messages"; it loads the previous 50 without moving what the researcher is reading, and while they load it is disabled and the status "Loading earlier messages…" is announced politely. Scrolling to the top loads nothing, as `design-system.md` requires: "Long lists are paginated or loaded in batches through an explicit action; scrolling does not load content without notice." Once the first item is loaded, the button disappears. If that page fails, the top of the timeline shows a danger notice with `octagon-alert`, announced as an alert: "Ohtia couldn't load earlier messages. They're still saved.", with "Load earlier messages" below it, which reads the same page again. This adds the read `timeline.listBefore`, which takes the conversation, the cursor `(position, rank, id)` of the oldest item the window holds, empty for the first read, and a count of at most 50, and returns the items before that cursor in the timeline order, newest first; the first read returns the conversation's latest items. A page that ends among items sharing a position, such as a reply and its card, therefore loses and repeats none of them.
- **Panel states.** Each looks different and has its own text:
  - loading: a skeleton shaped like messages, marked `aria-busy`, and the status "Loading conversation…";
  - empty conversation: "This conversation has no messages yet.", in secondary text; STORY-003-015 gives the setup conversation its own text;
  - failed load: a danger notice with `octagon-alert`, announced as an alert: "Ohtia couldn't load this conversation. Its messages are still saved.", with "Reload conversation";
  - data service unavailable: an attention notice with `triangle-alert`, announced as a status: "Conversations are unavailable until local data and the local service are ready.", the wording the existing preferences notice uses.
- **Screen readers.** Each message is a block named by its author. When a reply finishes, "{agent} replied" is announced, with the name of the agent that wrote it; streamed parts are not announced.
- **Message language.** An agent message carries `lang` equal to the `document_language` that its producing task recorded at acceptance (STORY-003-005), which is its confirmed project's document language, because agents always answer in it (STORY-003-017), and `dir="auto"`. The language is the one retained with the message, never the project's current value, as `design-system.md` asks of generated content. A researcher message, and every agent message whose task recorded no language because its project was still in setup, such as a `project_setup` reply, carries `lang=""` and `dir="auto"`, the `research-presentation.md` rule for an unknown language. The timeline reads return each message's language. The reason is the screen reader: it chooses its voice and pronunciation from `lang`, so a declared document language reads the reply correctly, while a guessed language would read a whole message in the wrong voice; `lang=""` leaves an unknown language undeclared, and the same attribute also selects the packaged font fallback and Korean line breaking, as `design-system.md` requires generated content to declare its language.[^design][^research-presentation]
- **Port.** From this story on, the application port carries the research operations that the blocks of this EPIC name, under the rule of SPEC-003-002: main admits each one as it admits the existing operations and forwards it to the data service, which validates it and rechecks its domain preconditions. Every value stays within the port's existing bounds (65,536 bytes per message, 4,096 bytes per string, 128 items per container and 512 values per message); a research request payload may reach the new `research_input_bytes` of 32,768 bytes, and results and events keep the existing 32,768-byte payload bound. This story exposes the operations of STORY-003-004 and, when STORY-003-014 merged before it, that story's changed `project.confirmSetup` and its `project.rejectSetup`; the commands of STORY-003-006 to STORY-003-008 reach the port with STORY-003-012 and STORY-003-013, which start after them (SPEC-003-002). This story adds:
  - the reads `conversation.read`, given up to 128 conversation identifiers of one project, which returns each one's name, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `revision` and answering agent, `virgilio` for every conversation until STORY-003-021 adds `conversations.agent_id`; `timeline.listBefore`; and `timeline.read`, given timeline items of one conversation by identifier, at most 128;
  - the stream `research.subscribe`, one per window, for its open project and shown conversation; a window with no open project, such as one that shows only the welcome or Settings, also holds it, and its events then name only the records shared by every project. Each event names one changed record with its kind, identity and revision: a conversation of the open project, with its `revision` (STORY-003-004); a timeline item of the shown conversation, with its revision under the timeline contract; or a record shared by every project: a project, whose revision is 1 in setup and 2 once confirmed, a usage pause, 1 while open and 2 once resumed, or the execution limits, with their record's revision (STORY-003-019). Events also carry the streamed parts of the shown conversation's replies. The window rereads what changed and ignores a revision it already has. A story that adds a read whose values can change while none of these records changes adds its own event, as STORY-003-013 does for the pause counts. Opening another project or conversation replaces the subscription, so a window holds at most four (presentation, locale, connection and research), within the existing `subscriptions_per_window` of 4. After a `sequence_gap`, the window subscribes again with a fresh identifier and rereads everything it shows: the projects with `project.list`, the shown conversation with `conversation.read`, its latest page with `timeline.listBefore` without a cursor, the other items it holds with `timeline.read`, a streaming reply's parts with `reply.readParts`, and each record that a later view shows, as that view's block states;
  - the text parts below, `text.part`, `text.readPart` and `reply.readParts`.
- **Text parts.** A text longer than 4,096 UTF-8 bytes crosses the port in parts. Each part takes as many whole code points as fit in 4,096 bytes, in order, so no part splits a character and joining the parts gives the exact text; a text has at most 64 parts.
  - Sending. Before a command that carries such a text (`conversation.start` and `message.append`, the selection of `handoff.return` in STORY-003-029, and the memory commands of STORY-003-033), the window sends each part with `text.part`, under that command's operation identifier, with the payload `{ index, count, bytes, sha256, text }`: the part's number from 1 to `count`, and the byte length and SHA-256 of the whole text. The command then carries `{ parts, bytes, sha256 }` in place of the text. The data service keeps received parts only in memory, per window and operation identifier, joins them when the command arrives, checks the count, the byte length and the SHA-256, and runs the command as if the text were inline. The receipt's `payload_sha256` covers the text's SHA-256, so a repeated command returns its first result whether or not its parts arrive again, and the same identifier with another text is rejected as a conflict.
  - Errors. A command whose parts are missing or do not match fails with the new error `parts_incomplete`, whose next action is `resend_parts`; it stores nothing and discards those parts, and the window sends every part again with the same operation identifier. A part whose `count`, `bytes` or `sha256` differs from an earlier part of the same operation, whose index is outside 1 to `count`, or that repeats an index with other text is rejected with `invalid_message` and discards that operation's parts.
  - Abandoned parts. They are discarded when their command uses them, 60 seconds after their last part, when their window closes, reloads or changes generation, and when the data service restarts; discarding writes nothing. A window holds at most 4 texts in parts and 512 KiB of parts at once; a part beyond either bound is refused with `busy`. Parts write no receipt.
  - Reading. No result carries a string over 4,096 bytes. A stored text that is longer, such as a message, a card's request, note or shared context, or a memory entry version, appears in a result as a text reference `{ source, parts, bytes, sha256 }`, where `source` is an identifier that the data service issues for that immutable text; `text.readPart`, with `{ source, index }`, returns one part. The window joins the parts, checks the byte length and the SHA-256, and reads the text again from its first part when they do not match.
  - Pages. A read that returns a list, such as timeline items, history rows, projects or memory entries, puts in one result as many whole items as fit the port's bounds, up to the count requested, and returns where to continue and whether more items exist; the window repeats the read until it has that count or reaches the end. A read given identifiers returns as many whole items as fit, in the order requested, and the identifiers it did not return; the window reads those again. A window asks for the next page only when the researcher uses that list's button, never because the list was scrolled (SPEC-003-002).
  - Streamed text. Each streamed part of a reply is at most 4,096 bytes and carries its sequence number. After a gap, the window reads all the reply's parts again with `reply.readParts`, `{ taskId, fromSequence }`, from sequence 1 and then from the sequence after the last part each result returns, until it reaches the latest sequence, which every result also returns.
- **Error codes.** Research operations return the port's existing errors and the research codes below. Each research code enters the port's closed `errorActions`, with its next action, in the story named beside it, the first that returns it, because the data service already returns port errors; that story also adds any new next action the code needs: `refresh_record` with STORY-003-004's codes, meaning that the window reads the named records again and shows their current state without repeating the command, and `resend_parts` with this story's `parts_incomplete`. Each operation's `operationErrorCodes` entry lists the codes it can return and is added by the story that exposes the operation (SPEC-003-002); a later story that adds a rejection adds its code and next action to this set. Every rejection with one of these codes saves nothing and writes no receipt, so the same command repeated later is checked again against the current data.
  - `parts_incomplete`, next action `resend_parts`: a command whose text parts are missing or do not match (Text parts; this story).
  - `message_invalid`, `check_request`: `conversation.start` or `message.append` with an empty researcher message (STORY-003-004), and from STORY-003-012 on also a whitespace-only one or one over 20,000 code points.
  - `input_too_large`, `check_request`: `conversation.start` or `message.append` with a message over the `max_input_bytes` of the operation whose task it would create (STORY-003-005).
  - `pending_limit_reached`, `retry_later`: `conversation.start`, `message.append` and every later command that creates a task, at the operation's `max_pending_tasks` (STORY-003-005).
  - `project_in_setup`, `refresh_record`: `conversation.start` in a project in setup (STORY-003-004).
  - `project_not_in_setup`, `refresh_record`: `project.confirmSetup` of a confirmed project (STORY-003-004).
  - `name_invalid`, `check_request`: `conversation.rename` with a name outside the title rules (STORY-003-004).
  - `record_not_found`, `refresh_record`: an identifier that names no record the command needs (STORY-003-004).
  - `newer_task_exists`, `refresh_record`: `task.startAgain` or `task.tryAgain` for a task that is no longer the conversation's latest reply task (STORY-003-007 and STORY-003-013).
  - `task_not_retryable`, `check_request`: `task.startAgain` for a task that is not uncertain, and `task.tryAgain` for one that did not fail or failed with `not_eligible` (STORY-003-007 and STORY-003-013).
  - `proposal_not_waiting`, `refresh_record`: `project.confirmSetup` or `project.rejectSetup` for a proposal that is no longer waiting (STORY-003-014).
  - `setup_reply_active`, `retry_later`: `project.confirmSetup` while a setup reply runs or waits (STORY-003-014).
  - A rename that another rename preceded returns the existing `stale_revision`, whose `currentRevision` is the conversation's `revision` (STORY-003-004). Its fixed next action stays `refresh_preference`; for a research operation it means that the window reads the named record again, with that record's read, such as `conversation.read`, and shows its current state without repeating the command.

**Measurements,** all expressed with existing tokens unless stated:

- Timeline: `space.4` padding, `space.3` between items and `space.2` inside a message; `VoiceLabel` in `type.ui.label` with a 24 px symbol; the researcher bubble has `space.2` block and `space.3` inline padding.
- Message actions: the row sits `space.2` closer to the text above, so the first glyph aligns with the text's start.

**Documentation amendments:**

- `docs/application-port.md`, "Scope and authority", as left by STORY-003-001: "The portable API carries application configuration and the provider connection operations `connection.read`, `connection.subscribe`, `connection.probe`, `connection.listModels`, `connection.connect` and `connection.disconnect`, which main handles without forwarding them to the data service; it has no project, research, credential, filesystem, SQL or subprocess authority, and no operation returns a credential." becomes "The portable API carries application configuration; the provider connection operations `connection.read`, `connection.subscribe`, `connection.probe`, `connection.listModels`, `connection.connect` and `connection.disconnect`, which main handles without forwarding them to the data service; and the research operations of its section "Research operations and text parts", which main forwards to the data service. It has no credential, filesystem, SQL or subprocess authority, and no operation returns a credential."
- `docs/application-port.md`: add the section "Research operations and text parts" after "Envelopes and named operations", stating this block's "Port" and "Text parts" rules: research operations are named `<record>.<action>` and listed in each research feature's specification; main forwards them to the data service, which validates them and rechecks their domain preconditions; data-changing ones carry an operation identifier and record a command receipt whose hash covers the payload; request payloads are bounded by `research_input_bytes` and results and events by `initial_output_bytes`; `research.subscribe`, with the revision of each record kind and the recovery after a `sequence_gap`; the timeline order and the `timeline.listBefore` cursor; text parts in both directions with `text.part` and `text.readPart`; pages; and streamed text with `reply.readParts`. For error codes, the section does not list the codes, because `errorActions` and `operationErrorCodes` are already canonical in the contract source; it ends with this paragraph, in which "port.ts" is a relative link to `../packages/contracts/src/port.ts`, as in "Canonical schemas and resource policy": "Research operations return the port's errors and the research error codes. The exported `errorActions` map and `operationErrorCodes` in port.ts are the canonical list of each research code, its next action and the operations that return it; each research feature's specification names the codes its operations add, and the change that adds them extends both maps. Research adds the next actions `resend_parts`, which sends every part of the text again under the same operation identifier, and `refresh_record`, which reads the named records again and shows their current state without repeating the command. For a research operation, `stale_revision`, whose next action stays `refresh_preference`, means the same: the window reads the named record again and shows its current state. A rejection with a research code saves nothing and writes no receipt."
- `docs/application-port.md`, "Outcomes, cancellation and receipts": "only `stale_revision` takes `currentRevision`, and every other current error takes an empty object." becomes "`stale_revision` takes `currentRevision`, a research code takes the parameters its specification names, and every other error takes an empty object."
- `config/desktop/resource-policy.json`, keeping `policy_id` `foundation-1` because no existing bound changes: add six budgets, each with `owner` `003`, the port contract's owner, and minimum and maximum equal to its value: `research_input_bytes`, 32768 `byte`, scope "Payload of each research request, including one text part", failure `invalid_message`, rationale "One 4,096-byte part fits even when every character needs a JSON escape"; `text_part_bytes`, 4096 `byte`, scope "Each part of a text longer than one port string", failure `invalid_message`, rationale "Equal to the string bound"; `text_parts`, 64 `part`, scope "Parts of one text", failure `invalid_message`, rationale "Holds a 192 KiB agent reply with margin"; `staged_texts_per_window`, 4 `text`, scope "Texts whose parts one window has sent and no command has used yet", failure `busy_before_acceptance`, rationale "Bounds memory held for unfinished uploads"; `staged_text_bytes_per_window`, 524288 `byte`, scope "Bytes of those parts in one window", failure `busy_before_acceptance`, rationale "Holds four researcher messages of 20,000 code points"; and `staged_text_idle_ms`, 60000 `millisecond`, scope "Time after the last part before unused parts are discarded", failure `discard_unused_parts`, rationale "An interrupted upload leaves nothing behind"; and change the scope of `initial_output_bytes`, as left by STORY-003-001, from "Payload only for each initial or connection operation result or event" to "Payload only for each operation result or event", and the scope of `request_deadline_ms`, as left by STORY-003-001, from "Status, preference and connection requests from admission to reply; includes queues" to "Every port request from admission to reply; includes queues". `scripts/desktop_contract.py` gains the six names and their units.
- `docs/design-system.md`, "Iconography", fixed mappings: add the row "Jump to latest message | `arrow-down`".

**Decisions and rationale:** The static role symbol with name and role is enough under `agent-identity.md`, which asks for "a distinct static avatar or role symbol with name and role"; portraits and gestures need final art and recognition testing.[^identity] Text travels in parts, under its command's operation identifier, because the port's 4,096-byte strings and 64 KiB messages are smaller than one researcher message or agent reply, and a single receipt for the whole text keeps every command idempotent.[^port] The view shows plain text in this story so that the port, streaming and paging can merge and be verified on their own; Markdown, "Copy message" and links are STORY-003-011, which runs beside the composer.

**Verification:** Hostile content (`<script>`, HTML and `javascript:` addresses in message text) shows as plain text and never inserts HTML or violates the packaged CSP. The view works at 280 and 320 CSS px, in light and dark themes, and in German, Korean and pseudolocalization. With the fake provider: a long reply while reading above the end does not move the view and shows the round "Jump to latest message" button; a streaming reply shows "{agent} is writing…" and no actions until it is saved, and every saved message shows its actions row without hover; a missing sequence number makes the window request the full text; a 120-item conversation loads its latest 50 and then 50 more each time "Load earlier messages" is used, scrolling to the top loads nothing, the button disappears once the first item is loaded, and a failed earlier page shows its notice with the button below it; each panel state shows its text; a 192 KiB reply, the largest any operation of this EPIC allows (STORY-003-032), and a 20,000-code-point Korean or emoji message cross the port in parts and arrive exactly; a `conversation.read` of 128 conversations whose items exceed the result bound returns the whole items that fit, in the order requested, and the identifiers left, which the window reads again; a repeated command with its parts returns its first result; a missing part yields `parts_incomplete` and a resend succeeds; abandoned parts are discarded after 60 seconds and when their window closes; agent messages of a confirmed project declare the language their task recorded and researcher messages declare none; pages of 50 that end inside a position, such as between a reply and its card, lose and repeat no item; after a sequence gap the window subscribes again and rereads what it shows; a window with no open project receives the events of the records shared by every project; a research read held by a slow data service past `request_deadline_ms` ends with `timeout`; and each research rejection returns its code with its next action. Automated tests run in the 12 CI cells. With the researcher's account on the x86-64 virtual machines, the development command sends a request and the real reply appears in the panel as it streams.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-016: Markdown, copy and links

Render every message of the conversation view of STORY-003-010 as restricted Markdown, and let the researcher copy a message and open its links through main.[^adr-0014]

- **Markdown.** Ohtia parses Markdown into a syntax tree and renders only allowed node types; it never inserts HTML, as ADR-0014 requires. This replaces the plain text of STORY-003-010 for every message.
  - Rendered: paragraphs, emphasis, strong text, inline and block code, block quotes, lists, links, thematic breaks and tables.
  - Headings render as bold text, not page headings, so the page's heading order stays intact for screen readers.
  - Shown as written, with their markers: HTML such as `<script>`, formulas, images, footnotes and strikethrough. Images are never fetched.
  - Code blocks and wide tables scroll inside their own box; the panel never scrolls horizontally, even at 280 CSS px. Each box is focusable, as `design-system.md` requires, and named "Code block" or "Table".
  - Links open in the system browser only when they start with `https`, `http` or `mailto`, through the new main broker operation `openExternalLink(url)`: main parses the address again with the WHATWG URL parser and opens it only when it is at most 4,096 bytes and its scheme is one of those three, because the existing `openLink()` accepts only fixed project destinations. They carry the external-link icon, whose accessible name is "opens in your browser", and show the full address on hover and keyboard focus. A link with any other scheme is shown as its label text, without a link. Reference-style links resolve to their definitions like inline links, and the definitions themselves are not shown.
- **Parser.** `mdast-util-from-markdown` 2.1.0 with `micromark-extension-gfm-table` 2.1.2 and `mdast-util-gfm-table` 2.0.1, all MIT-licensed. Paperclip and Multica use `react-markdown` 10.1.0, built on the same parts plus about ten more, and Multica also accepts and then sanitizes HTML; OpenClaw uses `markdown-it` 15.0.3, which produces HTML that it sanitizes with DOMPurify. ADR-0014 forbids inserting HTML even when sanitized, so Ohtia uses the parser parts and decides what to draw.[^paperclip][^multica][^openclaw]
- **Streaming.** A streaming reply's formatting is redrawn within the limit of STORY-003-010, at most ten times per second.
- **Copy.** Each saved message has a "Copy message" button for its original text, the first button of its actions row (STORY-003-010): an always-visible quiet icon button with the Lucide `copy` icon, vendored from the `lucide-static` 1.66.0 package pinned in `packages/renderer/icons/manifest.json`, with its hash in the manifest and the generated `design-icons.ts` and icon mappings updated through the existing `scripts/design_assets.py` procedure, whose `--write-icon-module` regenerates `design-icons.ts`, and "Copy message" as its accessible name and tooltip; a reply still streaming has no button until it is saved. The copy goes through the new main broker operation `copyMessage(messageId)`: the window sends only the message identifier; main reads that message's text from the data service, in parts with `text.readPart` when it is longer than 4,096 bytes (STORY-003-010), writes it to the clipboard and returns success only after reading the clipboard back, as `copyVersionInformation` does. Only after that success a neutral `clipboard-check` status reading "Copied" appears beside the button, in `type.ui.caption` with a 16 px icon; a failure shows an inline danger alert with `octagon-alert`: "Ohtia couldn't copy this message. Select its text and copy it manually."

**Measurements,** all expressed with existing tokens unless stated:

- Markdown: `space.2` between blocks; lists indented `space.6` with items `space.1` apart; headings at weight 600; inline code in `type.ui.code` on `surface.emphasis` with `radius.small` and `space.1` inline padding; code and table boxes with a 1 px `border.subtle`, `radius.medium` and `surface.page`, code with `space.2` block and `space.3` inline padding, cells with `space.1` block and `space.2` inline padding and numerals aligned to the end; links in `content.primary`, underlined, with the 16 px `external-link` icon after `space.1`.

**Documentation amendments:**

- A new ADR records the Markdown parser choice and owns its version lines.
- `docs/design-system.md`, "Iconography", fixed mappings: add the row "Copy message | `copy`".
- `docs/application-port.md`, "Named presentation commands": "`openLink()` accepts a project destination ID rather than a URL." becomes "`openLink()` accepts a project destination ID rather than a URL. `openExternalLink(url)` accepts the address of a link in a rendered agent message; main parses it again with the WHATWG URL parser and opens it in the system browser only when it is at most 4,096 bytes and its scheme is `https`, `http` or `mailto`. `copyMessage(messageId)` accepts a message identifier, never text: main reads that message's original text from the data service, writes it to the clipboard and verifies a readback before returning success."

**Decisions and rationale:** The parser parts give Ohtia the syntax tree without any HTML step, which is the only approach compatible with ADR-0014. Links and "Copy message" go through main because the port's `openLink()` accepts only fixed destinations and main's session refuses every permission request and check of the window, the clipboard included.[^port] This work is a story of its own, beside the composer, because only the notices, the history and the consultation card need its output.

**Verification:** Rendering tests with hostile content (`<script>`, `javascript:` links, images, HTML, broken tables and unclosed text) never insert HTML or violate the packaged CSP. Code blocks and wide tables scroll inside their own box at 280 and 320 CSS px, in light and dark themes, and in German, Korean and pseudolocalization. With the fake provider: "Copy message" is an always-visible icon button with its name and tooltip on every saved message and absent from a streaming reply; a failed copy shows its notice, and `copyMessage` of a message over 4,096 bytes copies its exact text; `openExternalLink` refuses a `javascript:` or `file:` address sent directly to main. Automated tests run in the 12 CI cells. With the researcher's account on the x86-64 virtual machines, the development command sends a request and the real reply appears in the panel formatted as it streams.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-017: Message composer

Let the researcher write and send messages, and stop replies, from a composer that behaves like ChatGPT's and Claude's.[^workspace][^frontend]

- **The box.** Always visible below the messages, using `type.ui.body` (16/24 px). It starts at one line attached to the bottom, grows upward line by line up to 8 lines (192 px) and never beyond 40% of the panel height; after that it scrolls inside, keeping the caret visible. It shrinks when text is deleted and returns to one line after sending. It never scrolls horizontally and has no resize handle. Pasting inserts plain text only. Send/Stop sits at the end of the bottom line. The text "Message {agent}", where `{agent}` is the name of the agent that answers the conversation (Virgilio until STORY-003-021), is the placeholder and the accessible name, an exception to the design system's visible-label-above rule.
- **Limit.** 20,000 Unicode code points; a composed emoji counts as several. From 18,000 a counter appears below the box, "{used} / {max}", where `{used}` is the number of code points in the box and `{max}` is 20,000, both formatted as numbers in the interface locale, such as "18,250 / 20,000"; screen readers read it as "{used} of {max} characters", with plural forms. Over the limit, Send is disabled and an attention notice with `triangle-alert`, read with the box, says how many characters to remove: "Remove {excess} characters to send this message.", with plural forms, where `{excess}` is the number of code points over the limit. Ohtia never truncates, including on paste. The data service applies the same limit and rejects an empty, whitespace-only or longer researcher message with `message_invalid` (STORY-003-010), as `frontend-architecture.md` requires domain preconditions to be rechecked.[^frontend]
- **Sending.** Enter sends and Shift+Enter inserts a line break. While an input method such as Korean is composing, Enter finishes the composition and does not send. Empty or whitespace-only messages cannot be sent. Text is stored exactly as written. Send calls `message.append`; a message over 4,096 bytes first crosses the port in parts under the same operation identifier (STORY-003-010), so a 20,000-code-point message is never cut.
- **Destination line.** Below the box, a line states where the message goes, what is sent and the limit, as `frontend-architecture.md` requires, with the values of the operation and connection that will answer: for a `subscription` connection "To {service} ({email}) · This conversation · Stops after {limit}", and for an `api_key` connection "To {service} · API key · This conversation · Stops after {limit}", where `{service}` is the connection's display name (STORY-003-001), `{email}` its account email and `{limit}` the operation's `max_attempt_duration_ms` in whole minutes, such as "10 min". The values come from the new read `destination.read`, which takes the project and either a conversation or, for a conversation not created yet, the agent that will answer it, `virgilio` until STORY-003-021 lets the researcher choose. It returns what the next message there would create: the operation that the routing of `message.append` and `conversation.start` assigns, that operation's current `max_attempt_duration_ms` and the connection its task would use, whatever that connection's state; when no operation would answer, it returns none and the line is not shown. The line takes the connection's display name and kind from its declaration (STORY-003-001) and its email from `connection.read`. The window reads `destination.read` when it shows a conversation or a new one, and again when `research.subscribe` names the open project or the shown conversation.
- **Send and Stop.** One icon button with a tooltip. While the save of a message that this box sent awaits its result, it is Stop, whatever the box holds. After any result of that save, the button follows the box: with text, it is Send: icon Lucide `arrow-up`, accessible name and tooltip "Send message". A box that holds only whitespace counts as empty. With an empty box and a reply running or waiting in this conversation, it is Stop: icon Lucide `square`, accessible name and tooltip "Stop replies". With an empty box and nothing to stop, Send stays visible and focusable but is marked `aria-disabled`, with the tooltip and description "Write a message to send it."; over the limit, its description is the over-limit notice. Both icons are vendored from the `lucide-static` 1.66.0 package pinned in `packages/renderer/icons/manifest.json`, with their hashes in the manifest and the generated `design-icons.ts` and icon mappings updated through the existing `scripts/design_assets.py` procedure, whose `--write-icon-module` regenerates `design-icons.ts`. Sending turns it into Stop at once, before the save returns its result and before the provider answers. A Stop pressed while that save awaits its result is bound to the send, as `workspace-layout.md` requires: the button becomes disabled as after Stop, and Ohtia sends `conversation.stop` once the save returns any result, confirmed, failed or unconfirmed (Text after sending), so the new message's reply stops too when the message was saved, and a reply already running stops in every case. A second message while a reply streams waits and coalesces (STORY-003-005). Stop stops everything in this conversation, the running reply and the waiting one, through one conversation-level command, `conversation.stop`, with one operation identifier, that applies SPEC-003-012's Stop to every unfinished task of the conversation in one transaction; the researcher's messages stay saved and the next send includes them. After Stop the button stays disabled until Ohtia confirms the result, with the tooltip and description "Wait until the replies stop.", which also shows below the box in secondary text while the button is disabled, so that the reason is visible, as `design-system.md` requires of a disabled button; the over-limit notice is the visible reason over the limit, and the empty box itself is the reason for a disabled Send, the exception recorded below. Double clicks never send twice or stop twice, because each action carries its own operation identifier.
- **Text after sending.** The box clears only when Ohtia confirms the message was saved; text typed meanwhile is kept. A failed reply never resends the message by itself. Every other result of the save keeps the text in the box and shows its own notice below the box, read with it, because `frontend-architecture.md` requires a rejection, a failure and an uncertain result to be shown distinctly; `conversation.start` (STORY-003-018) shows the same notices:[^frontend]
  - `pending_limit_reached`: an attention notice with `triangle-alert`, announced as a status: "Too many replies are waiting. Your message wasn't sent and is still in the box; send it again when some finish."
  - `input_too_large`: an attention notice with `triangle-alert`, announced as a status: "This message is too long for {agent} to read. Your message wasn't sent and is still in the box; shorten it and send it again.", where `{agent}` is the agent that answers the conversation.
  - An unconfirmed result, which the port reports as `uncertain`, `timeout`, `cancelled`, `disposed` or `invalid_service_result`, after which the message may or may not be saved: an attention notice with `triangle-alert`, announced as a status: "Ohtia couldn't confirm that your message was saved. Send it again to check; it won't be saved twice." Send resends the same text with the same operation identifier, which returns the first result when the message was saved; once the text in the box changes, the next Send uses a new identifier.
  - `parts_incomplete`: the window sends every part and the command once more with the same identifier, and a second `parts_incomplete` shows the failure notice below.
  - Any other error, including `message_invalid` and `project_in_setup`, which the composer's own checks prevent: a danger notice with `octagon-alert`, announced as an alert: "Ohtia couldn't save your message. Your text is still in the box; send it again to retry."; Send retries with the same identifier, so the message is never stored twice.
- **Reuse.** Each researcher message has a "Reuse message" button that copies its text to the box: the second button of its actions row (STORY-003-010), an always-visible quiet icon button with the Lucide `text-cursor-input` icon, vendored like `arrow-up`, and "Reuse message" as its accessible name and tooltip. If the box has text, Ohtia asks before replacing it, as `workspace-layout.md` requires: a confirmation dialog titled "Replace the text in the box?", with the body "Reusing this message replaces the text you're writing.", the primary button "Replace text" and "Close dialog", which has the initial focus and keeps the box unchanged. "Close dialog" is a secondary footer button with the Lucide `x` icon, the design system's icon for closing a dialog, placed before the primary button; the dialog has no close button in its header. Every confirmation dialog of this EPIC that offers "Close dialog" draws it this way.
- **Below the box.** What shows below the box keeps this order: the counter, then a notice, then the reason of a disabled button, then the destination line. The counter, the disabled reason and the destination line use `type.ui.caption` in `content.secondary`, the counter aligned to the inline end with tabular numerals.
- **Unsent text.** Kept in memory while the window is open, never written to disk.
- **Screen readers.** The counter and notices are read with the box, and the change from Send to Stop is announced politely: "Sending your message. The button now stops the replies."
- **Port.** This story carries through the application port its own `conversation.stop` and `destination.read` and the command `task.stop` of STORY-003-007, which STORY-003-010 does not expose (SPEC-003-002).

The agent selector is STORY-003-021 and the status notices are STORY-003-013.

**Measurements,** all expressed with existing tokens unless stated:

- Composer: the region has `space.3` block and `space.4` inline padding, a `space.2` gap and a top `border.subtle`; the box has `space.1` padding, a 1 px `border.control`, `radius.medium` and `surface.page`, and the focus ring when focused; the input has `space.1` block and `space.2` inline padding and grows to 8 lines of 24 px plus that padding; the placeholder uses `content.secondary`. Send and Stop are 32 × 32 px with `radius.small` in `action.primary.*`; disabled, they are transparent with a 1 px `content.disabled` ring, and every disabled primary button of this EPIC looks the same.

**Documentation amendments:**

- `docs/workspace-layout.md` scenario V17: "Use a synthetic 20000-code-point input limit to test emoji and over-limit rejection without truncation; production limits come from the enabled operation." becomes "Use the composer's 20000-code-point input limit to test emoji and over-limit rejection without truncation; that limit is fixed and is not taken from the enabled operation."
- `docs/design-system.md`, "Base components", Text field: after "Optional fields are marked, not required ones." add "The conversation composer is an exception to the visible label above: its placeholder, “Message {agent}”, is also its accessible name, as in ChatGPT and Claude."
- `docs/design-system.md`, "Base components", Button: after "Prefer allowing the action and explaining the error over disabling it; if disabled, the reason is visible." add "The conversation composer's send button is the one exception: while the box is empty it is disabled without a separate visible reason, because the empty box shows it, and its tooltip and accessible description state it."
- `docs/design-system.md`, "Iconography", fixed mappings: add the row "Reuse message | `text-cursor-input`".

**Decisions and rationale:** The box geometry was measured on ChatGPT's page (17 px text, 24 px lines, one line at start, 192 px or 8 lines at most at 800 and 1,100 px windows) and adapted to Ohtia's 16/24 body type. Unsent text stays in memory so that private unsent writing never reaches the disk. A Stop pressed before the save returns waits for its result, because a Stop committed first would find nothing to stop and the new message's reply would then run; it is sent whatever the result, so a reply already running always stops.

**Verification:** Automated tests cover Enter, Shift+Enter and Enter during composition; 20,000 code points with emoji, sent in parts and stored exactly, and an over-limit paste without truncation; the destination line of a `subscription` and of an `api_key` connection, with a fake API-key connection, read with `destination.read` for an existing conversation and for one not created yet; double clicks; a slow confirmation and a failed save that keeps the text with its notice; a pending-limit rejection, an `input_too_large` rejection and an unconfirmed result, each with its own notice, and a resend after an unconfirmed result that saves the message once; text typed after sending that is not erased; Stop before the provider answers; Stop pressed while a 20,000-code-point message is still being saved, which stops the new message's reply once the save is confirmed and still stops the running reply when the save fails or is unconfirmed; Send, not Stop, after an unconfirmed result while the box holds the text; Stop and completion at the same time in both orders; the reason of a disabled Stop visible below the box; the counter, the over-limit notice in its plural forms and the names, icons and disabled reasons of Send and Stop; "Reuse message" as an always-visible icon button with its name and tooltip, and with a full box, whose dialog keeps the text unless "Replace text" is chosen and opens with focus on the footer's "Close dialog"; the order below the box: counter, notice, disabled reason and destination line; and the V07 cases of `workspace-layout.md` for multiline preview and reuse with prior input at 280 CSS px: a multiline message shows its lines in the growing box before it is sent, and reusing a message with text in the box asks first. They run in the 12 CI cells. Real keyboards are tested on the x86-64 virtual machines: IBus Hangul on GNOME and KDE in Ubuntu 26.04, Fedora 44 and Debian 13, and the Microsoft Korean keyboard on Windows; macOS keyboards are tested in CI with simulated events. With the researcher's account in the development project: writing and sending, a second message while a reply streams, and Stop.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-018: Task notices

Give every task state from STORY-003-005 to STORY-003-009 its notice in the conversation.[^research-presentation][^siwc-errors]

Each notice appears where the task's reply goes: once the task has started, at the reply position it reserved (STORY-003-005), even when no reply text was saved there and even when researcher messages written meanwhile appear below it; before it starts, below the last message it answers. Each notice shows an icon and text and never relies on color alone. Its box holds only that icon and text; its buttons and links, such as "Resume {service} requests ({n})", "Check usage", "Try again" and "Start reply again", sit below the box on the chat panel's surface, as `research-presentation.md` requires (SPEC-003-002). A notice shows the answering agent's symbol and name through `VoiceLabel`, `space.2` above the notice box, only when no text of that agent is directly above it; a notice right below its agent's message, such as "Incomplete — stopped by you" or the uncertain notice below a partial reply, does not repeat the label. Status notices carry `info`, the usage-pause and uncertain notices `triangle-alert`, and failed notices `octagon-alert`, the design system's icons for information, attention and operation failure. A status notice uses `surface.emphasis` with a 1 px `border.subtle` border, so it stands out from the chat panel's `surface.panel`; attention and failed notices keep the design system's `attention.*` and `danger.*` tones. `{service}` is the display name of the task's connection (STORY-003-001), "ChatGPT" in this story; `{agent}` is the name of the agent that answers the task; `{running_agent}` is the name of the agent whose task runs now in the same conversation; `{limit}` and `{inactivity}` are the task policy's `max_attempt_duration_ms` and `max_inactivity_ms`, written in whole minutes in the interface locale's unit format, such as "10 min" and "2 min". A text whose meaning depends on how the service charges has one variant per kind of connection, `subscription` and `api_key`, never one per company; this story shows the `subscription` variants, and STORY-003-036 and STORY-003-037 show the `api_key` variants through the same notices.

- **Waiting.** "Waiting for {running_agent} to finish the current reply." when another task in this conversation runs, such as Josefa's consultation while Virgilio's reply waits (STORY-003-022); "Waiting to start: the limit of {running_limit} replies at the same time is reached." when waiting for a slot, where `{running_limit}` is the limit that applies (the general limit or the connection's), with plural forms in every catalog.
- **Replying.** Before the first text: "Sent to {service}. Waiting for the reply." Then the streamed text (STORY-003-010). "Thinking" is not used, because `agent-identity.md` asks to describe the real phase.
- **Blocked without a connection.** "{service} isn't connected. {agent} will reply when you connect.", with an "Open Connections" button.
- **Blocked by a usage pause.** For a `subscription` connection: "You've reached your {service} plan's usage limit. Ohtia paused new requests to {service}."; for an `api_key` connection: "{service} says this key's account has no credit or reached its spending limit. Ohtia paused new requests to {service}." When the connection declares a usage address, a "Check usage" link opens it in the system browser (`https://chatgpt.com/settings/usage` for ChatGPT), with the `external-link` icon, whose accessible name is "opens in your browser" (STORY-003-011). The button "Resume {service} requests ({n})" runs `usagePause.resume` (STORY-003-006), which closes that connection's pause and resumes every task it holds; `{n}` is the number of tasks the pause holds, in all projects, formatted as a number. The notice never states when the plan renews or the credit returns: OpenAI asks Sign in with ChatGPT clients not to infer a reset time, and says that retrying an API key's billing error does not help (STORY-003-037).
- **Paused by a crash.** "Ohtia stopped before this reply started.", with "Continue all ({n})", which runs `task.continueAll` (STORY-003-008); `{n}` is the number of tasks paused by the restart, in all projects, formatted as a number. The text says "stopped" because the same pause follows a crash, a power loss, a forced quit, an operating-system shutdown or logout, and a data-service restart while Ohtia stays open (STORY-003-008 and STORY-003-009).
- **Pause counts.** Both counts come from the new read `pause.read`, which returns, across all projects, the number of tasks that each open usage pause holds and the number paused by a restart, with the counts' revision. The counts can change while no record that `research.subscribe` names changes, so this story adds to that subscription (STORY-003-010) a pause-count event that carries the counts' revision. The data service sends it, and advances the revision, in every transaction that opens or closes a usage pause or moves a task of any project into or out of `blocked` with `usage_limit_reached` or `restart_paused`, by blocking, resuming, coalescing, Stop or cancellation; the revision starts at 1 each time the data service starts. The window reads `pause.read` again when an event carries a revision it doesn't have, and after a `sequence_gap`.
- **Stopping.** "Stopping…", with Stop disabled.
- **Stopped.** A stopped task that saved partial text shows only the partial-text notice "Incomplete — stopped by you" below that text, with the usage line below; "Stopped." appears only when no text was saved. For a task whose request was dispatched and that saved no text: "Stopped." and, in small text, "{service} doesn't confirm whether the reply stopped on its side, so its full usage is unknown.", one text for both kinds of connection, because no service of this EPIC confirms it (STORY-003-007, STORY-003-036 and STORY-003-037). For a task stopped before its request was sent: "Stopped before it was sent.", with no usage line, because nothing was sent. A task cancelled by coalescing shows no notice; the task that replaces it shows its own.
- **Partial text.** Below an incomplete message: "Incomplete — stopped by you", "Incomplete — time limit reached", "Incomplete — no activity for {inactivity}", "Incomplete — longer than Ohtia allows", "Incomplete — {service} reported an error", after an error in the stream that fails with `provider_error` or `provider_unavailable` (STORY-003-003), "Incomplete — Ohtia couldn't read the rest of the reply", after an event that fails with `invalid_output`, or "Incomplete — connection lost". "Incomplete — stopped by you" of a dispatched task carries, in small text, the same usage line as "Stopped.".
- **Failed.** The reason in plain words and what to do:
  - `usage_limit_reached`: the usage-pause notice above, with its "Check usage" link. Only the conversation's latest reply task that failed this way shows the buttons, "Resume {service} requests ({n})" and then "Try again"; an earlier reply that failed this way shows the notice with no buttons, because "Try again" exists only on the latest reply task;
  - `not_eligible`: for a `subscription` connection "{service} doesn't allow this account to use its plan in Ohtia. Sign out in Settings › Connections, then choose “Sign in with another account”, or connect another service.", where “Sign in with another account” is STORY-003-002's button; for an `api_key` connection "{service} doesn't allow this key to make this request. Check the key's permissions in your {service} account, or replace it in Settings › Connections."; both with "Open Connections". OpenAI's errors page does not state which plans qualify, so the notice names none;[^siwc-errors]
  - `connection_expired`: for a `subscription` connection "Your {service} session expired. Sign in again in Settings › Connections."; for an `api_key` connection "{service} no longer accepts this key. Replace it in Settings › Connections."; both with "Open Connections", placed before "Try again" on the latest reply task;
  - `provider_unavailable`: "{service} isn't available right now.";
  - `provider_error`: "{service} reported an error.";
  - `request_rejected`: "{service} rejected the request.";
  - `invalid_output`: "Ohtia couldn't read {service}'s reply.";
  - `empty_reply`: "{agent} sent an empty reply.";
  - `output_limit_exceeded`: "The reply was longer than Ohtia allows.";
  - `deadline_exceeded`: "The reply took longer than {limit}.";
  - `inactivity_timeout`: "{service} stopped sending text for {inactivity}.";
  - `service_restarted` or `permit_expired` with no attempt left: "This reply couldn't start: both attempts ended before it was sent."
- **Try again.** Only the conversation's latest reply task offers "Try again", when it failed for any reason except `not_eligible`. The new command `task.tryAgain` creates a new task linked by `replaces_task_id` that covers the conversation up to its last message, like "Start reply again", except a partial reply that the failed task saved: the new task takes the failed task's `input_through_position`, so that partial reply stays out of its input, which ends with a researcher message or another agent's message in the `user` role (STORY-003-005). The new reply takes a new reply position, below the failed task's notice. The failed task is never reopened, as ADR-0011 requires. When the failed task's connection still reports a model list with no entry whose `visibility` is `list` (STORY-003-005), the data service sends main `model_list_refresh` with that connection in the same step that creates the new task, and the new task stays queued until main's report arrives, then runs or fails under STORY-003-005's model rule. A failed task that is no longer the latest offers no "Try again", because the newer task already answers every message before it; if a newer task appears between showing the button and running the command, the command is rejected with `newer_task_exists` and changes nothing, and an attention notice with `triangle-alert`, announced as a status, appears below the button: "A newer reply already answers these messages." A task that did not fail, or failed with `not_eligible`, is rejected with `task_not_retryable`. Like every command that creates a task, `task.tryAgain` is rejected with `pending_limit_reached` at its operation's pending limit (STORY-003-005); that rejection, and the same one of `task.startAgain`, shows below the button an attention notice with `triangle-alert`, announced as a status: "Too many replies are waiting. Nothing was started; try again when some finish." A reply task is one that answers the conversation's messages; a consultation, contribution or synthesis task that failed offers its own "Try again", which repeats its own request, under the rules of its block (STORY-003-022, STORY-003-023 and STORY-003-026).
- **Uncertain.** For a `subscription` connection: "Ohtia lost contact with {service} while it was replying. {service} may have finished and counted this reply against your plan; Ohtia can't know."; for an `api_key` connection: "Ohtia lost contact with {service} while it was replying. {service} may have finished and charged this reply to your key's account; Ohtia can't know." Its button "Start reply again" opens a confirmation titled "Start this reply again?" with the body "Starting again may count this reply against your plan twice." (`subscription`) or "Starting again may charge this reply to your key's account twice." (`api_key`) and the buttons "Start reply again" and "Close dialog", with initial focus on "Close dialog" as `design-system.md` requires for a consequential choice; accepting records the acknowledgement (STORY-003-007). Only the conversation's latest reply task offers "Start reply again"; when `task.startAgain` is rejected with `newer_task_exists` because a newer task appeared meanwhile, the same attention notice as for "Try again", "A newer reply already answers these messages.", appears below the button.
- **Button results.** "Resume {service} requests ({n})", "Continue all ({n})", "Try again" and "Start reply again" keep their notice while their command runs. After every success of `usagePause.resume` or `task.continueAll`, including one without change because another window already resumed the pause or continued the paused tasks, the window reads `pause.read` again and the notices follow the tasks' current states. A rejection shows its notice above; any other failure shows below the button a danger notice with `octagon-alert`, announced as an alert, "Ohtia couldn't save this action. Nothing changed; you can try again.", and the button repeats the command with the same operation identifier; an unconfirmed result, `uncertain`, `timeout`, `cancelled`, `disposed` or `invalid_service_result`, shows an attention notice with `triangle-alert`, announced as a status, "Ohtia couldn't confirm that this action was saved. Use the button again to check; it won't run twice.", and the button repeats the command with the same identifier, which returns its first result when it was saved.
- **Destination line.** Changes in two cases, while messages are still saved and wait: without a connection, "{service} isn't connected. Messages will wait until you connect."; and while a usage pause is open, "Requests to {service} are paused: plan limit reached. Messages will wait." (`subscription`) or "Requests to {service} are paused: no credit or spending limit reached. Messages will wait." (`api_key`). STORY-003-035 and STORY-003-036 each add one case, for an agent without a usable model and for a service that asked Ohtia to wait.
- **Screen readers.** Each notice is announced once and never moves focus. Waiting, replying, stopping, stopped, blocked-without-connection, crash-pause, partial-text and newer-reply notices are status messages; failed, uncertain and usage-pause notices are alerts, as `research-presentation.md` requires.
- **Other blocking reasons.** `permission_required` and `input_changed` do not occur in this EPIC; `configuration_unavailable` first occurs in STORY-003-035, which adds its notice.
- **Port.** This story carries through the application port its own `task.tryAgain` and `pause.read` and the commands `usagePause.resume`, `task.startAgain` and `task.continueAll` of STORY-003-006 to STORY-003-008, which STORY-003-010 does not expose (SPEC-003-002).

Per-reply usage belongs to STORY-003-034.

**Measurements,** all expressed with existing tokens unless stated:

- Notices: as in EPIC-002, `space.2` block and `space.3` inline padding, `radius.medium`, a 1 px border in the state's border token, a 16 px icon and a `space.2` gap in `type.ui.body-small`; a task notice's `VoiceLabel` sits `space.2` above it, and its small text and usage line use `type.ui.caption`; its buttons and links form a row `space.2` below the box, outside it, with a `space.2` gap, and wrap at 280 CSS px.

**Decisions and rationale:** "Try again" extends `replaces_task_id` beyond uncertain replacement and coalescing, so every replacement stays traceable. It is offered only on the latest reply task, so an old reply never lands after newer ones, and it covers the conversation like "Start reply again". The usage pause and the restart pause have different buttons because they run different commands, and both count paused tasks in every project because both resume them all. Service names come from the task's connection, with one variant per kind of connection where the meaning differs, so later connections reuse the same notices.

**Verification:** With the fake provider, each state and reason shows its notice at 280 CSS px, in German, Korean and pseudolocalization, announced once, in its `subscription` variant and, with a fake API-key connection, its `api_key` variant. Each notice carries its icon, and every notice's buttons and links sit below its box, never inside it. Double clicks on "Resume {service} requests ({n})", "Continue all ({n})", "Try again" or "Start reply again" create no extra tasks. A notice right below its agent's text shows no `VoiceLabel`; a stopped task with saved text shows only "Incomplete — stopped by you" with the usage line; only the latest reply that failed with `usage_limit_reached` shows "Resume {service} requests ({n})" and "Try again", and an earlier one shows its notice without buttons; status notices use `surface.emphasis` with a `border.subtle` border. "Try again" appears only on the latest reply task, leaves a partial reply of the failed task out of the new task's input and is rejected, with its notice, when a newer task exists; "Try again" after a failure for an empty model list sends `model_list_refresh` and its new task waits queued for main's report; "Start reply again" likewise appears only on the latest reply task and shows the same notice when rejected. At the pending limit, "Try again" and "Start reply again" show "Too many replies are waiting. Nothing was started; try again when some finish."; a failed and an unconfirmed result of each of the four buttons show their notices, and repeating an unconfirmed one creates no extra task. "Resume {service} requests ({n})" and "Continue all ({n})" pressed in a second window after the first window already ran them succeed without change, and the second window reads `pause.read` again. Each `{n}` counts paused tasks of other projects too, and changes, through the pause-count event, when tasks of another project are blocked, resumed, coalesced or stopped; after a sequence gap the window reads `pause.read` again. "Check usage" opens the system browser. Automated tests run in the 12 CI cells. With the researcher's account on the x86-64 virtual machines: disconnecting ChatGPT in Settings leaves a message waiting and reconnecting gets Virgilio's reply; cutting the virtual machine's network mid-reply leaves the task uncertain when the connection reports the loss, or failed with `inactivity_timeout` after the inactivity limit when it does not, each with its notice, and "Start reply again" or "Try again" answers it. Plan exhaustion is tested with the fake provider only.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-019: Project setup operation

Let Virgilio agree a project's working title and document language with the researcher through a proposal the researcher confirms.[^data-model]

- **When.** In a project in setup, every researcher message creates a task of the new operation `project_setup`, answered by Virgilio. The window arrives in STORY-003-015; until then the development command exercises it with the `create-project`, `send`, `confirm-setup` and `reject-setup` actions (STORY-003-003) in ordinary projects. Development projects keep their `development_test` routing after this story.
- **Project types.** Each type in Ohtia's catalog has an English name and description for instructions and translated names for the interface. The thesis is "Thesis: the document a student writes and defends to obtain an academic degree." The type is stored when the project is created and passed to Virgilio; Virgilio does not detect it.
- **Instructions** (English, instruction version 1):

  ```text
  You are Virgilio, the research guide in Ohtia, a desktop application where researchers write research documents with the help of AI agents.

  The researcher has just created a new project.
  Project type: {project_type_name}. {project_type_description}

  Your task in this conversation is limited to agreeing with the researcher on two facts:

  1. A working title for the document.
  2. The language in which the document will be written.

  How to work:
  - Reply in the language the researcher writes in.
  - Keep replies short and ask one question at a time.
  - If the researcher has no title yet, ask about the topic and suggest a provisional title built from their own words. Do not invent a topic, a method or findings.
  - The document language can differ from the language of this conversation. Ask if it is not clear.
  - Express the language as a BCP 47 tag of the form language[-script][-region]: a language subtag; then a script subtag only when the researcher indicates a writing system (for example sr-Latn); then a region subtag only when the researcher indicates a regional variety (for example es-AR, pt-BR, en-GB or es-419). Never guess a script or a region.
  - Do not give research advice yet. If asked, say that you will help with the research once the project is set up.
  - When both facts are clear, write one sentence summarizing them and call propose_project_setup once, with the title exactly as agreed and the language tag.
  - Ohtia shows your proposal to the researcher, who confirms or rejects it. You cannot confirm it, and you must never say that the project has been created or saved.
  - If the researcher corrects or rejects a proposal, continue the conversation and make a new proposal when both facts are clear again.
  ```

- **Tool.** Grouped in the `ohtia` namespace with strict parameters:

  ```json
  {
    "type": "namespace",
    "name": "ohtia",
    "description": "Actions that Ohtia shows to the researcher for confirmation.",
    "tools": [
      {
        "type": "function",
        "name": "propose_project_setup",
        "description": "Propose the working title and the language of the document text. The researcher confirms or rejects the proposal in Ohtia.",
        "strict": true,
        "parameters": {
          "type": "object",
          "properties": {
            "title": {
              "type": "string",
              "description": "Working title exactly as agreed with the researcher."
            },
            "document_language": {
              "type": "string",
              "description": "BCP 47 tag of the form language[-script][-region]: a script subtag only for a writing system and a region subtag only for a regional variety that the researcher indicated."
            }
          },
          "required": ["title", "document_language"],
          "additionalProperties": false
        }
      }
    ]
  }
  ```

  The call arrives as a `function_call` output item with separate `namespace` and `name` fields, and its arguments stream through `response.function_call_arguments.delta` and `response.function_call_arguments.done`. OpenAI's reference defines this namespace shape but does not state verbatim that the top-level `tools` list accepts it; the documented alternative is an `additional_tools` input item with the developer role. The first real-account test uses the top-level list and falls back to `additional_tools` if ChatGPT rejects it; the working route is recorded before the story closes.[^siwc-models]

- **Adapter contract.** This story extends the adapter contract of STORY-003-003, the fake provider and the conformance suite: the request data gains the permitted tools, each with its namespace, name, description and strict parameter schema; and the events gain a tool-call event with its sequence number, namespace, name and complete arguments. Main buffers every tool call of the attempt, complete or not, and emits the complete calls, in the order received, only after the provider reports the response completed within the output budget, just before the completed result; while the response streams, main emits no tool-call event, and a response that ends failed, cancelled, uncertain or past the budget emits none, because main then discards every buffered call. Main enforces the attempt's output budget, `max_output_bytes` from the request data's limits (STORY-003-005), with one count per attempt of the UTF-8 bytes of every text part and every tool-call argument part received, added as each part arrives and never reduced, so a completed and buffered call keeps its bytes in the count: when a text part or an argument part would take that count past `max_output_bytes`, main stops accumulating, closes the request's connection as for `provider_interrupt`, forwards the longest beginning of an overflowing text part that fits and ends at a whole code point, discards every buffered call, emits no tool-call event for the attempt, and returns the attempt failed with the new failure reason `output_limit_exceeded`. The data service saves the text it received as the reply, which STORY-003-007 marks `partial`, and the failed attempt applies none of its tool calls (Only completed replies propose). Conformance checks that an adapter sends exactly the permitted tools, reports each call once in order, reports a call whose arguments never complete as part of the failed or uncertain result, never as a call, that a complete call followed by more streamed text is emitted only after the response completes, and that a response of tool calls alone whose arguments pass `max_output_bytes`, a complete first call followed by text that passes it, and a complete first call followed by a second call whose arguments pass it each end with `output_limit_exceeded` and no tool-call event, with main never holding more than `max_output_bytes` of output for the attempt.[^adr-0029]
- **Input.** The instructions; the conversation's messages, the researcher's and Virgilio's, newest first up to the input limits, whole messages only, with the English note "Earlier messages in this conversation are not included." when older ones are left out, and sent oldest first; the cut notes of STORY-003-007; and, for each proposal of the conversation, one of the notes below, an English input item with the `user` role (STORY-003-005). Each goes right after the message of the reply that proposed it or, when that reply saved no message, at that reply's position, between the messages around it, and is sent only when that place lies among the messages sent. `{title}` and `{document_language}` are the proposal's values as received. A `confirmed` proposal has no note, because a confirmed project creates no more `project_setup` tasks.
  - `proposed`: "Note from Ohtia: at this point you proposed a setup that is waiting for the researcher to confirm or reject it.", followed by the two lines "Working title: {title}" and "Document language: {document_language}".
  - `replaced`: "Note from Ohtia: at this point you proposed a setup that a later proposal of yours replaced.", followed by the same two lines.
  - `rejected`: "Note from Ohtia: at this point you proposed a setup that the researcher rejected; nothing was saved.", followed by the same two lines.
  - `invalid` with `multiple_calls`: "Note from Ohtia: at this point you called propose_project_setup more than once in one reply, so Ohtia accepted none of the calls. The researcher was told."
  - `invalid` with `title_empty`: "Note from Ohtia: at this point Ohtia did not accept your proposal because its title was empty. The researcher was told."
  - `invalid` with `title_whitespace`: "Note from Ohtia: at this point Ohtia did not accept your proposal because its title started or ended with whitespace. The researcher was told."
  - `invalid` with `title_too_long`: "Note from Ohtia: at this point Ohtia did not accept your proposal because its title was longer than 200 characters. The researcher was told."
  - `invalid` with `language_malformed`: "Note from Ohtia: at this point Ohtia did not accept your proposal because its language was not a BCP 47 tag made of a language subtag, an optional script subtag and an optional region subtag. The researcher was told."
  - `invalid` with `language_unknown`: "Note from Ohtia: at this point Ohtia did not accept your proposal because its language tag contains a subtag that does not exist, or is deprecated, in the IANA Language Subtag Registry. The researcher was told."
- **Model.** `project_setup` tasks request the model that STORY-003-005's model rule gives every task until STORY-003-035: the first model of the connection's list, which main reads on connection and again when it is older than one day. The attempt records the requested and the observed model.
- **Policy `project_setup`, version 1.** `max_pending_tasks` 20; `max_input_bytes` 64 KiB and `max_input_items` 200; `max_output_bytes` 32 KiB, about 5,000 words; `max_work_units` 1; `max_attempt_duration_ms` 5 minutes; `max_inactivity_ms` 120 seconds; `stop_grace_ms` 5 seconds; `max_attempts` 2, `max_auto_retries` 0, `retry_backoff_ms` 0. When the pending limit is reached, `message.append` rejects the message with `pending_limit_reached` and saves nothing, under the pending-limit rule of STORY-003-005, as ADR-0011 requires a request over its admission cap to be rejected before acceptance; the development command prints the rejection, and from STORY-003-015 on the composer keeps the message with an attention notice with `triangle-alert`, announced as a status, "Too many replies are waiting. Your message wasn't sent and is still in the box; send it again when some finish.", the same notice that `direct_guidance` uses (STORY-003-017).[^adr-0011]
- **Valid proposal.** The title has 1 to 200 Unicode code points without leading or trailing whitespace; the language tag has the form that `projects` checks and exists in the bundled IANA registry copy (STORY-003-004); Virgilio calls the tool once per reply. Otherwise the proposal is invalid with its reason. A reply with more than one accepted call stores one invalid proposal with `multiple_calls` and the first call's values. A single call is checked in the order `title_empty`, `title_whitespace`, `title_too_long`, `language_malformed`, `language_unknown`, and the first reason that applies is stored.
- **Only completed replies propose.** Only the transaction that completes the task stores the proposals of its reply. When the attempt ends stopped, failed, including by a time or output limit, or uncertain, its tool calls are discarded and store nothing, even when their arguments were complete, because ADR-0011 lets only a valid finalization publish research effects; its partial text is still saved (STORY-003-007). Every proposal tool of this EPIC follows this rule.[^adr-0011]
- **Reply with only a proposal.** A reply whose only output is the tool call saves no message, because the database rejects empty content (STORY-003-004); its task still completes, and the proposal records the task's `task_id`, so its card appears at that task's `reply_position`, where the reply would have been (STORY-003-005 and STORY-003-010). Every proposal tool of this EPIC follows this rule.
- **Empty reply.** STORY-003-005's `empty_reply` now covers a completed reply with neither text nor a tool call that Ohtia accepts. Ohtia accepts a call that names one of the task's permitted tools and whose arguments match that tool's schema; a proposal that Ohtia then stores as invalid for its content, such as `title_too_long`, still counts as an accepted call, because its notice already tells the researcher what happened. A call to a tool the task was not given, or with arguments outside its schema, is ignored, so a reply that carried only such calls fails with `empty_reply`.
- **`project_setup_proposals`:** `id` (UUIDv7); `project_id`; `task_id` and `attempt_id`, the task and attempt whose reply proposed it; `title` and `document_language` as received; `state` (`proposed`, `replaced`, `rejected`, `confirmed` or `invalid`); `invalid_reason` (`title_empty`, `title_whitespace`, `title_too_long`, `language_malformed`, `language_unknown` or `multiple_calls`), only when invalid; `created_at`; `decided_at`; `decided_by_operation_id`. Each project has at most one waiting and one confirmed proposal. A new valid proposal replaces the waiting one in the transaction that completes its task and saves the reply; an invalid proposal never replaces a waiting valid one. A proposal's state changes at most once, so its timeline revision (STORY-003-010) is 1 while it is `proposed` or `invalid` and 2 once it is `replaced`, `rejected` or `confirmed`. One table serves every project type; a later type that needs more facts adds columns.
- **Confirm.** `project.confirmSetup` now takes the proposal's identifier instead of title and language (changing STORY-003-004's operation). Only the waiting proposal can be confirmed, and only while no setup reply runs or waits in the conversation: otherwise the command fails with `proposal_not_waiting`, which also covers a project already confirmed, or with `setup_reply_active`, the proposal check first, and changes nothing. STORY-003-004's `project_not_in_setup` no longer applies to this command. One transaction writes title, document language and confirmation time to the project and marks the proposal confirmed.
- **Reject.** The new command `project.rejectSetup`, with the proposal's identifier, marks the waiting proposal rejected; Virgilio learns it in his next reply. A proposal that is not waiting fails with `proposal_not_waiting` and changes nothing.
