This story adds `proposal_not_waiting`, next action `refresh_record`, and `setup_reply_active`, next action `retry_later`, to the port's closed `errorActions` (SPEC-003-015).
- **After confirmation.** New messages are saved but no longer create setup tasks; STORY-003-017 defines who answers.
- **Port.** When STORY-003-010 merged before this story, this story carries the changed `project.confirmSetup` and the new `project.rejectSetup` through the application port, with their error codes; otherwise STORY-003-010 does (SPEC-003-002).

**Documentation amendments:**

- `docs/agents/agent-virgilio.md`, "Operations": before the heading "Clarify a research question", add the level-three heading "Set up a project" followed by these four paragraphs: "**Operation:** `project_setup`."; "**Input:** The new project's type with its description, the setup conversation's messages and Ohtia's notes on the earlier proposals of that conversation."; "**Output:** Short replies in the language the researcher writes in that agree on a working title and the document's language, and one proposal of both through the `propose_project_setup` tool, which the researcher confirms or rejects in Ohtia."; and "Virgilio gives no research advice before setup ends, never guesses a script or region for the language, cannot confirm the proposal and never says that the project was created or saved."; in "Evaluation and enablement", "Enable only the evaluated scope after research-quality and adapter-conformance requirements pass." becomes "Enable an operation once its adapter conformance and operational checks pass; Ohtia does not grade the quality of the models researchers connect."; in the same section, "Declare rubrics, severity definitions, quality thresholds and bounded work/cost/latency before trials. Compare direct and coordinated use with a capable single-agent baseline where relevant under comparable evidence and total budgets. Human assessors need appropriate disciplinary and language competence." becomes "Research-quality review is optional and gates no enablement, because Ohtia does not grade the models researchers connect, and it compares no single-agent baseline. When the owner runs one, declare its rubrics, severity definitions and bounded work/cost/latency before the trials; human assessors need appropriate disciplinary and language competence."; and "Assess usefulness of the next action, preservation of researcher choices, warranted routing, faithful attribution, honest coverage and whether coordination improves the result enough to justify total work and review burden." becomes "An optional quality review assesses usefulness of the next action, preservation of researcher choices, warranted routing, faithful attribution and honest coverage."
- `docs/agents/agent-collaboration-evaluation.md`: remove the research-quality gate on enablement: "Enable only the evaluated operation/language/runtime scope after its operational guarantees and owner-defined quality thresholds are satisfied." becomes "Enable an operation/language/runtime scope after its operational guarantees are satisfied."; the frontmatter description "Evaluate research usefulness, evidence fidelity, human review, and bounded execution against a single-agent baseline." becomes "Verify bounded execution and collaboration scenarios, and review research usefulness, evidence fidelity and human review as optional material that gates no enablement."; in "Purpose and evaluation unit", "Evaluate whether agent collaboration improves research work enough to justify its coordination cost." becomes "Verify that agent collaboration stays within its bounds and preserves evidence, attribution and human review; research-quality review is optional and gates no enablement.", where "agent collaboration" is the link to `agent-collaboration.md` in both sentences, as in the current text; in "Baselines and comparison", "Compare a capable single agent with the specialist configuration on the same research questions, authorized evidence, tool capabilities and comparable total work budgets." becomes "An optional quality review runs the specialist configuration on declared research questions, authorized evidence, tool capabilities and total work budgets; it compares no single-agent baseline, because Ohtia does not grade the models researchers connect."; and under "Research-quality cases", before "Reference discovery", add the paragraph "These cases are optional review material for the owner and gate no enablement, because Ohtia does not grade the models researchers connect."
- `docs/agents/index.md`: the description of the "Agent collaboration evaluation" entry, "Evaluate research usefulness, evidence fidelity, human review, and bounded execution against a single-agent baseline.", becomes the playbook's new description, "Verify bounded execution and collaboration scenarios, and review research usefulness, evidence fidelity and human review as optional material that gates no enablement."
- `docs/agents/agent-josefa.md`, `docs/agents/agent-jorge-luis.md`, `docs/agents/agent-erasmus.md`, `docs/agents/agent-boris.md` and `docs/agents/agent-maria.md`, "Evaluation and enablement", the same two changes in each of the five profiles: "Declare rubrics, severity definitions, quality thresholds and bounded work/cost/latency before trials. Compare direct and coordinated use with a capable single-agent baseline where relevant under comparable evidence and total budgets. Human assessors need appropriate disciplinary and language competence." becomes "Research-quality review is optional and gates no enablement, because Ohtia does not grade the models researchers connect, and it compares no single-agent baseline. When the owner runs one, declare its rubrics, severity definitions and bounded work/cost/latency before the trials; human assessors need appropriate disciplinary and language competence."; and "Enable only the evaluated scope after research-quality and adapter-conformance requirements pass." becomes "Enable an operation once its adapter conformance and operational checks pass; Ohtia does not grade the quality of the models researchers connect."
- `docs/agents/agent-thomas.md`, "Evaluation and enablement": "Compare Thomas with bounded methodological guidance from a capable single-agent baseline under comparable evidence and total work budgets." becomes "Research-quality review is optional and gates no enablement, because Ohtia does not grade the models researchers connect, and it compares no single-agent baseline."; "Declare assessment rubrics, quality thresholds, severity definitions and work/cost/latency limits before trials. Human assessors need methodological, disciplinary and language competence appropriate to the cases." becomes "When the owner runs a quality review, declare its assessment rubrics, severity definitions and work/cost/latency limits before the trials; human assessors need methodological, disciplinary and language competence appropriate to the cases."; and "Enable only the operation/language/material/configuration scope supported by both quality evidence and adapter conformance." becomes "Enable an operation/language/material/configuration scope once its adapter conformance and operational checks pass; Ohtia does not grade the quality of the models researchers connect."
- `docs/agents/agent-coordination-references.md`, "Architectural inference": "That benefit remains a comparison against the single-agent baseline under declared evaluation conditions." becomes "Ohtia does not measure that benefit: it does not grade the models researchers connect, so it runs no comparison against a single-agent baseline."
- `docs/agents/agent-identity.md`, Thomas's paragraph: "Enabling this contributor requires its operation contract and quality evidence." becomes "Enabling this contributor requires its operation contract and the operational checks of its profile; Ohtia does not grade the quality of the models researchers connect."
- `docs/agents/agent-collaboration.md`, "Methodological guidance and analysis extension": "Do not relabel a general agent as a qualified specialist without its operation contract and quality evidence." becomes "Do not relabel a general agent as a specialist without its operation contract."
- `docs/product-definition.md`: "Model output quality must be evaluated for each enabled operation and language; translated labels do not establish model competence." becomes "Ohtia does not grade model output quality; translated labels do not establish model competence."; and in the boundaries that require further evidence, "Define manuscript import fidelity, bibliography-manager integration, operation/language quality thresholds, launch OS/CPU/version matrix and achievable adapter access methods before the corresponding delivery commitments." becomes "Define manuscript import fidelity, bibliography-manager integration, launch OS/CPU/version matrix and achievable adapter access methods before the corresponding delivery commitments."
- ADR-0029: "Evaluate selected operations/languages before enabling them; transport conformance does not establish research quality." becomes "Transport conformance does not establish research quality, and Ohtia does not grade it."
- `docs/product-roadmap.md`: in the EPIC-003 boundary, "Later role operations require their own inputs and evaluated quality." becomes "Later role operations require their own inputs."; the "Agent quality" shared acceptance rule becomes "Agent quality: Ohtia does not grade the models researchers connect; operations are enabled after operational conformance, and chargeable checks require bounded authorization."; in the readiness list, "quality thresholds before trials" is removed from the EPIC-003 entry; "role/language quality trials" is removed from the list of checks that deterministic scenarios do not replace; and in the review milestone of the entry "Develop the bibliography through examined source work", "Reading/critical operations need operation/language quality evidence and an honest insufficient-evidence outcome." becomes "Reading/critical operations need an honest insufficient-evidence outcome; Ohtia does not grade the quality of the models researchers connect."

**Decisions and rationale:** The type is data because it is stored at creation and each type may need different facts. The researcher decided that Ohtia does not grade model quality, so `project_setup`, the first operation enabled for researchers, carries the removal of the quality gates; ADR-0037's single-agent baseline comparison is already replaced by the ADR written in STORY-003-005. The adapter contract gains tools here because `project_setup` is the first operation that uses them. Names are generic (`project_setup`, `propose_project_setup`, `project_setup_proposals`, `document_language`) because `data-model.md` defines a research project as a thesis, paper or other investigation.[^data-model]

**Verification:** With the fake provider: a valid proposal waits and a second replaces it; an unknown language, an overlong title or two calls in one reply leave an invalid proposal with its reason, reported in the next task with that reason's note at the proposal's place; two calls store the first call's values with `multiple_calls`, and a call failing several checks stores the first reason in the stated order; each proposal state gives the next task its note, and a note whose place falls among omitted messages is not sent; a complete tool call in an attempt that is then stopped, ends by a limit or becomes uncertain stores no proposal; confirming a proposal that is no longer waiting fails with `proposal_not_waiting`, confirming during a setup reply with `setup_reply_active`, and rejecting a proposal that is no longer waiting with `proposal_not_waiting`; a proposal's timeline revision moves from 1 to 2 when it is decided; a reply with only the proposal and no text completes its task, saves no message and records the proposal with its `task_id`; a reply with no text and only a call outside the permitted tools or their schemas fails with `empty_reply`; the pending limit rejects the message and saves nothing; confirming a replaced proposal or during a running reply is rejected; confirming twice returns the same result; proposals survive a restart; a conversation over the input limits sends the newest whole messages with the omission note; the extended conformance suite passes with the fake provider and with ChatGPT, including tool calls whose arguments never complete, a complete call followed by more text, which is emitted only after the response completes, and a response of tool calls alone whose arguments pass `max_output_bytes`, a complete first call followed by text that passes it and a complete first call followed by a second call whose arguments pass it, each of which ends with `output_limit_exceeded`, no tool-call event and no proposal, with main never holding more than `max_output_bytes` of output for the attempt; a development project's messages still create `development_test` tasks. Automated tests run in the 12 CI cells. With the researcher's account on the x86-64 virtual machines and the development command: a Spanish conversation stating Argentina yields `es-AR`, an English one without a region yields `en`, and one is confirmed.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-020: New project flow

Let the researcher start a project and complete its setup with Virgilio in the window.[^workspace]

- **Where to start: "New project".** The primary button of the welcome, with the Lucide `plus` icon, followed by the secondary "Open Settings", "Keyboard shortcuts" and "About Ohtia", which wrap to a second row when the welcome is narrower than the four; the `Plus` button in the context bar, which appears now that projects can be created (until STORY-003-016 the project name shows as text); the application menu, as the first item of File, above "New window"; the command palette, as the first command of its General group; and Ctrl+N (Cmd+N on macOS), which was free while Ctrl+Shift+N stays "New window". All five run the new registry command `project.new` of `packages/contracts/src/commands.ts`. "New project" creates a thesis without asking while thesis is the only type. "New project" is a stated exception to the design system's rule that a button label is a verb with its object or complement, recorded in that rule with "Try again" wherever an action is repeated and the Preference saving labels "Check", "Choose again" and "Apply again" (amendment below), because researchers know this creation action by this name from other applications.
- **Text rule.** Texts about the action say "project"; texts about an existing project use its type's name.
  - Welcome: "Start a project and set it up with Virgilio. You can also adjust language, appearance, zoom and layout in Settings."
  - Project contents with no project: "No project is open."
  - Project contents with a project open, in setup or confirmed: "Sources and documents aren't available in this version."
  - Chat with no project: "Create a project to talk with Virgilio."
- **Without a connection,** that is, when `connection.read` reports no connection in the state `connected`, nothing is created and a dialog appears, titled "Connect an AI service to set up a project", with the body "Virgilio needs a connected AI service to help you set up the project.", "Open Connections" (primary) and "Close dialog", which has the initial focus. The dialog names no service, so it stays correct when STORY-003-036 and STORY-003-037 add API-key connections.
- **Creating.** Ohtia calls STORY-003-004's `project.create` with its operation identifier, so a double click creates one project. A failure creates nothing and changes neither the open project nor the panels; because creation can start from five places, it is reported in an alert dialog titled "Ohtia couldn't create the project", with the body "Nothing was created, and your other projects are safe.", "Try again" (primary), which repeats `project.create` with the same identifier, and "Close dialog", which has the initial focus. The new project becomes the open one even if another was open; the other's work continues (STORY-003-016 restores its unsent text when the researcher returns). A hidden chat is shown, with the caret in the composer.
- **Project in setup.** The context bar says "New thesis · Setting up". The Navigator says "Talk with Virgilio in the chat to set up your thesis." The empty conversation shows an Ohtia text, not a Virgilio message: "Tell Virgilio about your thesis: what it's about, a working title if you have one, and the language you'll write it in.", a centered paragraph in `type.ui.body-small` and `content.secondary` without a `VoiceLabel`, like EPIC-002's empty states. The setup conversation can be renamed like any other conversation (STORY-003-018).
- **Pending and input limits.** When `message.append` rejects a message with `pending_limit_reached`, because `project_setup`'s pending limit is reached (STORY-003-014), or with `input_too_large`, because the message alone exceeds `project_setup`'s 64 KiB input limit, as 20,000 characters of four bytes each would (STORY-003-005), nothing is saved and the composer keeps the message with STORY-003-012's attention notice with `triangle-alert`, announced as a status: "Too many replies are waiting. Your message wasn't sent and is still in the box; send it again when some finish." or "This message is too long for Virgilio to read. Your message wasn't sent and is still in the box; shorten it and send it again."
- **Proposal card.** Uses the new `ActionProposalCard` (amendment below), at the reply position of the task that proposed it: right below Virgilio's message, or in its place when his reply carried only the proposal and saved no message (STORY-003-014). The card reads the proposals through the timeline reads of STORY-003-010. A card right below Virgilio's own message does not repeat his `VoiceLabel`; a card in place of a reply shows it.
  - Title: "Proposed thesis setup".
  - State label, right below the title: while open, "Waiting for your answer" with `circle-dashed`, in `voice.ohtia.content`; each decided state replaces it with its own sentence and icon (Other card states).
  - "Type: Thesis".
  - "Title: {title}", where `{title}` is the proposed title, marked with the proposed language so screen readers read it correctly.
  - "Language: {language_name} · {tag}", where `{tag}` is the proposed tag and `{language_name}` its name in the interface language, such as "Language: Spanish (Argentina) · es-AR".
  - "Virgilio and the other agents will work in this language. In this version, the title and language can't be changed after you confirm."
  - "Confirm setup" (primary), which runs `project.confirmSetup`, disabled with "Wait for Virgilio to finish replying." while a reply runs or waits, a reason shown below the buttons in `type.ui.caption`; "Correct setup", which focuses the composer and changes its placeholder and accessible name to "Tell Virgilio what to change" until the researcher sends a message, the card leaves its open state, or the window shows another conversation or project, when it returns to "Message Virgilio"; "Reject setup", which runs `project.rejectSetup` and which Virgilio learns in his next reply.
  - When "Confirm setup" or "Reject setup" fails, the card stays open and a danger notice with `octagon-alert`, announced as an alert, appears below its buttons. With `proposal_not_waiting`, the window reads the proposal again: when a newer proposal replaced it, the notice is "A newer proposal replaced this one. Nothing was confirmed." or "A newer proposal replaced this one. Nothing was rejected.", and when it was confirmed or rejected meanwhile, for example in another window, the card shows that state and no notice. With `setup_reply_active`, the notice is "Virgilio started replying first. Nothing was confirmed; confirm the setup when he finishes." With any other error it is "Ohtia couldn't confirm the setup. Nothing was saved; you can confirm it again." or "Ohtia couldn't reject the setup. Nothing was saved; you can reject it again.", and the same button repeats the command with the same operation identifier.
  - An unconfirmed result, `uncertain`, `timeout`, `cancelled`, `disposed` or `invalid_service_result`, instead shows below the buttons an attention notice with `triangle-alert`, announced as a status: "Ohtia couldn't tell whether the setup was confirmed. Confirm it again to check; nothing will be saved twice." or "Ohtia couldn't tell whether the proposal was rejected. Reject it again to check; nothing will be saved twice.", and the same button repeats the command with the same operation identifier.
- **Other card states.** Each shows its sentence as the state label right below the title, and none has buttons. Replaced: "Replaced by a newer proposal." with `circle-x`. Rejected: "You rejected this proposal." with `circle-x`. Confirmed: "Confirmed. Ohtia saved the title and language; it doesn't evaluate them." with `circle-check`, because `ActionProposalCard` states that accepting a proposal does not certify its content.
- **Invalid proposal.** An attention notice with `triangle-alert`, announced as a status, instead of the card and, like the card, without a `VoiceLabel` when it is right below Virgilio's message, with the sentence for its code followed by "Virgilio will see this in his next reply.": `title_empty` "Virgilio proposed an empty title."; `title_whitespace` "Virgilio proposed a title with spaces at the start or end."; `title_too_long` "Virgilio proposed a title longer than {max} characters.", with plural forms, where `{max}` is 200; `language_malformed` "Virgilio proposed a language code Ohtia can't read: {tag}."; `language_unknown` "Virgilio proposed a language code that doesn't exist: {tag}."; `multiple_calls` "Virgilio made more than one proposal in one reply.", where `{tag}` is the code as received.
- **After confirmation.** "Thesis set up: {title}" is announced, with the confirmed title; the context bar shows the title; the Navigator says "The Navigator isn't available in this version. Talk with Virgilio in the chat."; until STORY-003-017 the composer is disabled with "Virgilio can't reply here yet."
- **Unfinished setup** leaves the project in setup; reopening it is STORY-003-016, and deleting it is excluded from this EPIC.

**Measurements,** all expressed with existing tokens unless stated:

- `ActionProposalCard`: `space.3` padding, `space.2` gap, `surface.page` and `radius.medium`; open 2 px dashed `voice.ohtia.border`, accepted 2 px solid `border.strong`, declined and replaced 1 px `border.subtle`; the title in `type.ui.subheading`; the state label a 16 px icon with text in `type.ui.label`; labelled sections `space.1` apart, their labels in `content.secondary` at weight 500 and their text in `type.ui.body-small`; quoted requests and notes with `space.3` inline-start padding and a 2 px `border.subtle` rule.

It shows the proposing contributor's `VoiceLabel`, except when the card is right below that contributor's own message, a title naming the action, a state label right below the title, labelled sections of recorded text that keep their language and, while open, one primary action and one or two secondary actions; a disabled action shows its reason below the actions. Its states are open (2 px dashed border, `circle-dashed`, `voice.ohtia.*`, with the state label “Waiting for your answer”), accepted (2 px solid border, `circle-check`, `border.strong`), declined (secondary text, `circle-x`, `content.secondary`) and replaced (secondary text, `circle-x`, `content.secondary`, stating that a newer proposal replaced it). Declining an action is not a rejection of research content, so it needs no reason; the researcher tells the agent what to change in the conversation. After a decision the card keeps its content and offers no accept or decline action; a card that also shows the progress of the work it started shows it as rows with state labels and offers that work's own actions. Accepting records what the researcher read; it does not certify the content. `ProposalCard` remains the component for proposed research content."

**Decisions and rationale:** A project is created by conversing with Virgilio, so Ohtia without a language-model service cannot create one. The type line shows which type "New project" chose without asking. Agent action proposals get their own component because `ProposalCard` reviews research content with four fixed actions, a reason for every rejection and a version and scope for every confirmation, none of which fits an action that the researcher accepts or declines.[^design]

**Verification:** Automated tests with the fake provider cover the five ways to start; with no connection in the state `connected`, including one that is `expired` or `connecting`, nothing is created and the dialog that names no service appears; a double click creates one project; a failure shows its dialog, changes nothing and "Try again" reuses the identifier; a hidden chat appears; the explorer text with and without a project; the setup conversation's centered Ohtia text without a label; every card state of `ActionProposalCard`, each with its state label below the title, "Waiting for your answer" while open, and no `VoiceLabel` on a card or invalid-proposal notice right below Virgilio's message; a card in place of a reply that carried only the proposal; "Confirm setup" disabled while Virgilio replies; confirming twice gives the same result; "Correct setup", with the placeholder returning to "Message Virgilio" after sending, and "Reject setup"; each failure notice of "Confirm setup" and "Reject setup" by error code, a proposal confirmed or rejected meanwhile in another window, which shows its state without a notice, and both unconfirmed-result notices; the pending-limit and `input_too_large` notices that keep the message; "New project" as the first item of File, running `project.new`; and each invalid-proposal notice. All of it at 280 CSS px, in German, Korean and pseudolocalization, with screen-reader announcements, in the 12 CI cells. With the researcher's account on the x86-64 virtual machines: a complete setup in Spanish for Argentina and one in English, both confirmed, and a rejected proposal followed by a new one.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-021: Open and switch projects

Let the researcher see all projects, switch between them and reopen the last one.[^workspace][^design]

- **Selector.** In the context bar next to `Plus`, which follows it, where `workspace-layout.md` places it, following the APG select-only combobox pattern, the APG combobox that `design-system.md` allows for a selector: a combobox button that opens a listbox of the projects, 40 at a time. The button's accessible name is "Current project" and its value is the open project's title, "New thesis · Setting up" for a project in setup, or "No project open" in a window without an open project. While no project exists, the listbox shows the status text "No projects yet." instead of rows. Each row shows the type icon and the title on the first line, or "New thesis" for a project in setup, whose title is empty, and, on a smaller second line, the type name, "Thesis", or, for a project in setup, "Thesis · Setting up · {created}", where `{created}` is its creation date and time with a medium date and a short time in the interface locale, so two projects in setup can be told apart. Example: `graduation-cap`, "Redes de cuidado en Rosario", and below it "Thesis". The open project's row carries the `check` icon and `aria-current="true"`; `aria-selected` follows the option the keyboard highlights, as the pattern defines. It works with arrows, Enter and Esc. A long title is truncated visually and keeps its full accessible name and tooltip. The rows come from `project.list`, which the listbox reads afresh each time it opens, because opening a project in another window changes the order without changing any record that `research.subscribe` names; while the first page loads, the listbox shows "Loading…" and is marked `aria-busy`, and if it fails, the listbox shows a danger notice with `octagon-alert`, announced as an alert, "Ohtia couldn't load the projects. They're still saved.", with "Reload projects" below it, which reads the first page again.
- **Pages.** STORY-003-004's read `project.list` gains a cursor: it takes the cursor `(last_opened_at, id)` of the last row received, empty for the first page, and a count of at most 40, and returns the next projects in the order below, packed as STORY-003-010's pages are, with where to continue and whether more projects exist. The listbox shows the first 40 projects. While more exist, its last row is the button "Show more projects": an option, because a select-only combobox moves only among options, that does not choose a project; choosing it with Enter or a click keeps the listbox open, appends the next 40 projects after the last row, moves the highlight to the first of them, and is disabled while they load, with the status "Loading more projects…" announced politely. Once no more projects exist, the button disappears. Scrolling the listbox loads nothing (SPEC-003-002). If a later page fails, the rows already shown stay, and above the button a danger notice with `octagon-alert`, announced as an alert, reads "Ohtia couldn't load more projects. They're still saved."; "Show more projects" then reads that page again.
- **Icon.** The thesis icon is Lucide `graduation-cap`, vendored from the `lucide-static` 1.66.0 package pinned in `packages/renderer/icons/manifest.json`, with its hash in the manifest and the generated `design-icons.ts` and icon mappings updated; each later type brings its own icon. The icon always comes with text, because the design system forbids an icon as the sole carrier of meaning.
- **Order.** Most recently opened first, then by `id` from highest to lowest, through the new required column `projects.last_opened_at` (UTC with milliseconds): the migration sets it to `created_at` for every existing project, `project.create` sets it to the creation time, and the new command `project.open` writes it each time a window opens the project; it does not change `updated_at`.
- **Port.** This story carries its new command `project.open`, with its operation identifier and the project, through the application port, adds `project.open` to the commands that `command_receipts.command` accepts, and lists in its `operationErrorCodes` entry `record_not_found`, next action `refresh_record`, for an identifier that names no project (SPEC-003-002 and SPEC-003-015). When it fails, the window keeps the project it opened, and the order is written the next time it opens.
- **Switching.** The conversation the researcher had open in that project returns with its unsent text and reading position; if none was opened in this session, the one with the latest message opens. Work in other projects continues.
- **Starting Ohtia.** The first window opens the last opened project, or the welcome when none exists.
- **New window.** Opens without a project, on the welcome; the researcher picks any project in the selector, even one open in another window.
- **No open project.** In a window without an open project while projects exist, the welcome keeps its buttons and its paragraph reads "Choose a project in the project selector, or start a new one and set it up with Virgilio. You can also adjust language, appearance, zoom and layout in Settings."; the chat says "Choose a project in the project selector to talk with Virgilio.", without a composer; Project contents says "No project is open."; and the selector shows "No project open". While no project exists, the texts of STORY-003-015 stay.
- **Narrow layout.** Below 1,024 px the selector stays in the bar's first row, as `workspace-layout.md` defines.

**Measurements,** all expressed with existing tokens unless stated:

- Project selector button: 32 px high, at most 320 px wide, `space.2` padding and gap, 1 px `border.control`, `radius.small`, `surface.page`, `type.ui.label` and 16 px icons. Its listbox is `component.workspace.chat.initial` (360 px) wide, with `space.1` padding, 2 px between rows, `surface.panel`, `border.subtle`, `radius.medium` and `shadow.overlay`; rows have `space.2` padding, a 16 px icon, the title in `type.ui.label` and the second line in `type.ui.caption`; the highlighted row looks like the command palette's active option.

**Documentation amendments:**

- `docs/design-system.md` fixed icon table: add "Thesis | `graduation-cap`" and "Open project in the project selector | `check`".
- `docs/frontend-architecture.md`: "The installed app opens to create/open/recent-project actions." becomes "The installed app opens the last opened project, or the welcome with its New project action when none exists."
- `docs/epics/EPIC-002-basic-interface/epic.md`, SPEC-002-023, as left by STORY-003-015: in "First run without a project", after "The chat region shows Create a project to talk with Virgilio. and renders no composer." add "When projects exist but the window has none open, the paragraph reads Choose a project in the project selector, or start a new one and set it up with Virgilio. You can also adjust language, appearance, zoom and layout in Settings. and the chat region shows Choose a project in the project selector to talk with Virgilio."; in "Project context", after "they appear only with the capability that makes them work, in the positions workspace-layout.md defines." add "Once the project selector exists, a window without an open project shows it with the value No project open, and while no project exists its listbox shows No projects yet."
- `docs/workspace-layout.md`, first-run welcome, as left by STORY-003-015: after "The explorer says that no project is open, and chat invites the researcher to create a project to talk with Virgilio, without a composer." add "When projects exist but the window has none open, the paragraph and the chat sentence point to the project selector instead."

**Decisions and rationale:** Icon plus text keeps the design-system rule; an icon alone would have needed an exception. The selector is a combobox and not a menu, because `design-system.md` allows a select or an APG combobox for a selector and keeps menus for actions. The researcher decided that the list shows the 40 most recently opened projects and loads more only through "Show more projects", because `design-system.md` requires an explicit action to load more of a long list and each page then fits the port's bounds.[^design]

**Verification:** Automated tests: switching A to B and back with delayed reads keeps unsent text and reading position; switching during a reply does not interrupt it; starting Ohtia restores the last project; selector order follows `last_opened_at` and then `id`, and the migration gives existing projects their `created_at`; a new window with projects shows "No project open" in the selector and the welcome and chat texts that point to it, and with no project the listbox shows "No projects yet."; the selector stays in the bar's first row below 1,024 px; two projects in setup are distinguishable and read "New thesis"; the open project carries the `check` icon and `aria-current`; a slow and a failed `project.list` show "Loading…" and the failure notice with "Reload projects" below it; with 95 projects the listbox shows 40, "Show more projects" appends 40 and then 15 and disappears, scrolling loads nothing, a failed later page keeps the rows with its notice, and pages that end between projects with the same `last_opened_at` lose and repeat none; reopening the listbox after another window opened a project shows the new order; `project.open` with an unknown project identifier returns `record_not_found` and a repeat with the same identifier returns its first result; long titles, keyboard and screen readers work with the select-only combobox pattern; 280 CSS px chat width, German, Korean and pseudolocalization. They run in the 12 CI cells. With the researcher's account on the x86-64 virtual machines: create two projects, switch while Virgilio replies, and close and reopen Ohtia to find the last project. The V08 scenario of `workspace-layout.md` passes for project switching: A to B to A during delayed reads and replies keeps buffers, reading positions and running work.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-022: Virgilio: clarify and next step

Make Virgilio's direct guidance the normal way to work in a confirmed project.[^virgilio][^siwc-limitations]

- **One operation, `direct_guidance`.** `agent-virgilio.md` describes clarifying a research question and proposing a next research action under two headings, each evaluated and enabled separately; this story runs both in one operation, `direct_guidance`, and its amendment records that. In a free conversation Virgilio chooses which to do from the message; the researcher does not pick a button.
- **When.** In a confirmed project, every message to Virgilio creates a `direct_guidance` task, so the composer that STORY-003-015 disabled after confirmation now works. New conversations arrive in STORY-003-018; until then the setup conversation continues.
- **Language.** Virgilio answers in the document language even when the researcher writes in another; quotations stay in their original language, and a translation is labeled as Virgilio's own.
- **Instructions** (English, instruction version 1):

  ```text
  You are Virgilio, the research guide in Ohtia, a desktop application where researchers write research documents with the help of AI agents. You guide the researcher; you are not their human supervisor, and you do not replace them.

  Project type: {project_type_name}. {project_type_description}
  Working title: {title}
  Document language: {document_language_name} ({document_language})

  Write every reply in the document language. Keep quotations in their original language; if you translate one, say that the translation is yours.

  You help in two ways, depending on what the researcher asks:

  Clarify the research. Help the researcher state their topic, problem, question and objectives, and tell these apart. Suggest possible delimitations, make your assumptions explicit, and end with the next useful question. Keep choices the researcher has already made unless you give a reason to revisit them. Do not require a complete profile before helping.

  Propose a next action. Suggest one feasible next action, with its reason, its prerequisites and what will show that it is done. If no action is justified yet, explain what blocks it or why stopping here is reasonable.

  Rules:
  - Your suggestions are proposals. The researcher decides; never present a suggestion as a decision already taken.
  - You have no access to sources, files or the internet in this conversation. Do not claim to have read, searched or verified anything that is not in this conversation, and never claim that a research gap or novelty exists.
  - Do not invent requirements, sources, data or deadlines. If something is missing, say that it is missing.
  - Do not impose a fixed sequence of stages.
  - If a request is ambiguous, ask one short clarifying question instead of guessing.
  - You cannot approve, save or change the researcher's document, and chat replies are not approvals.
  ```

- **Input.** The instructions with type, title and language: `{project_type_name}` and `{project_type_description}` are the type's English name and description (STORY-003-014), `{title}` the confirmed title, `{document_language}` the project's tag, and `{document_language_name}` its English name from `new Intl.DisplayNames(["en"], { type: "language", languageDisplay: "standard" }).of(tag)`, such as "Spanish (Argentina)" for `es-AR`; the conversation's messages, newest first, up to the input limit, whole messages only, with the note "Earlier messages in this conversation are not included." when older ones are left out; and STORY-003-007's notes for cut messages. The attempt records exactly which messages were sent, by identifier. The limit exists because Sign in with ChatGPT rejects `truncation`; project memory (STORY-003-032) gives continuity beyond it.
- **Policy `direct_guidance`, version 1.** `max_pending_tasks` 100; `max_input_bytes` 256 KiB (about 40,000 words, counting message text only) and `max_input_items` 500; `max_output_bytes` 64 KiB, about 10,000 words; `max_work_units` 1; `max_attempt_duration_ms` 10 minutes; `max_inactivity_ms` 120 seconds; `stop_grace_ms` 5 seconds; `max_attempts` 2, `max_auto_retries` 0, `retry_backoff_ms` 0. When `message.append` is rejected with `pending_limit_reached`, the message is not saved and stays in the composer with STORY-003-012's attention notice "Too many replies are waiting. Your message wasn't sent and is still in the box; send it again when some finish." A researcher message of at most 20,000 code points takes at most 80,000 bytes, so `input_too_large` cannot occur under this limit.
- **Model.** The first model of the connection's list, under STORY-003-005's model rule, until STORY-003-035.
- **No tools** in this version; later stories add Virgilio's proposal tools.
- **Quality.** Ohtia does not grade the models the researcher connects; tests check that the operation works, not that replies are good.

**Documentation amendments:**

- `docs/agents/agent-virgilio.md`, "Operations": "Each operation has a versioned policy, instruction and output contract under ADR-0029. Evaluate and enable operations separately; the headings identify research operations, not provider API methods." becomes "Each operation has a versioned policy, instruction and output contract under ADR-0029, and is enabled once its adapter conformance and operational checks pass. The headings identify research capabilities, not provider API methods. Clarifying a research question and proposing a next research action run in one operation, `direct_guidance`, the standard way Virgilio answers in a confirmed project; Virgilio chooses from the researcher's message which of them to do.", keeping the ADR-0029 link; and at the start of "Clarify a research question" and of "Propose a next research action", before their "Input" line, add "**Operation:** `direct_guidance`."

**Decisions and rationale:** The researcher confirmed that Virgilio answers in the document language whatever language they write in. The quality gates are removed in STORY-003-014, because Ohtia cannot grade arbitrary models connected by researchers.

**Verification:** With the fake provider: a project in setup uses `project_setup` and a confirmed one `direct_guidance`; instructions carry type, title and language, with `es-AR` written as "Spanish (Argentina) (es-AR)"; a conversation over the input limit sends only recent messages with the note; the attempt records the messages sent; the composer works after confirmation; the pending limit keeps the message in the composer. Automated tests run in the 12 CI cells. With the researcher's account on the x86-64 virtual machines, in a confirmed Spanish thesis: ask Virgilio to clarify the question, ask for a next action, and write in English to see the reply in Spanish.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-023: Conversation history

Let the researcher list, start, rename and switch conversations within the open project.[^workspace]

- **Where.** The `List` icon button in the chat header, named "Show conversation history" with the same tooltip, opens the history, which replaces the chat panel's content under the heading "Conversations"; it is neither a column nor a dialog. `ArrowLeft`, "Return to conversation", goes back to the open conversation. Opening the history changes no document, unsent text or running work. A project in setup has one conversation and shows neither `List` nor "New conversation"; its conversation can still be renamed, so it shows the `pencil` button.
- **Headers.** The chat header shows, in order, the conversation's name, or its creation date and time when unnamed, in `content.primary`, then "Rename conversation" (`pencil`), "Show conversation history" (`List`) and "New conversation" (`MessageSquarePlus`). A new conversation that does not exist yet, before its first message, shows the title "New conversation" and no `pencil`, because nothing exists to rename yet. The history header shows, in order, "Return to conversation" (`ArrowLeft`), the heading "Conversations" and "New conversation". While a name is being edited, the field takes the place of the name and the `pencil` button, the other buttons stay, and the field's notices show in a band below the header.
- **Rows.** The open project's conversations, newest message first: ordered by `latest_at`, the `created_at` of the conversation's latest message, or of the conversation while it has no message, where the latest message is always the one at the highest position, so a reply saved after a later researcher message does not become it, from newest to oldest, then by `id` from highest to lowest. Each row shows, in this order: on its first line, the compact `VoiceLabel` of the agent that answers the conversation (amendment below), its symbol and name, Virgilio's until STORY-003-021 lets other agents answer, and the time of the latest message at the row's inline end; the name, or, when unnamed, its creation date and time as in the chat header (STORY-003-010); one plain-text line with the start of the latest message; and a status when relevant: "Replying…" with `pen-line` (STORY-003-010) with a reply running, "Waiting" with `circle-dashed` with a reply waiting, queued or blocked for any reason, or "Needs your attention", with `triangle-alert` in `attention.content`, when its latest task failed or is uncertain. The open conversation's row carries the inline-start `component.list-row.selection-indicator` and `aria-current="true"`.
- **Times.** The latest message's time uses the interface locale: a short time of day for a message from today, such as "2:03 PM" in `en-US`; the day and abbreviated month for an earlier day of this year, such as "Nov 3"; and the day, abbreviated month and year for an earlier year, such as "Nov 3, 2025". Its `time` element carries the exact value, and its tooltip shows the full date with the time. When more than one applies, "Needs your attention" comes before "Replying…", and "Replying…" before "Waiting". A blocked task does not need attention: its notice in the conversation already says what it waits for.
- **Preview line.** The first 120 code points of the latest message's plain text. The data service never parses Markdown (SPEC-003-002): `conversation.list` and `conversation.read` return, as `latest_prefix`, the first 1,024 code points of the latest message exactly as stored, ending at a whole code point, and `latest_truncated`, whether the message is longer. The renderer parses that prefix with STORY-003-011's Markdown parser, keeps only the text of its nodes, joins every run of whitespace, line breaks included, into one space, trims both ends and keeps the first 120 code points; when the text is longer, or the message was longer than the prefix, the row ends it with "…". The row shows it on one line and cuts what does not fit its width with an ellipsis; the full preview stays in the row's accessible name. The line takes the message's language as STORY-003-010 defines it.
- **Many conversations.** The list loads 50 at a time, newest first. While older conversations exist, the end of the list shows the secondary button "Load older conversations", which loads the next 50; while they load it is disabled and the status "Loading older conversations…" is announced politely. Scrolling to the end loads nothing (SPEC-003-002), and the button disappears once the last conversation is loaded. While the first page loads, the history shows "Loading conversations…" in place of the list, marked `aria-busy` and announced politely. A failed first page shows, in place of the list, a danger notice with `octagon-alert`, announced as an alert: "Ohtia couldn't load the conversations. They're still saved.", with "Reload conversations"; a failed later page shows at the end of the list a danger notice with `octagon-alert`, announced as an alert: "Ohtia couldn't load older conversations. They're still saved.", with "Load older conversations" below it, which reads that page again. With the data service unavailable, the history shows STORY-003-010's notice "Conversations are unavailable until local data and the local service are ready." STORY-003-004's read `conversation.list` gains a cursor: it takes the project, the cursor `(latest_at, id)` of the last row received, empty for the first page, and the count, 50, and returns the next rows in the order above, each with its name, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `revision`, `latest_at`, `latest_prefix` and `latest_truncated` with the latest message's language (STORY-003-010), and status, packed as STORY-003-010's pages are; from this story on, STORY-003-010's `conversation.read` returns the same fields. The window keeps one row per conversation identifier. When `research.subscribe` names a conversation of the open project with a revision the window does not have, the window reads it with `conversation.read`, up to 128 conversations per read, and places it by the order above: a conversation that sorts above the last row received, such as one that got a new message or one created in another window, appears or moves to its place, and one that sorts below it leaves the list until a later page brings it; a later page never repeats a row the window shows. After a `sequence_gap`, the window reads its rows again with `conversation.read` and the first page with `conversation.list` (STORY-003-010).
- **New conversation.** The `MessageSquarePlus` button, "New conversation", shows an empty conversation with the caret in the composer. "New conversation" is a stated exception to the design system's button-label rule, for the same reason as "New project" (amendment below). The conversation is created only when the first message is sent, so the history never holds empty conversations: Ohtia calls STORY-003-004's `conversation.start`, which creates the conversation and its first message in one transaction with one operation identifier. If it does not succeed, nothing is created and the text stays in the composer with STORY-003-012's notice for that result: "Too many replies are waiting. Your message wasn't sent and is still in the box; send it again when some finish." for `pending_limit_reached`, "Ohtia couldn't confirm that your message was saved. Send it again to check; it won't be saved twice." for an unconfirmed result, whose resend returns the created conversation when it was saved, and "Ohtia couldn't save your message. Your text is still in the box; send it again to retry." for any other error; Send retries with the same identifier. The empty conversation's destination line comes from STORY-003-012's `destination.read` with the project and the agent that will answer. An empty conversation says "Ask Virgilio about your thesis." The action is the new registry command `conversation.new` of `packages/contracts/src/commands.ts`, the second item of File, below "New project", and in the command palette's General group, with the shortcut Ctrl+Shift+O (Cmd+Shift+O on macOS). With no project open the command is unavailable with the new `CommandReason` `no_project`, "Open a project first.", and in a project in setup with the new reason `project_in_setup`, "Finish setting up the thesis first."; `packages/contracts/src/commands.ts` lists both, and the palette and menu show them as they show the existing reasons.
- **Rename.** A `pencil` button next to the conversation name turns it into a text field. The icon is Lucide `pencil`, vendored from the `lucide-static` 1.66.0 package pinned in `packages/renderer/icons/manifest.json`, with its hash in the manifest and the generated `design-icons.ts` and icon mappings updated through the existing `scripts/design_assets.py` procedure, whose `--write-icon-module` regenerates `design-icons.ts`; the button's accessible name and tooltip are "Rename conversation", as the design system requires of an icon button, and the text field, which takes the name's place and so has no visible label, is named "Conversation name", the second exception to the design system's visible-label rule after the composer (amendment below). Enter saves and Esc cancels; Enter does not save while an input method composes. The name has 1 to 200 Unicode code points without leading or trailing whitespace. A name that breaks these rules is not saved, and an attention notice with `triangle-alert` below the field, read with it, says how to correct it: "Enter a name, or press Esc to keep the current one." for an empty field; "Remove the spaces at the start or end of the name." for leading or trailing whitespace; "Remove {excess} characters to save this name.", with plural forms, where `{excess}` is the number of code points over 200. A saved rename is announced politely as "Conversation renamed." Each rename carries the conversation's `updated_at` as its expected version (STORY-003-004). When another window renamed the conversation first, the rename is rejected with `stale_revision` and the window reads the conversation again with `conversation.read`; the field keeps the typed name, and an attention notice with `git-compare` says "This conversation was renamed in another window. Press Enter to use your name instead."; Enter then saves against the new `updated_at`, and Esc shows the other window's name. An unconfirmed result keeps the text in the field with an attention notice with `triangle-alert`, announced as a status: "Ohtia couldn't confirm that the name was saved. Press Enter to check; it won't be saved twice.", and Enter repeats the rename with the same operation identifier while the name is unchanged, and with a new one once it changes. Any other failure, including `name_invalid`, which the field's own checks prevent, keeps the text in the field with a danger notice with `octagon-alert`, announced as an alert: "Ohtia couldn't rename this conversation. Your name is still in the field; press Enter to try again."
- **Switching.** A running reply in another conversation continues; returning restores unsent text and reading position.

Archiving and deleting conversations stay in TD-003.

**Measurements,** all expressed with existing tokens unless stated:

- Chat header: `component.panel.header-minimum` high, `space.4` start and `space.2` end padding, `space.1` gap and a bottom `border.subtle`; 32 px icon buttons with a 16 px `pencil` and 20 px `List` and `MessageSquarePlus`.
- History rows: `space.2` block and `space.4` inline padding, `space.3` inline at a 280 px chat, 2 px between lines and `border.subtle` separators; the name in `type.ui.label`, the preview in `type.ui.body-small` and `content.secondary`, the time and the status in `type.ui.caption`, and "Needs your attention" in `attention.content` at weight 500.
- Rename field: 32 px high, `space.2` padding, a 1 px `border.control`, `radius.small`, `surface.page` and `type.ui.label`; its notice band has `space.2` block and `space.4` inline padding.

**Documentation amendments:**

- `docs/workspace-layout.md`: the `MessageSquarePlus` action is "New conversation" instead of "Create conversation in {projectName}"; "`List`: Conversation history." becomes "`List`: Show conversation history."; in V05, "distinguish Create project from Create conversation" becomes "distinguish New project from New conversation".
- `docs/design-system.md`, "Base components", Button, as left by STORY-003-015: "The exceptions are “New project”, the name researchers know from other applications for creating a project, “Try again” wherever an action is repeated, and the Preference saving labels “Check”, “Choose again” and “Apply again”." becomes "The exceptions are “New project” and “New conversation”, the names researchers know from other applications for these creation actions, “Try again” wherever an action is repeated, and the Preference saving labels “Check”, “Choose again” and “Apply again”."
- `docs/design-system.md`, "Base components", Text field, as left by STORY-003-012: after the composer exception, add "The inline field that replaces a conversation's name while renaming it is the second exception; its accessible name is “Conversation name”."
- `docs/design-system.md`, "Voice", `VoiceLabel`: after "Researcher text retains the ordinary unlabelled treatment." add "Its compact variant, for list rows and one-line attributions where the full label does not fit, shows only the contributor's static symbol at 20 px and name, in `type.ui.caption` at weight 500 and `voice.ohtia.content`, with a `space.1` gap; the full label stays the attribution of messages, notices and cards."
- `docs/epics/EPIC-002-basic-interface/epic.md`, SPEC-002-024, as left by STORY-003-015: "File: New project; New window; Settings… (Windows and Linux); Close window; Quit (Windows and Linux)." becomes "File: New project; New conversation; New window; Settings… (Windows and Linux); Close window; Quit (Windows and Linux)."; and in the shortcut inventory, after the "New project" row, add the row "New conversation", "Ctrl+Shift+O", "Cmd+Shift+O".

**Decisions and rationale:** Lazy creation keeps empty conversations out of the history, as ChatGPT and Claude do, and one operation for the conversation and its first message means that a failure leaves nothing behind. A rename checks the expected version and a conflict keeps the typed name, as `architecture.md` and `workspace-layout.md` require. Only a failed or uncertain reply needs the researcher's attention; a blocked reply waits for something its notice names, so it shows "Waiting".[^architecture][^workspace] The project name is not part of the button text because titles can be long. Ctrl+Shift+O follows ChatGPT's web shortcut for a new chat, recalled from use because OpenAI's help page refused automated access; Ctrl+N stays "New project", as word processors use it for a new document.

**Verification:** Automated tests with the fake provider cover the V13 cases and the V14 cases of `workspace-layout.md` except archiving, deleting and a generated title arriving after a manual edit, which this EPIC does not have: opening and closing the history without touching documents, unsent text or work; delayed reads from A to B and back without mixing conversations; a late-created conversation appearing in its place; paging by 50 through "Load older conversations", with errors and an unavailable service, while scrolling to the end loads nothing and the button disappears after the last page; hiding and showing the panel from both views; renaming with an input method, an empty name, an overlong name, a name with spaces at its ends, a failure and a conflict with another window, which keeps the typed name, each with its notice; the three time formats; the failed first and later pages with their notices; Ctrl+Shift+O with no project and in a project in setup, showing its reason; a failed start that creates nothing and keeps the text for a retry with the same identifier, a start rejected with `pending_limit_reached` that keeps the text with its notice, and an unconfirmed start whose resend creates one conversation; the destination line of a new conversation and its header title "New conversation" without a `pencil`; the header buttons in their order, and the `pencil` without `List` or "New conversation" in a project in setup; "Loading conversations…" while the first page loads; the row order of compact `VoiceLabel` and time, name, preview and status, the open row's selection indicator and `aria-current`, and the preview cut on one line; every row status with its icon and their precedence; the preview's 120 code points without Markdown markers, derived in the renderer from `latest_prefix`, which the data service returns unparsed, with "…" when `latest_truncated` is set; a reply saved after a later researcher message, which changes neither the row's time nor its preview; paging while conversations reorder, with no row lost or repeated, a conversation that gets a new message or is created in another window moving to its place through `conversation.read`, and the rows read again after a sequence gap; a rename conflict rejected with `stale_revision` and an unconfirmed rename, each with its notice; and "New conversation" as the second item of File, running `conversation.new`. All at 280 CSS px, in German, Korean and pseudolocalization, in the 12 CI cells. With the researcher's account on the x86-64 virtual machines: create two conversations in a confirmed thesis, switch while Virgilio replies, and rename one.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-024: Concurrency limits

Let the researcher choose how many AI replies run at the same time.[^adr-0011]

- **Settings section "Simultaneous work".** One field, "AI replies at the same time", bound to `max_concurrent_inference`; its default is 10, set by STORY-003-005's migration.
- **No local-processing field.** ADR-0011's `max_concurrent_local_processing` is unused in this EPIC; the Settings rule in `docs/settings.md`, "Add a section only with its real feature controls and operations", applies, so its stored value 1 stays unchanged.
- **Values.** Whole numbers from 1 to 50, the cap Paperclip and Multica use. A value outside the range is not saved, and an attention notice with `triangle-alert` below the field, read with it, states the range: "Enter a whole number from {min} to {max}. The saved value, {value}, is kept.", where `{min}` is 1, `{max}` is 50 and `{value}` is the saved value, each formatted as a number.[^paperclip][^multica]
- **When it applies.** On leaving the field or pressing Enter, not on each keystroke, for every window and project. The section shows the design system's preference-saving states with the existing Settings texts, `{setting}` being "AI replies at the same time".
- **Help text below the field:** always, one line for each connected service, "More replies at once can use more of your {service} plan at the same time." for a `subscription` connection or "More replies at once can spend your {service} key's credit faster." for an `api_key` connection; for each connected service whose declared limit is below the field's value, "{service} allows at most {connection_limit} replies at a time.", with plural forms, where `{connection_limit}` is that connection's declared limit, which Settings cannot exceed (ChatGPT declares 3; the API-key connections declare none); when lowered below what is running, "{running} replies are running. New ones will wait until fewer than {value} are running.", with plural forms on each count, where `{running}` is the number of inference replies running and `{value}` the saved value, such as "4 replies are running. New ones will wait until fewer than 2 are running."; the `=1` branch of `{value}` reads "{running} replies are running. New ones will wait until none are running."
- **ADR-0011 rules.** Lowering the limit cuts nothing that runs. Raising it lets waiting tasks start but does not resume paused tasks or touch uncertain ones. Saving the value and admitting a task are serialized, so no task starts under a superseded limit.
- **Operations.** The new port operations `limits.read`, `limits.replace` and `limits.receipt` follow the existing preference operations of `docs/application-port.md`. `limits.read` returns the `execution_limits` record's revision, its `max_concurrent_inference` and the number of inference replies running now. `limits.replace` carries only the new value, with its operation identifier, and the last write wins: the data service saves the value whatever revision the window last saw, advances the record's revision by one and returns the saved record with the running count, which the help text uses. `limits.receipt` returns `not_found`, `pending` or `recorded` for an operation identifier, as `appearance.receipt` does. A repeated `limits.replace` and a `limits.receipt` that finds it `recorded` return the recorded value and revision with the current running count, because the receipt stores no running count. Each accepted change records a row in the new table `execution_limits_receipts` (`operation_id`, `value` and `result_revision`) in the same transaction as the record, like `appearance_receipts` of migration `0002`; a value outside 1 to 50 is rejected and leaves no row. Every window learns of a change through `research.subscribe` (STORY-003-010), which a window with no open project, such as one that shows only Settings, also holds for the records shared by every project, and reads the record again, ignoring a revision it already has; a window that shows the field also reads it again after a `sequence_gap`. No operation changes `max_concurrent_local_processing`.

**Documentation amendments:**

- `docs/frontend-architecture.md`, "Connections, tasks and consumption", as left by STORY-003-005: "Application Settings provides separate concurrency limits for inference, defaulting to 10, and for local processing, defaulting to 1." becomes "Application Settings provides the inference concurrency control, from 1 to 50 with a default of 10; local processing has no control until a feature uses it."
- `docs/product-roadmap.md`, Settings inventory: the row "Inference and local-processing concurrency" becomes "Inference concurrency".
- `docs/application-port.md`, "Envelopes and named operations": after the `bounds.read`, `bounds.replace` and `bounds.receipt` bullet, add "`limits.read`, `limits.replace` and `limits.receipt`: the execution-limit preference operations. `limits.read` has empty input and returns the record's revision, its `max_concurrent_inference` and the number of inference replies running. `limits.replace` input contains exactly `value`, a whole number from 1 to 50; the last accepted write wins, advances the revision by one and returns the saved record with the running count. `limits.receipt` input contains exactly `targetOperationId` and returns `not_found`, `pending` or `recorded`; a repeated `limits.replace` and a recorded receipt return the recorded value and revision with the current running count. Each accepted replacement records its receipt in the same transaction, and every window learns of it through `research.subscribe`."
- `docs/settings.md`, "Ownership": after "Settings groups Language, Appearance and Workspace layout in one Accessibility and appearance section." add "Simultaneous work holds the AI replies at the same time control."

**Decisions and rationale:** The field exists because the researcher may want fewer parallel replies to spare the plan or the key's credit; a range of 1 to 50 matches the reference systems. The last write wins, without a stale-revision rejection, because the value is one number the researcher sets on purpose and every window shows the saved value as soon as it changes. The ADR written in STORY-003-005 already records the range and the single Settings control in place of ADR-0011's two controls, so this story changes no ADR.

**Verification:** Automated tests: 0, 51, decimals and text are not saved and leave no receipt; lowering the limit with running tasks keeps them running; raising it starts waiting tasks but not paused ones; saving while a task is admitted uses the new limit; two windows both see the change, including a window with no open project that shows Settings, and two saves from two windows keep the later one; a repeated `limits.replace` returns its recorded value and revision with the current running count, and `limits.receipt` reports the same; the help text names each connected service in its kind's variant; keyboard, screen reader and German. Scenario 10 of `agent-collaboration-evaluation.md`, as STORY-003-007 amends it, passes: changing the limit during competing admissions respects both the application limit and the connection's limit, and an uncertain attempt holds no slot once its connection closes, because no adapter of this EPIC has background execution or a cancellation endpoint (STORY-003-007). They run in the 12 CI cells. With the researcher's account on the x86-64 virtual machines, with the limit at 1 and two conversations, the second waits for the first.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-025: Josefa: search plan

Let Josefa propose a search strategy as a written plan in the conversation.[^josefa][^workflow]

- **Operation `search_strategy_planning`.** It matches "Plan a search strategy" in Josefa's profile and the "Search strategy" artifact of `thesis-workflow.md`, which covers every kind of search location. It answers when Josefa answers in a conversation of a confirmed project; replies are Josefa's messages. Until STORY-003-021, the development command's `send-to-josefa` action reaches Josefa: it appends a researcher message to a given conversation of a confirmed ordinary project and creates a `search_strategy_planning` task instead of a `direct_guidance` one, through the development-only command `development.sendToJosefa` (STORY-003-003). STORY-003-022 defines what Josefa receives when consulted.
- **Input.** As in STORY-003-017: the instructions with the project data, the most recent messages up to the input limit with the omission note, and the cut-message notes. It also carries a fixed section, tested here: "Search locations Ohtia can query: none in this version."
- **Instructions** (English, instruction version 1):

  ```text
  You are Josefa, the reference librarian in Ohtia, a desktop application where researchers write research documents with the help of AI agents. You help the researcher plan how to search for literature.

  Project type: {project_type_name}. {project_type_description}
  Working title: {title}
  Document language: {document_language_name} ({document_language})
  Search locations Ohtia can query: {queryable_locations}

  Write every reply in the document language. Keep quotations and search terms in their original language; if you translate a quotation, say that the translation is yours.

  In this version you cannot run searches. Your task is to propose a search strategy that the researcher can review.

  If the search purpose is unclear, ask one short question before proposing a plan.

  When the purpose is clear, propose a plan with these eight parts, in this order:
  1. Purpose: the question and what the search should contribute.
  2. Vocabulary: concepts, keywords, synonyms, translations, possible descriptors and operator combinations.
  3. Where to search: databases, catalogs, repositories and citation routes that fit the topic, with the reason for each. Separate open locations from those that usually require an institutional subscription.
  4. Scope: periods, languages, contexts, populations and kinds of material. Explain any filter that could exclude relevant work.
  5. Selection: inclusion and exclusion criteria, how to treat duplicates and versions, and reading priorities.
  6. Planned work: initial queries, and the order of exploratory and deep reading.
  7. When it is enough: the coverage needed for the next step, how to recognize that results are repeating, and which gaps would prevent closing.
  8. What would change the plan: findings that would require other terms, places, question or scope.

  Rules:
  - Start by stating that this is a plan that has not been run.
  - Mark search terms, descriptors and search locations as unverified unless Ohtia lists them above as locations it can query.
  - Never invent authors, titles, results, numbers of results or access conditions.
  - Never assume that the full text of a work is available; a citation does not prove access.
  - An exploratory plan is not a systematic review; never describe it as exhaustive.
  - Finding no results in a place does not prove that no work exists; say so when relevant.
  - End with the questions that would help decide when the search is enough, and ask which subscription databases the researcher can access through their institution.
  - The plan is a proposal. The researcher decides what to search and what to include.
  ```

  In this version `{queryable_locations}` is "none in this version". Ohtia does not store the researcher's answer about institutional access; it stays in the conversation.

- **Policy `search_strategy_planning`, version 1.** The same values as `direct_guidance` version 1: `max_pending_tasks` 100; `max_input_bytes` 256 KiB and `max_input_items` 500; `max_output_bytes` 64 KiB; `max_work_units` 1; `max_attempt_duration_ms` 10 minutes; `max_inactivity_ms` 120 seconds; `stop_grace_ms` 5 seconds; `max_attempts` 2, `max_auto_retries` 0, `retry_backoff_ms` 0. A search plan is a text reply like Virgilio's; executing searches would need other limits and is excluded.
- **Model.** The first model of the account's list until STORY-003-035.
- **No tools.** Josefa answers with text only.
- **Excluded.** Running searches, reviewing candidates and reviewing coverage, which need search adapters, and storing the plan as a record.

**Documentation amendments:** `docs/thesis-workflow.md`, "Search strategy": "**Search locations:** pertinent and accessible databases, catalogs, repositories and reference/citation routes through enabled adapters." becomes "**Search locations:** pertinent and accessible databases, catalogs, repositories and reference/citation routes; a strategy marks those that enabled adapters can query as such and every other proposed location as unverified." The "Evaluation and enablement" section of `docs/agents/agent-josefa.md` already enables an operation once its adapter conformance and operational checks pass, as STORY-003-014 amends it, so this story does not change it.

**Decisions and rationale:** The name `search_strategy_planning` covers web, bibliographic and other searches because it names the strategy artifact. The search-adapter registry is not built now, because its declaration shape will come from the first real adapters and the instructions will need a new version then anyway; only the fixed input section is prepared.[^adr-0026]

**Verification:** With the fake provider: the reply is saved as Josefa's message; instructions carry the project data and the fixed locations section; a conversation over the input limit sends only recent messages with the note; the policy limits apply. Automated tests run in the 12 CI cells. With the researcher's account on the x86-64 virtual machines and the development command, a search-plan request in a Spanish thesis returns the eight parts, the unverified marks, the open-versus-subscription separation and the institutional-access question, with no invented author or title; the reply is shown to the researcher.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-026: Agent selector and direct use

Let the researcher choose which agent answers a new conversation.[^roadmap][^collaboration]

- **Column `conversations.agent_id`.** The agent that answers the conversation; required; new conversations default to `virgilio`; the migration sets `virgilio` on existing conversations. STORY-003-004's `conversation.start` now receives the agent with the first message; an agent without an enabled operation, such as `jorge-luis`, is rejected with the new error `agent_unavailable`, next action `check_request`, which saves nothing and which the selector's disabled options prevent, so the composer shows it as any other error (STORY-003-012). Changing it later is a handoff (STORY-003-029).
- **Reads.** `conversation.read` (STORY-003-010) and `conversation.list` (STORY-003-018) return each conversation's `agent_id` in place of the fixed `virgilio`, and `destination.read` (STORY-003-012) receives, for a conversation not created yet, the agent chosen in the selector.
- **Agent band.** A band right below the chat header shows the conversation's agent in every conversation, the setup conversation included: its static symbol and "{agent} · {role} · AI", where `{role}` is the agent's localized role. In a new, empty conversation the band shows the label "Talk to:" followed by the selector, whose value is the chosen agent, Virgilio by default; opening it lets the researcher choose another agent. With the first message the agent is fixed and the band shows it without the label or the selector. In a conversation whose agent is not Virgilio, the band also shows the "Continue with Virgilio" button of STORY-003-029.
- **Selector.** Its options show the agent's 20 px symbol, then the name and, on a second line, the role, and, for a disabled option, the reason on a third line in `type.ui.caption`; "Writing review" is a group heading above its two modes. The chosen option carries the `check` icon, the selection mark of the project selector (STORY-003-016). While the conversation is empty, its destination line comes from `destination.read` with the project and the chosen agent, read again each time the choice changes, so it names the operation limit and the connection of the agent that will answer. The setup conversation has no selector; Virgilio always answers there.
- **Listed agents.** The roles the roadmap names for the selector: Virgilio, the research guide; Josefa, the reference librarian; Jorge Luis, the analytical reader; Erasmus, the critical evidence reviewer; and one entry, "Writing review", a group in the selector that holds its two modes, Boris for academic editing and María for proofreading, as the roadmap names one writing-review role with two modes. Thomas does not appear. Jorge Luis, Erasmus and both writing-review modes are disabled with "Not available in this version.", without naming an alternative, because an unavailable role is never passed to another agent.
- **Routing.** Each message creates a task with the operation of the conversation's agent: `direct_guidance` for Virgilio, `search_strategy_planning` for Josefa, `project_setup` in setup.
- **Panel follows the agent.** The conversation's agent replaces Virgilio's name in every chat text of STORY-003-010 to STORY-003-013: the composer says "Message {agent}", a finished reply announces "{agent} replied", and the notices say "Waiting for {running_agent} to finish the current reply.", where `{running_agent}` is the agent whose task runs now in the conversation (STORY-003-013), and "{service} isn't connected. {agent} will reply when you connect." An empty Josefa conversation says "Ask Josefa about your search."; each history row shows, on its first line before the conversation's name, the compact `VoiceLabel` (STORY-003-018) of the agent that the row's `agent_id` names. Once a conversation exists, its agent is fixed; because `conversation.start` stores the conversation and its first message together, no conversation exists without its first message.
- **Keyboard and screen readers.** The selector follows the APG select-only combobox pattern, like the project selector (STORY-003-016), with "Writing review" as a group of its listbox; disabled options remain reachable and read their reason.
- **Excluded.** Changing the agent of a started conversation (STORY-003-029), Virgilio suggesting or consulting Josefa (STORY-003-022) and agent profile views.

**Measurements,** all expressed with existing tokens unless stated:

- Agent band: `space.2` padding, `space.4` at the start, a bottom `border.subtle` and 32 px controls; "Talk to:" in `type.ui.label` and `content.secondary`. The agent listbox is the chat's width minus `space.4` per side, aligned with the panel's padding; its options have `space.2` padding, a 20 px symbol and the reason in `type.ui.caption`.

**Documentation amendments:**
