Agents keep their current models; you can change them in Settings › Agents." The default never changes by itself, neither on connecting nor when the list refreshes.
- **Model list.** Requested on connection, on account change and with "Refresh {service} model list", one secondary button per connected service, such as "Refresh ChatGPT model list", so that no two buttons share a name, with the Lucide `list-restart` icon, vendored from the `lucide-static` 1.66.0 package pinned in `packages/renderer/icons/manifest.json`, with its hash in the manifest and the generated `design-icons.ts` and icon mappings updated, as STORY-003-016 vendors `graduation-cap`; `refresh-cw` is not used, because `design-system.md` maps it to "Requires review"; it runs main's `connection.probe` (STORY-003-001) and, while it runs, announces politely the status "Updating the model list from {service}…"; the section states its age: "Model list from {service}, updated {age}", where `{age}` is the time since the list was read, formatted with the interface locale's relative-time format in its short style, such as "5 min. ago" in `en-US`; relative times written in this EPIC are examples of that format, not catalog text. If it cannot be refreshed, choices are kept, and an attention notice with `triangle-alert` says "Ohtia couldn't update the model list. Your choices are kept."
- **Complete lists only.** Main treats a list as read only after it has read every page the service returns and parsed every entry: Anthropic's list is paged (STORY-003-036), while the list endpoints of Sign in with ChatGPT and of the OpenAI API document no pages, so one complete, parsed response is the whole list.[^siwc-models][^openai-models-list] A refresh that fails or stops before the end counts as a failed refresh and keeps the previous list, so a model counts as gone only when a complete list no longer offers it.
- **Retiring models.** Models that the service marks as deprecated or retired are not offered: an Anthropic model whose `lifecycle` is not `active` (STORY-003-036), and an OpenAI API model with a `shutdown_date` (STORY-003-037). Sign in with ChatGPT's list marks none.
- **Operations.** The section reads the preferences with the new read `agentPreference.list`, which also returns, for each connection, its stored list's `list_revision` and number of models, and saves a change with the new command `agentPreference.replace`; the first-connection question saves through the same command. Every select of this story that offers a connection's models, the first-connection question's included, reads that connection's complete stored list with the new read `providerModel.list`, given the connection, the `list_revision` held, a cursor (the next model's position, 0 for the first page) and a count of at most 128, which returns the models in the list's order under STORY-003-010's page rule, as many whole models as fit within every port bound, with the next cursor and whether more remain, or the existing `stale_revision` with `currentRevision` when the stored list has changed, after which the window reads again from the first page, and `operationErrorCodes` lists `stale_revision` for `providerModel.list`; the select reads every page before it shows the list. `research.subscribe` (STORY-003-010) also announces each change of the agent preferences, and each new stored model list with its connection and `list_revision`, as it does for the other records shared by every project, so every window, and every open card that names an agent's connection, reads them again.
- **When it applies.** "Changes apply to new replies. Replies already waiting keep their settings." Each task stores, when created, its service, model and effort and where they came from (an invocation choice, the agent's choice or the researcher's default); the order is invocation choice, then the agent's saved preference, then the researcher's default model, which every agent may inherit (amendment below). There are no invocation choices in the interface yet. A queued task keeps its settings; only the blocked tasks below resolve them again.
- **No usable model.** When a task is created or admitted and its agent resolves to no model at all, or to a model that the connection's complete list no longer offers, the task becomes `blocked` with `configuration_unavailable`, ADR-0011's code for a prerequisite missing before dispatch, and nothing is sent.[^adr-0011] Its notice (STORY-003-013) reads "{agent} has no model chosen. Choose one in Settings › Agents." or "{agent}'s model “{model}” isn't available in your account now. Choose another in Settings › Agents.", where `{model}` is the model's name, with the button "Open Agents settings". While a reply task of the conversation is blocked this way, the destination line (STORY-003-013) reads "{agent} has no usable model. Messages will wait."; on a plan card, a contribution or synthesis row whose task is blocked this way reads "Waiting: {agent} has no usable model" with "Open Agents settings" (STORY-003-028). "Continue all ({n})" and reconnection revalidate the model too: a model no longer offered leaves `configuration_unavailable`.
- **Resolving again.** When the researcher saves a preference, every reply task that it now governs and that is blocked with `configuration_unavailable` or `connection_disabled` resolves its service, model and effort again from the current preference, records the new source and is revalidated in its original order, so it starts without a new message once its connection and model are available. A contribution or synthesis task keeps the connection recorded when its plan or consultation was accepted (STORY-003-024): it resolves only its model and effort again. When the saved preference makes such a blocked task's agent resolve to another connection, the same transaction cancels that task, which never dispatched, and fails the work it was for with `destination_changed`, as an activation that finds the connection changed does (STORY-003-024): a plan's or a consultation's contribution becomes `failed` with that `failure_reason`, its dependents are blocked and a required one leaves the plan in "Needs your attention"; a synthesis's continuation takes the new `continuations.failure_reason` `destination_changed`, cleared by the `synthesis.tryAgain` that creates its next task. The plan row, or the consultation task's notice, then reads "Not sent: {agent} now uses a different service than the one you approved. Choose {service} for {agent} again in Settings › Agents, then try again.", where `{service}` is the connection recorded at acceptance, with "Try again": `contribution.tryAgain` and `synthesis.tryAgain` accept a task cancelled this way, and STORY-003-024's destination check rejects them with `destination_changed` until the agent resolves to that connection again. This story therefore changes two earlier rules: the rule of `contributions.failure_reason` (STORY-003-023), as Records states, and `task_not_retryable`, which `contribution.tryAgain` (STORY-003-022) and `synthesis.tryAgain` (STORY-003-026) return for a task that did not fail and now no longer return for a task cancelled this way, the one cancelled task they accept. A reply task's "Try again" also uses the current preference; the "Try again" of a contribution or synthesis keeps the connection recorded at acceptance (STORY-003-024). If the default's or an agent's service is disconnected, its reply tasks wait as in STORY-003-006 and their blocked notice, "{service} isn't connected. {agent} will reply when you connect.", adds "Choose another model in Settings › Agents." and the button "Open Agents settings"; choosing a model of a connected service there starts them. A contribution or synthesis task blocked by a disconnected service keeps its connection, so its notice has no such addition. Ohtia never moves an agent to another service by itself.
- **Upgrade.** A researcher connected before this story has no default model. The migration creates the default's row, without a model and with `seed_pending` set, only when at least one task exists, which shows that Ohtia sent replies before the upgrade; with no task it creates no row, so a new researcher's first connection asks the question above. While `seed_pending` holds, the first complete model list that main reports for a connected service sets the default to that list's first model, the one every reply used until now (STORY-003-005), with the source `migration`, and clears `seed_pending`, so replies keep working without a question; a default saved through `agentPreference.replace` also clears it.
- **Recorded.** The requested model and effort and the ones the service reports, or "Unknown", shown in "Details" (STORY-003-034), which this story extends with the terms "Requested effort" and "Observed effort"; "Requested effort" reads "Model default" when no effort was sent.
- **Policy.** Any listed model and any declared level are allowed, and every operation of this EPIC permits inheriting the researcher's default; quality is not graded per model, and the policy says so.
- **Records.** `agent_inference_preferences`: one row per agent and one for the default, with service, model, effort, inherit flag, revision, who chose it (the researcher, the first-connection question, or the upgrade, `migration`) and, on the default's row only, `seed_pending` (Upgrade); `provider_models`: each connection's complete list with its `list_revision`, which the data service raises by one, per connection, each time it stores a complete list that main reports, name, order, `effort_support` (`supported`, `unsupported` or `unknown`), for each level the service names whether it is `supported` or `unsupported` (a level it does not name is unknown and not offered), default level, maximum output when the service reports it, the origin of each datum (the service's list or Ohtia's catalog), date and the account fingerprint that main reported with the list (STORY-003-034), never an email; `tasks` gains the resolved service, model and effort and their source; `continuations` gains `failure_reason`, empty except as stated in "Resolving again"; and the rule of `contributions.failure_reason` (STORY-003-023) becomes: it is set while the contribution is `failed` without a task, or `failed` with `destination_changed` after "Resolving again" cancelled its task, and the retry that creates its next task clears it; the migration changes the database check to match. A consultation's contribution failed this way stays `failed` while its latest task is `cancelled`, the one case where a consultation's state is not the state of its current task (STORY-003-023).
- **Measurement at the highest effort.** Run the longest replies, such as a synthesis and a large plan, at the highest effort level that the account's ChatGPT list declares; it declares none (Effort above), so they run at the model default and no effort is sent. Measure their duration and the longest silence between provider events. STORY-003-036 and STORY-003-037 repeat this measurement at the highest level their models declare. Apply the measurement rule: over half the attempt duration raises the limit in a new version; a silence over 60 seconds, half of 120, raises `max_inactivity_ms` in a new version. Ohtia requests no reasoning summaries, so no provider reasoning text needs storing or a diagnostics review.

**Documentation amendments:** `docs/agents/agent-collaboration.md`, "Inference preferences and reproducibility": "Resolve an authorized invocation-specific choice first, then the contributor's saved preference, then the operation's declared default; runtime inheritance is allowed only when the operation policy explicitly permits it." becomes "Resolve an authorized invocation-specific choice first, then the contributor's saved preference, then the researcher's default model; a contributor inherits that default only when the operation policy permits it."; "Freeze the resolved configuration with accepted work. Preference changes affect subsequently accepted work, not queued contributions or a resumed session's immutable inputs." becomes "Freeze the resolved configuration with accepted work. Preference changes affect subsequently accepted work and accepted work that has not started because its connection or model is unavailable, which resolves again from the new preference before admission; they do not affect queued work, a contribution's authorized destination or a resumed session's immutable inputs."; and `docs/design-system.md`, "Iconography", fixed mappings: add the row "Refresh a model list | `list-restart`".

**Decisions and rationale:** Multica and Paperclip choose the runtime or adapter first and the model second, while OpenClaw uses one grouped picker with search, collapsible groups and access labels; the researcher judged a grouped list confusing, so Ohtia uses two steps. OpenClaw's automatic fallback to other models is rejected.[^multica][^paperclip][^openclaw] A missing model blocks the task instead of failing it, as ADR-0011 requires for a prerequisite that disappeared before dispatch, and the blocked task resolves again when the researcher fixes the preference, so the notice's advice works without a new message. No operation of this EPIC declares a model of its own, so the researcher's default is the third level. Unsupported and unknown effort are stored apart, as `agent-collaboration.md` requires.[^adr-0011][^collaboration]

**Verification:** Automated tests with two fake services: "Default model" and each agent with their own three selects; models with effort declared supported, declared unsupported and not declared, each with its control text; after a service change, "Choose a model" and the disabled effort with "Choose a model first."; one "Refresh {service} model list" per connected service with distinct names; the list age in the locale's relative-time format; a failed list keeps choices, and a list that fails on its second page keeps the previous list and marks no model gone; a stored list of 1,500 models reaches the selects through `providerModel.list` pages that each stay within every port bound, every model appears in order, and a page read after the list changed returns `stale_revision` and is read again from the first page; a model the service marks deprecated is not offered; an account change; a model disappearing before task creation and while queued blocks the task with `configuration_unavailable` and its notice, destination line and plan-row text, and choosing another model starts it; a reply blocked by a disconnected service starts after choosing a model of a connected one, while a contribution task keeps its connection and its notice offers no other model; saving a preference that moves an agent to another connection cancels its blocked contribution, consultation or synthesis task and fails that work with `destination_changed`, its row or notice shows the "Not sent" text with "Try again", the plan needs attention when the contribution is required, and "Try again" is rejected with `destination_changed` until the approved connection is chosen again, while the contribution keeps that `failure_reason` beside its cancelled task until the retry that creates its next task clears it; ChatGPT models store effort as `unknown`; the Settings texts show `{value}` as the select's label; "Refresh {service} model list" announces its progress; the second-service notice; "Try again"; queued tasks unaffected by preference changes; a model change that lacks the saved effort resets it to "Model default"; after an upgrade with stored tasks the first list sets the default with the source `migration` and no question, while a first connection without any task asks the first-connection question; `provider_models` stores the account fingerprint and no email; "Use the default" stored apart from a chosen model; changing service waits for a model choice; the first-connection question inline in the Connections row, below the state line and above the row's buttons, which stay available, with focus on "Model", the first model preselected, no dismiss button and no dialog over Settings, kept until a default model is saved and hidden while the connection is not `connected` or its model list has no model; "Refresh {service} model list" uses `list-restart`; `contribution.tryAgain` and `synthesis.tryAgain` accept a task cancelled with `destination_changed` and still reject any other cancelled task with `task_not_retryable`; "Details" shows requested and observed values; the section with keyboard, screen reader, 280 CSS px, German, Korean and pseudolocalization. They run in the 12 CI cells. With the researcher's account on the x86-64 virtual machines: see the real model list in order and record in the pull request the fields its entries carried; choose another model for Josefa, message her and see it in "Details"; run the measurement at the model default, raising only `max_inactivity_ms` when a silence exceeds 60 seconds.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-041: Anthropic API key

Add Anthropic as a second service through an API key from the Anthropic Console.[^anthropic-streaming][^anthropic-tools]

- **Connect** (Settings › Connections › "Add {service} API key", with `{service}` "Anthropic"). Every text of this connection takes `{service}` and the `api_key` variant (STORY-003-001), and STORY-003-037 reuses them.
  - Before asking for the key: "{service} charges this key's usage per token to the key's {service} account. It is separate from any subscription to {service}'s apps."
  - The field, labelled "{service} API key", masks the key and accepts pasting; "Save key" (primary), which Enter also runs, sends the key, and "Close key entry", which Esc also runs, clears and closes the field without sending anything. While main checks the key, the status "Checking the key with {service}…" is announced. The key passes through the window once, when saved, to main through `connection.connect` or the new `connection.replaceKey`; the field clears, and the key never returns to the window and never reaches the data service. Both are connection operations handled by main: idempotent by state, with no `command_receipts` row, so no value derived from the key reaches the database (STORY-003-001).
  - Main checks the key by listing models with its listing client (Requests, below): `GET /v1/models`, with `anthropic-version: 2026-10-21` and the key as `Authorization: Bearer`, the header that SDK 1.4.0 sends and that Anthropic documents for API keys, keeping `x-api-key` as a legacy alternative.[^anthropic-authentication][^anthropic-sdk-1-4-0] On success it stores in the operating-system credential store, as the entry `anthropic-api-key` (STORY-003-001), the JSON object `{"key": …, "instance": …}`, where `instance` is 32 random hexadecimal digits generated each time a key is stored; a key of about 100 bytes and its instance fit Windows' 2,560-byte limit. The instance is this connection's account input for the fingerprint of STORY-003-034: it identifies the stored key without being derived from it, so replacing the key changes the fingerprint and no value derived from the key reaches the data service. On failure nothing is stored, the field clears and a danger notice with `octagon-alert` says why: 401 "{service} rejected this key. Check that you copied all of it, or create a new key in your {service} account.", 403 "{service} didn't let this key see its models. Check the key's permissions in your {service} account.", no network "Ohtia couldn't reach {service}, so the key wasn't saved. Check your internet connection, then paste it again.", the workspace 400 (below) "This key isn't limited to one workspace. Create a key for a single workspace in your {service} account, then paste it.", and a 429, any 5xx response, 529 included, or any other status, such as another 400 or a 402, "{service} couldn't check the key right now. Try again later."
  - The workspace 400. Anthropic requires the `anthropic-workspace-id` header on every request made with a key that isn't scoped to a workspace, and answers a request without it with HTTP 400, an `error.type` of `invalid_request_error` and the `error.message` "This API key is not scoped to a workspace, so this request must include the anthropic-workspace-id header with the ID of the workspace to use. Add the header, or use an API key that is scoped to a workspace."[^anthropic-authentication] Ohtia never sends that header, and none of the five headers of main's `fetch` (Requests, below) carries it. Main recognizes this response, in the key check and in Messages requests, by all three parts: status 400, `error.type` `invalid_request_error` and an `error.message` that starts with "This API key is not scoped to a workspace". Any other 400 is not this response. Its message is never shown or stored.
  - The check's outcome. `connection.connect` and `connection.replaceKey` return at once, as `connection.connect` does for ChatGPT (STORY-003-001). While main checks the key, `connection.read` and its events carry `checking_key: true`; the connection is `connecting` after `connection.connect` and keeps its state after `connection.replaceKey`. Main ends a check without an answer after `connection_probe_ms`, 30 seconds. The result arrives through `connection.subscribe`: a check that passes stores the key and leaves the connection `connected` with an empty reason; a check that fails stores nothing and leaves the connection in the state it had before the operation, `disconnected` after `connection.connect`, and after `connection.replaceKey` the earlier state, whose stored key stays in use. A failed check carries a reason that this story adds to STORY-003-001's closed list and that selects the notice above: `key_rejected` (401), `key_not_permitted` (403), `key_needs_workspace` (the workspace 400), `key_check_unreachable` (no network, or no answer within `connection_probe_ms`) or `key_check_unavailable` (a 429, any 5xx or any other status). These reasons go with whatever state the connection keeps and live only in main's memory, like STORY-003-001's other reasons without a stored session, until the next `connection.connect`, `connection.replaceKey` or `connection.disconnect` or until Ohtia quits. `key_needs_workspace` also goes with the `expired` state that the workspace 400 sets on a Messages request (Errors before the stream, below), and lives in main's memory in the same way.
  - The row reads "{service} · API key · Connected {date}", where `{date}` is the day the key was stored, as a medium date in the interface locale, with "Test connection", which runs `connection.probe` as in STORY-003-002, announcing "Testing the connection with {service}…", and shows, in one status notice with `info` below the row's buttons, "Your {service} key works." and "Models: {models}", or, when the service cannot be reached, keeps the key and shows a danger notice with `octagon-alert`, "Ohtia couldn't reach {service}. Your key is kept; check your internet connection and test again."; "Replace key", which shows the masked field again, and "Disconnect {service}", which asks for confirmation and deletes the key from the store. The confirmation appears inline in the row, in place of its buttons, never as a dialog over Settings, because `workspace-layout.md` allows one modal at a time: a group named by its title "Disconnect {service}?" and described by its body "Ohtia deletes this key from this computer. Replies that would use {service} wait until you connect it again.", with the buttons "Keep connection", which has the initial focus and returns the row to its buttons, and "Disconnect {service}", in the destructive variant. The connection's identifier is `anthropic_api_key`.
  - While the key field is open, "Add {service} API key" is hidden; "Close key entry" brings it back. When the credential store is unavailable, the row's keyring notice is followed by "Add {service} API key".
  - The other rows are the `api_key` variants of STORY-003-002's rows: `disconnected` reads "Not connected", with "Add {service} API key"; `expired`, after a 401 (below), reads "{service} no longer accepts this key.", and with the reason `key_needs_workspace` "This key isn't limited to one workspace. Create a key for a single workspace in your {service} account, then paste it.", each with "Replace key"; and on Linux without a usable keyring the row reads "Ohtia keeps your {service} key in your system's keyring, which isn't available. Turn on or unlock GNOME Keyring or KWallet, then add the key again." The `expired` row and the keyring explanation are attention notices with `triangle-alert`.
  - Then the default-model question when it is the first service, or the notice when it is the second (STORY-003-035).
- **Models.** Anthropic's list gives each model's name, context, maximum output and effort support, which feed STORY-003-035's selector.[^anthropic-models-list]
  - Main reads the whole list: pages of `limit` 1000, each next one with `after_id` set to the previous page's `last_id`, while `has_more` is true. A list that stops before its last page is a failed refresh (STORY-003-035).
  - It offers only models whose `lifecycle` is `active`, by `display_name` and in the order received, and only those with a `max_tokens` value, because every request must state one and Ohtia does not guess it.
  - Effort: when `capabilities` is null, effort is unknown; when `effort.supported` is false, effort is unsupported; otherwise each of `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh` and `max` is stored as `supported` or `unsupported`, as its entry declares, and a level whose entry is null stays unknown and is not offered.
- **Requests.**
  - The official `@anthropic-ai/sdk`, Apache-2.0, recorded in a new ADR with the newest stable version that is at least 7 days old when the ADR is written, the same cooldown that `.github/dependabot.yml` sets for its ecosystems (on 2026-11-09 that is 1.4.0, published on npm on 2026-11-02), running in main, which holds the key. The data service builds each request without the key and dispatches it to main as in STORY-003-003; main runs the SDK call and forwards its events.
  - SDK 1.4.0 reads a setting from an environment variable whenever its option is left out, and always merges the headers of `ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS` into its default headers; its OpenTelemetry tracing is on by default and reads the `ANTHROPIC_OPEN_TELEMETRY*` variables, one of which puts request and reply content in its spans.[^anthropic-sdk-1-4-0] So that no adapter inherits configuration from the environment (SPEC-003-002), main passes every such option to each client it builds: `apiKey`, the stored key or the key being checked, so the SDK reads no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_PROFILE` or credential file; `baseURL` `https://api.anthropic.com`; `webhookKey` `null`; `maxRetries` 0, because ADR-0029 forbids hidden retries; `logLevel` `off`; `openTelemetry` `{ propagation: false, traces: false }`, so that the client starts no span and sends no trace context; `defaultHeaders` with `anthropic-version` (below); and `fetch`, main's own function.
  - SDK 1.4.0 merges the `ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS` lines into `defaultHeaders`, where only a name that main's own `defaultHeaders` also sets overrides them, and applies `defaultHeaders` after its own `Accept`, `User-Agent` and `anthropic-version` and after the `Authorization` it builds from `apiKey`, so a line of that variable can replace any of them.[^anthropic-sdk-1-4-0] Main's `fetch` functions therefore copy no header from the SDK's request: each sends exactly these five headers, with values that main sets, and no other, so that neither `ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS` nor a header that an SDK update adds ever leaves: `accept: application/json`, the value SDK 1.4.0 sends on every request, streamed ones included; `anthropic-version: 2026-10-21`; `authorization: Bearer` followed by the key that the client was built with; `content-type: application/json`, only on a request with a body, which SDK 1.4.0 always encodes as JSON; and `user-agent: Anthropic/JS 1.4.0`, the value SDK 1.4.0 builds, which main forms from the `VERSION` that `@anthropic-ai/sdk/version` exports. They also refuse any address whose origin and path are not `https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages` for a request, or `https://api.anthropic.com/v1/models` for the list, whose query carries its paging. Main builds two clients with these options. The request client's `fetch` checks the attempt's dispatch permit just before the request leaves, as ADR-0028 requires before the body is released through a transport hook, and refuses a second request under the same permit; with retries at 0, each attempt makes one request.[^adr-0028] The listing client serves the key check, `connection.probe` and every model-list read; its `fetch` checks no permit, because those requests belong to no attempt and carry no research content.
  - Every request, the model list included, sends `anthropic-version: 2026-10-21`, the latest version that Anthropic's versioning page lists, checked on 2026-11-10.[^anthropic-versioning] SDK 1.4.0 sends the same value, and main also sets it in the client's `defaultHeaders`, so that an SDK update cannot change it unnoticed. The SDK's ADR records this value beside the SDK version line, and a later version is adopted only by changing that ADR.
  - Each operation's instructions go in `system`.
  - Tools are named `ohtia_propose_plan`, `ohtia_update_memory` and so on, because Anthropic tool names allow only letters, digits, `_` and `-`; they use `strict: true`, `tool_choice` `auto` (current models reject forced tool use, which Ohtia does not need) and `eager_input_streaming: true`, the tool-definition property that turns on fine-grained input streaming, with Ohtia validating every input against its schema.[^anthropic-tool-reference]
  - History is sent as text, as with ChatGPT: earlier replies go in as their saved text, their tool calls are never replayed as `tool_use` blocks, so no `tool_result` is ever sent, and the state of each earlier proposal reaches the agent through the same English notes as with ChatGPT (STORY-003-014). Ohtia never answers a tool call within the same reply. Consecutive input items of the same role are joined into one message, so that the roles alternate.
  - `max_tokens` is the model's maximum output; Ohtia still cuts by each operation's bytes and time.
  - Effort goes in `output_config.effort`, only when chosen. No `thinking` parameter is sent.[^anthropic-effort]
- **Reasoning blocks.** Anthropic returns signed reasoning blocks that can be replayed only if the history is unchanged. Ohtia's history changes (memory updates, omitted old messages), so Ohtia neither stores nor replays them, one of the recoveries Anthropic documents; only the previous turns' internal reasoning is lost.[^anthropic-preserved-thinking]
- **In the reply.** Any event, including `ping`, counts as activity for the inactivity limit. Usage arrives at the start and end (`message_delta` usage is cumulative); Anthropic does not separate reasoning tokens, so that counter stays unknown. The reply's `stop_reason` decides how it ends:[^anthropic-stop-reasons]
  - `end_turn` and `tool_use` complete the reply, to which the `empty_reply` rule of STORY-003-005 and STORY-003-014 then applies.
  - `max_tokens` and `model_context_window_exceeded`, the service's length limits, fail with the new reason `max_output_reached`, keeping the text received as a partial message; the failed notice reads "The reply reached the maximum length {service} allows for this model." and the partial line "Incomplete — maximum length {service} allows". Such a reply is not counted as cut in "Details" (STORY-003-034): its "Usage" reads as for a complete reply, because the service reports its usage in full.
  - `refusal` fails with the new reason `refused` and the notice "{service} declined to answer this request."; text received before it is discarded, as Anthropic asks. The new `attempts.refusal_category` stores `stop_details.category` when it is a code of 1 to 64 lowercase letters, digits and underscores, and stays empty when it is null; "Details" shows "Refusal category: {category}" when one is stored. The `explanation` is never stored or shown.[^anthropic-refusals]
  - `stop_sequence` and `pause_turn` cannot occur, because Ohtia sends no stop sequences and no server tools; if one arrives, the reply fails with `invalid_output`.
  - A stream that ends without `message_stop`, or a connection that drops after the request left, is uncertain with `connection_lost`, as with ChatGPT.
- **Errors before the stream.**[^anthropic-errors][^anthropic-rate-limits]
  - 400 `invalid_request_error` whose message starts with "You have reached your specified API usage limits" or "You have reached your specified workspace API usage limits", the spending limit the researcher set in the Console: `billing_limit_reached` (below).
  - The workspace 400 (Connect): `connection_expired`, as a 401; the connection becomes `expired` with the reason `key_needs_workspace`, so its row shows "This key isn't limited to one workspace. Create a key for a single workspace in your {service} account, then paste it." with "Replace key", and the failed reply shows the `api_key` notice of `connection_expired`, "{service} no longer accepts this key. Replace it in Settings › Connections." (STORY-003-013).
  - Any other 400, a 409 and a 413: `request_rejected`; below STORY-003-013's "{service} rejected the request." the notice shows the second line "{service} said: {provider_message}", where `{provider_message}` is Anthropic's message as plain text with `lang=""`, held in memory only and never stored or written to diagnostics, so the line is absent after a restart.
  - 401 `authentication_error`: `connection_expired`; the connection becomes `expired`, and the notice shows its `api_key` variant, "{service} no longer accepts this key. Replace it in Settings › Connections." (STORY-003-013).
  - 402 `billing_error`: `billing_limit_reached`.
  - 403 `permission_error`: `not_eligible`, with its `api_key` notice (STORY-003-013).
  - 404 `not_found_error`, "The requested resource was not found": Ohtia treats it as a model that is no longer available, because the endpoint's path is fixed and the model is the only resource a request names: the new reason `model_unavailable`, with the notice "{service} couldn't find {agent}'s model “{model}”. Choose another in Settings › Agents, then try again.", where `{model}` is the model's name as in STORY-003-035, and the button "Open Agents settings"; main reads the model list again, so later tasks resolve as STORY-003-035 defines.
  - 429 `rate_limit_error` whose `error.details.error_code` is `enforced_spend_limit_reached`, the monthly spend cap of the account's tier, which carries no `retry-after` and keeps failing until access resumes: `billing_limit_reached`.
  - Any other 429: the new reason `rate_limited`, with the notice "{service} limited how many requests this key can send right now." and "Try again". When the response carries `retry-after`, the attempt stores the new `attempts.retry_not_before_at`, the time the 429 arrived plus those seconds, and the connection admits no queued task before the latest such time among its attempts, also after a restart, because the time is stored. The waiting reply shows "Waiting: {service} asked Ohtia to wait {wait}.", where `{wait}` is the whole seconds left in the interface locale's short unit format, such as "30 sec" in `en-US`, and meanwhile the destination line (STORY-003-013) reads "{service} asked Ohtia to wait. Messages will wait." Nothing retries by itself.
  - 500 `api_error`, 504 `timeout_error`, 529 `overloaded_error` and any other 5xx, and a connection that could not be opened: `provider_unavailable`.

  In the stream, an `error` event, such as `overloaded_error`, fails with `provider_unavailable`, keeping the text already received as partial (STORY-003-007). Messages that come with a 401 or a failed connection check are never shown or stored, because they may repeat part of the key.

- **Billing limit.** The new reason `billing_limit_reached` opens the connection's pause in `usage_limit_pauses`, which now also accepts `anthropic_api_key`: waiting and new tasks of the connection block with `usage_limit_reached` as in STORY-003-006. The failed reply, with "Try again", and the waiting ones show the `api_key` usage-pause notice "{service} says this key's account has no credit or reached its spending limit. Ohtia paused new requests to {service}." and the destination line "Requests to {service} are paused: no credit or spending limit reached. Messages will wait." (STORY-003-013), with "Resume {service} requests ({n})" for when it is resolved and no "Check usage" link, because this connection declares no usage address. Anthropic says that requests keep failing until the limit is raised or access resumes, so nothing retries by itself.[^anthropic-rate-limits]
- **No provider memory.** A Messages request carries the whole conversation it needs, and Ohtia sends no tool other than its own, no MCP server, no container and no file reference, so a request carries only what Ohtia built; the conformance suite checks every request for these fields.
- **Stop** closes the connection; nothing more arrives, and usage not reported before the stop stays unknown, so "Details" shows "Incomplete: {service} reported usage only up to the interruption." (STORY-003-034). The Messages API has no background mode or cancellation endpoint, so the closed-connection rule of STORY-003-007 applies to this adapter.
- **No late usage and no own concurrency limit:** the adapter declares `late_usage: none`, and Anthropic declares no simultaneous-request cap, so the general limit applies.
- **No automatic model change.** Anthropic's server-side refusal fallbacks stay off: Ohtia never sends the `fallbacks` parameter that turns them on, by STORY-003-035's decision and ADR-0029.[^anthropic-refusals]
- **Service texts.** Every notice, card and dialog already takes `{service}` and the variant of its kind of connection (STORY-003-001 and STORY-003-013), and STORY-003-015's no-connection dialog names no service, so this story adds no replacement texts: Anthropic's tasks show the `api_key` variants. It adds only the texts of its new reasons above.
- **Records.** `attempts.outcome_reason` gains `refused`, `rate_limited`, `max_output_reached`, `model_unavailable` and `billing_limit_reached`; `attempts` gains `refusal_category` and `retry_not_before_at`, both set only with their reason; `usage_limit_pauses.connection` also accepts `anthropic_api_key`.

**Documentation amendments:**

- A new ADR for the Anthropic SDK and its version line, stating that the SDK runs in main and recording the `anthropic-version` value 2026-10-21 that it sends.
- `docs/application-port.md`, "Scope and authority", as left by STORY-003-010: add `connection.replaceKey` to the list of connection operations; in "It has no credential, filesystem, SQL or subprocess authority", remove "credential, "; and add after that sentence "The connect and replace-key operations of an API-key connection carry the pasted key once, from the window to main, which never forwards it to the data service."

**Decisions and rationale:** Paperclip and Multica reach Anthropic through locally installed tools such as Claude Code with their own sessions; OpenClaw accepts Anthropic API keys directly and labels those models "API" in its picker, as Ohtia does.[^paperclip][^multica][^openclaw] The SDK runs in main, like every provider transport, so the key stays where the credential store is and never reaches the data service (STORY-003-001). History goes as text with English notes, as for ChatGPT, because Ohtia stores no `tool_use` identifiers or arguments and the proposal states change after the reply; replaying tool results would need both. A reached spending limit pauses the connection, as OpenAI's billing errors do (STORY-003-037), because Anthropic documents that retrying fails until access resumes.[^anthropic-rate-limits] Ohtia sends no `anthropic-workspace-id` header, so a key that isn't scoped to a workspace can make no request; Anthropic documents the 400 such a key receives, so Ohtia recognizes it by its status, type and message, names the cause and asks for a key scoped to one workspace instead of telling the researcher to try again, and a Messages request that receives it stops using the key, as after a 401.[^anthropic-authentication]

**Verification:** Automated tests with a fake Anthropic server: connecting with "Save key" and closing the field with "Close key entry" and Esc, "Add {service} API key" hidden while the field is open, "Disconnect {service}" confirmed inline in the row with focus on "Keep connection", which keeps the key, and no dialog opened over Settings, each connection error, including a 429, a 529, a 400, a 402 and the workspace 400 with Anthropic's documented body during the check, each with its reason and notice, a 400 `invalid_request_error` with another message giving `key_check_unavailable`, the workspace 400 on a Messages request failing the reply with `connection_expired` and leaving the connection `expired` with `key_needs_workspace` and its row text, and no request carrying `anthropic-workspace-id`, a list of 1,500 models over two pages of the fake server, read completely and reaching "Test connection" through `connection.listModels` pages and the selects through `providerModel.list` pages, each page within every port bound and every model in order, a check without an answer for 30 seconds, `checking_key` while a check runs, a failed `connection.connect` that leaves `disconnected` and a failed `connection.replaceKey` that keeps the earlier key and state, every `api_key` row and test result, and disconnecting; with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS`, `ANTHROPIC_LOG`, `ANTHROPIC_OPEN_TELEMETRY` and `ANTHROPIC_OPEN_TELEMETRY_TRACES_CONTENT_MODE=content` set, `ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS` holding `Authorization: Bearer` with another key, `User-Agent`, `Accept`, `Content-Type` and `anthropic-version` lines and one extra header, and a tracer provider registered, every request and list read uses the stored key and the fixed address, carries only the five allowed headers with main's values, a model-list read without `content-type`, logs nothing and starts no span; the model list and key check pass no permit check, while each Messages request does; a model list of three pages read to the end, a failure on its second page keeping the previous list, deprecated and retired models and models without `max_tokens` not offered, and effort null, unsupported and per level; every request carries `anthropic-version: 2026-10-21` and passes main's permit check in the SDK's `fetch`, and a second request under one permit is refused; requests with tools, with earlier replies as text and their proposals as English notes, never a `tool_use` or `tool_result` block, and same-role items joined; `ping` events; usage; each `stop_reason`, including `max_tokens`, `model_context_window_exceeded`, whose reply "Details" does not count as cut, and a refusal with and without a category, whose earlier text is discarded; each error of "Errors before the stream", with the provider's message as the second line of a `request_rejected` notice and absent after a restart, the billing pause from a 400 spending limit, a 402 and a spend-cap 429, the 404 reread of the list, and the wait after a 429 kept across a restart, with its destination line; Stop; the key absent from the window after saving, from diagnostics (with STORY-003-034's sweep), from every message to the data service and from the database; a repeated connect or replace-key writing no receipt; replacing the key changes the account line of "Details"; a plan with Virgilio on ChatGPT and Josefa on Anthropic. They run in the 12 CI cells. With the researcher's key on the x86-64 virtual machines, after the researcher sets a spending cap in the Anthropic Console: connect, see the models, move Josefa to a Claude model, converse with her, run a mixed plan, stop a reply and see the tokens in "Details"; run STORY-003-035's measurement of the longest replies at the highest effort level the chosen model declares, and apply the measurement rule.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-042: OpenAI API key

Add an OpenAI Platform API key as a third service, reusing the Responses transport of STORY-003-003.[^openai-models][^openai-errors][^openai-reasoning]

- **Connect** (Settings › Connections › "Add {service} API key", with `{service}` "OpenAI"), with STORY-003-036's `api_key` texts.
  - Before asking for the key: "{service} charges this key's usage per token to the key's {service} account. It is separate from any subscription to {service}'s apps."
  - The rest is as in STORY-003-036: masked field with "Save key" and "Close key entry", and the check's outcome, with `checking_key`, its failure reasons and their notices, except `key_needs_workspace`, which only Anthropic's workspace 400 sets; the `api_key` rows and test results; the key goes from the window to main only, through `connection.connect` or `connection.replaceKey`, and never reaches the data service; main checks it by listing models and stores it in the operating-system credential store as the entry `openai-api-key` (STORY-003-001), with its random `instance` as the account input of the fingerprint, as in STORY-003-036; the field clears and the key never returns to the window; connection operations record no `command_receipts` row; the row "{service} · API key · Connected {date}" with "Test connection", "Replace key" and "Disconnect {service}", with its inline confirmation in the row, "Keep connection" with the initial focus and the destructive "Disconnect {service}"; and the test result in one status notice with `info`. The connection's identifier is `openai_api_key`.
  - In STORY-003-035's selector, the `subscription` connection "ChatGPT · {email}" and the `api_key` connection "OpenAI · API key" are different services. OpenClaw keeps them apart too: "OpenAI API-key and ChatGPT/Codex subscription credentials remain distinct."[^openclaw]
- **Model catalog.** OpenAI's model list returns only identifiers, without names, capabilities or effort levels, and mixes text models with audio, image and embedding models. Ohtia therefore ships a catalog of text models documented for the Responses API, with their names and effort levels, checked on 2026-11-09 on their model pages:
  - GPT-6 Astra (`gpt-6-astra`): `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh` and `max`;
  - GPT-6.1 Sol (`gpt-6.1-sol`): `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh` and `max`;
  - GPT-6.1 Terra (`gpt-6.1-terra`): `none`, `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh` and `max`;
  - GPT-6 Luna (`gpt-6-luna`): `none`, `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh` and `max`.

  No model lists `minimal`. The model pages of Sol, Terra and Luna give `medium` as the default and Astra's page states none; OpenAI's reasoning guide adds that Astra rejects `none` with HTTP 400.[^openai-model-astra][^openai-model-sol][^openai-model-terra][^openai-model-luna][^openai-reasoning]

  The catalog lists the models in this order, and "Model default" sends no effort.

  Ohtia shows only catalog models that the key can use according to the account's complete list and that the list does not mark with a `shutdown_date`, OpenAI's announced shutdown of a model, with the line "Models Ohtia hasn't checked yet aren't listed."[^openai-models-list] Each datum shows its origin: "Model details from Ohtia's catalog (checked {date}). Availability from your {service} account, updated {age}.", where `{date}` is the catalog's check date, 2026-11-09, as a medium date in the interface locale, such as "9 Nov 2026" in `en-GB`, and `{age}` is as in STORY-003-035. A new OpenAI model reaches the catalog in a new Ohtia version.

- **Requests.** Main sends them, with the transport of STORY-003-003, to `https://api.openai.com/v1` with the key as a Bearer token and `tool_choice` `auto`; the data service builds them without the key; as with ChatGPT: `store: false`, streaming, instructions apart from the conversation history, and tools grouped in the `ohtia` namespace, a tool type that the `tools` parameter of the Responses API accepts.[^openai-responses-create] Effort goes in `reasoning.effort`, only when chosen. No output maximum is sent; Ohtia cuts by bytes and time. As with ChatGPT, a request never carries `previous_response_id`, `conversation`, `prompt` or a tool other than Ohtia's own, so it neither reads nor writes provider-kept state (STORY-003-003), and main checks its dispatch permit in the shared transport.
- **Usage.** All four counters arrive, including reasoning tokens.
- **Closed connection.** Ohtia never uses background mode, the mode whose responses have a cancellation endpoint, `POST /v1/responses/{id}/cancel`, and whose response data OpenAI stores for roughly 10 minutes so that it can be polled; a response outside it is cancelled only by closing its connection. So no cancellation endpoint applies and the closed-connection rule of STORY-003-007 holds for this adapter as for ChatGPT.[^openai-background]
- **In the stream.** The OpenAI API maps each stream outcome this way, replacing for this connection the stream rule of STORY-003-003's transport:[^openai-responses-create]
  - `response.completed` completes the reply.
  - `response.incomplete` whose `incomplete_details.reason` is `max_output_tokens` fails with STORY-003-036's `max_output_reached`, keeping the text received as a partial message, with that story's notice and partial line, and "Details" does not count it as cut.
  - Any other `response.incomplete` (`incomplete_details.reason` `max_messages`, `content_filter` or `steered`), `response.failed` and an `error` event fail with `provider_error`, keeping the text received as partial, as STORY-003-003's transport does.
  - An event or output that Ohtia cannot parse fails with `invalid_output`.
  - A stream that ends without `response.completed`, `response.failed` or `response.incomplete`, or a connection that drops after the request left, is uncertain with `connection_lost`.
- **Errors.**[^openai-errors]
  - 401, in any of its documented forms: `connection_expired`; the connection becomes `expired`, and the notice shows its `api_key` variant, "{service} no longer accepts this key. Replace it in Settings › Connections." (STORY-003-013).
  - 403, which OpenAI documents for a country, region or territory it does not support: `not_eligible`, with its `api_key` notice (STORY-003-013).
  - 404, which OpenAI's errors page does not describe: Ohtia treats it, as for Anthropic, as a model that is no longer available to the key, because the endpoint's path is fixed and the model is the only resource a request names: `model_unavailable`, with STORY-003-036's notice and a new read of the model list.
  - A 429 with a billing code (`credit_balance_exhausted`, `organization_spend_limit_exceeded`, `project_spend_limit_exceeded` or `organization_usage_limit_exceeded`), for which OpenAI says retrying does not help: STORY-003-036's `billing_limit_reached`, which opens the connection's pause in `usage_limit_pauses`, which now also accepts `openai_api_key`; waiting and new tasks block with `usage_limit_reached` as in STORY-003-006; the destination line and the notice show their `api_key` variants of STORY-003-013, "Requests to {service} are paused: no credit or spending limit reached. Messages will wait." and "{service} says this key's account has no credit or reached its spending limit. Ohtia paused new requests to {service}.", with "Resume {service} requests ({n})" for when it is resolved and no "Check usage" link, because this connection declares no usage address.
  - Other 429s: `rate_limited`, waiting OpenAI's `Retry-After` when given, stored in `attempts.retry_not_before_at` with the same waiting texts as in STORY-003-036.
  - 503 (`server_is_overloaded`), 500 and a connection that could not be opened: `provider_unavailable`; errors in the stream follow "In the stream" above.
  - 400 and any other 4xx: `request_rejected`, with OpenAI's message as the notice's second line "{service} said: {provider_message}", as in STORY-003-036: plain text with `lang=""`, held in memory only, never stored or written to diagnostics.

  Messages that come with a 401 or a failed connection check are never shown or stored, because they may repeat part of the key.

- **Undocumented points.** OpenAI documents no keep-alive events, so the measurement rule checks the longest silence as with ChatGPT; nor does it say whether generation stops or is billed after the client disconnects, so a stopped reply shows usage as unknown with STORY-003-013's usage line of a stopped reply, below "Incomplete — stopped by you" when text was saved or "Stopped." when none was, the same text for every service, and its "Details" reads "Not reported by {service}" for "Usage" and "Unknown" for each token count.
- **No late usage and no own concurrency limit:** the adapter declares `late_usage: none`, and the general limit applies.
- **Records.** `usage_limit_pauses.connection` also accepts `openai_api_key`; this story adds no outcome reason, because STORY-003-036 already added every one it uses.

**Decisions and rationale:** The catalog shows only verified models, because showing every listed identifier would offer models that fail; the cost is that new models need an Ohtia update. It reuses the Responses transport of STORY-003-003, so no library is added.

**Verification:** Automated tests with a fake OpenAI server: connecting; disconnecting through the inline confirmation with focus on "Keep connection"; the catalog crossed with the account and the hidden models, including a catalog model with a `shutdown_date`; requests with tools and none of the provider-state fields; usage; each stream outcome of "In the stream", including a `response.incomplete` for `max_output_tokens` as `max_output_reached` and one for `content_filter` as `provider_error`; a failed key check with each reason; each error, a 401 with its `api_key` notice, a 403 as `not_eligible`, a 404 as `model_unavailable` with a new read of the list, the billing pause with its `api_key` notice and destination line and "Resume {service} requests ({n})", and the wait after a 429; Stop, with saved text and without, and "Details" of each stopped reply opened from its message or its notice; the key absent from the window, diagnostics, every message to the data service and the database; a plan with Virgilio on ChatGPT and Josefa on the OpenAI API. They run in the 12 CI cells. With the researcher's key on the x86-64 virtual machines, after the researcher sets a spending cap in their OpenAI account: connect, see the models, move Josefa to the API, converse with her, stop a reply after its text starts and see the "Incomplete — stopped by you" notice with its usage line and, in "Details", "Not reported by OpenAI" and "Unknown" token counts; run STORY-003-035's measurement of the longest replies at the highest effort level the chosen catalog model declares, and apply the measurement rule.

**Open questions:** None.

### SPEC-003-043: Reference specimen and captures

Agreed at the mockup review:[^mockups] the approved interface of this EPIC has a renderable reference specimen, [epic-003-interface-specimen.html](../../assets/epic-003-interface-specimen.html), and 50 captures generated from it. They illustrate SPEC-003-006, SPEC-003-007, SPEC-003-015 to SPEC-003-021, SPEC-003-023, SPEC-003-026 to SPEC-003-028 as they appear on screen and SPEC-003-031 to SPEC-003-042, with synthetic en-US content: a thesis titled "Urban heat islands and school attendance in Buenos Aires", written in English, with Virgilio and Josefa. Precedence: when a capture or the specimen disagrees with the text of this EPIC, the text prevails and the disagreement is reported as a defect in the reference. The specimen is reference material, not product code: implementers build the real components from the canonical tokens and do not copy its HTML, CSS or JavaScript. It starts from EPIC-002's reference specimen and keeps its tokens, fonts, title bar, panels, dialogs and notices; its CSS custom properties are named after the tokens they stand for, such as `--surface-panel` for `surface.panel`. It loads IBM Plex Sans, IBM Plex Mono and IBM Plex Sans KR from the pinned commit cited in `design-system.md` only to render itself; product fonts follow ADR-0025. Its icons are Lucide SVGs from `lucide-static` 1.66.0, those already vendored in `packages/renderer/icons/` and the others at the same version, with the Lucide license notice in the file; each story vendors the icons it introduces. The contributor symbols are the `ContributorSymbol` paths of `packages/renderer/src/contributor-identity.tsx`. Model names, account emails, provider messages and research text in the captures are samples.

Open the specimen with `?screen=<id>` to show one screen, `&variant=<name>` for a screen with several frames, `&theme=dark` for the dark theme and, on the `conversation` screen only, `&lang=de` or `&lang=ko` for German or Korean interface strings. The screens are `conversation`, `composer`, `notices`, `narrow`, `setup` (variants `main`, `states`), `new-project` (`main`, `none-open`, `first`, `palette`, `entry`), `project-selector` (`main`, `states`), `history` (`main`, `states`), `narrow-history`, `agent-selector` (`main`, `states`), `consultation` (`main`, `states`), `plan` (`main`, `stop`, `proposal`, `states`, `synthesis`), `plan-change`, `handoff` (`main`, `return`, `return-never`, `states`), `narrow-plan`, `memory` (`main`, `states`), `details` (`main`, `states`), `connections` (`main`, `states`), `agents-settings` (`main`, `states`) and `attention` (`main`, `a`, `b`, `c`, `states`); without a variant a screen shows `main`. The German and Korean strings are layout samples translated for the specimen, not catalog text, except the strings that its script's `I18N` table marks "catalog", which come from the `de-DE` and `ko-KR` catalogs; research content stays in English. Every state sheet starts with the line "Each box is a separate situation; only one appears at a time.", and a box that lists alternatives captions each one "Situation {n} of {m}"; a sheet never shows states that appear together unless its box says so.

Each capture was produced with Chromium's headless shell at device scale factor 1, an 8-second virtual-time budget for font loading, hidden scrollbars and a window exactly the capture's size, given below as width × height in CSS px. State sheets are as tall as their content, so their whole content is visible; in the application, long dialogs and panels scroll internally. A change to the specimen regenerates every affected capture in the same change.

**Location.** The specimen and its 50 captures live in `docs/assets/`, beside `epic-002-interface-specimen.html`, as EPIC-002's reference does; the specimen links Ohtia's illustration as `ohtia/illustration.png`. No copy is kept in this EPIC's directory.

The measurements that the earlier blocks left implicit live in the "Measurements" list of the block that owns each screen: the timeline and message actions in SPEC-003-015, Markdown in SPEC-003-016, the composer in SPEC-003-017, notices in SPEC-003-018, `ActionProposalCard` in SPEC-003-020, the project selector in SPEC-003-021, the chat header, history rows and rename field in SPEC-003-023, the agent band in SPEC-003-026, plan rows in SPEC-003-032 and SPEC-003-033 and the handoff in SPEC-003-034.

## Affected components

- `comp:llm`: provider adapters, operation policies and agent instructions.
- `comp:storage`: research tables, migrations and command receipts.
- `comp:app`: chat panel, Settings sections and notices.
- `comp:desktop`: credential store, provider requests from main, application exit and the application port.
- `comp:tooling`: the development command's switches in the workspace `dev` command.
- `comp:docs`: canonical documentation amendments.

Every dependency but two is projected as a native blocked-by relationship because each consumer builds directly on the producer's merged code and schema. The exceptions are completion gates, documented as acceptance criteria instead of native edges. STORY-003-008 → STORY-003-019: STORY-003-019 builds and merges its Settings field against STORY-003-005's queue, and only its check that raising the limit does not resume paused tasks needs STORY-003-008's pauses. STORY-003-031 → STORY-003-032: STORY-003-032 builds on STORY-003-030, and only its `direct_guidance` version 6 values must also apply the measurement rule to the replies that STORY-003-031 recorded.

## Execution order

Every story builds on the EPIC-001 and EPIC-002 deliveries already on the base branch (the application port, local state, menus, Settings and the workspace); they are not listed as dependencies because their outputs already exist.

- STORY-003-001 and STORY-003-004 can start at once.
- STORY-003-002 follows STORY-003-001.
- STORY-003-003 follows STORY-003-001 and STORY-003-002.
- STORY-003-005 follows STORY-003-003 and STORY-003-004.
- STORY-003-006 and STORY-003-007 follow STORY-003-005 and can run in parallel.
- STORY-003-008 follows STORY-003-006 and STORY-003-007.
- STORY-003-009 follows STORY-003-007 and STORY-003-008.
- STORY-003-010 follows STORY-003-005.
- STORY-003-011 follows STORY-003-010 and can run in parallel with STORY-003-012.
- STORY-003-012 follows STORY-003-010 and STORY-003-007.
- STORY-003-013 follows STORY-003-012, STORY-003-008, STORY-003-009 and STORY-003-011.
- STORY-003-014 follows STORY-003-005 and STORY-003-007, whose partial-message notes and inactivity limit it uses, and can run in parallel with the chat panel stories.
- STORY-003-015 follows STORY-003-013 and STORY-003-014.
- STORY-003-016 and STORY-003-017 follow STORY-003-015 and can run in parallel.
- STORY-003-018 follows STORY-003-017, STORY-003-016 and STORY-003-011.
- STORY-003-019 follows STORY-003-005, STORY-003-007, whose closed-connection rule frees an uncertain attempt's slot for scenario 10, and STORY-003-010, whose research subscription announces limit changes to every window, and can run in parallel with STORY-003-011 to STORY-003-018; its check on paused tasks completes once STORY-003-008 is merged.
- STORY-003-020 follows STORY-003-017.
- STORY-003-021 follows STORY-003-020 and STORY-003-018.
- STORY-003-022 follows STORY-003-021 and STORY-003-011.
- STORY-003-023, STORY-003-024, STORY-003-025 and STORY-003-026 follow each other in that order.
- STORY-003-027 follows STORY-003-026.
- STORY-003-028 follows STORY-003-027, whose plan card it extends, and can run in parallel with STORY-003-029.
- STORY-003-029, STORY-003-030 and STORY-003-032 follow STORY-003-027 in that order, because each raises the `direct_guidance` instruction version; STORY-003-030 also follows STORY-003-028, whose plan rows and their states its change card shows.
- STORY-003-031 follows STORY-003-028, whose plan states it marks, and can run in parallel with STORY-003-029, STORY-003-030 and STORY-003-032; STORY-003-032 closes only after STORY-003-031 is merged with its reply measurements, which `direct_guidance` version 6 takes into account.
- STORY-003-033 follows STORY-003-032.
- STORY-003-034 follows STORY-003-031 and STORY-003-033, whose memory commands complete the paths its sweep covers.
- STORY-003-035, STORY-003-036 and STORY-003-037 follow STORY-003-034 in that order.

The graph for these stories is acyclic. The task engine (STORY-003-005) is the main bottleneck; the provider and storage foundations before it run in parallel.

Four boundaries keep each story within one pull request with one commit. STORY-003-001 delivers the adapter contract, the fake provider and the credential store on their own, because Sign in with ChatGPT (STORY-003-002), ChatGPT requests and both API-key connections build on them. STORY-003-010 delivers the conversation view with plain text, the port, streaming and paging, and STORY-003-011 adds Markdown, "Copy message" and links beside the composer, because only the notices, the history and the consultation card need that output. STORY-003-027 delivers the plan proposal, its card, "Run plan" and "Decline plan" and the notes to Virgilio, and STORY-003-028 the progress rows, "Stop plan", "Finish plan" and the state icons, so that the handoff (STORY-003-029) can start from `direct_guidance` version 3 while the progress is built. STORY-003-032 delivers the agent side of the project memory, its records, `update_memory`, what each agent receives and "Undo memory changes", and STORY-003-033 the researcher side, the "Project memory" tab, the entry form, the researcher's commands and the history, because both sides need the records and the tool that the agent side writes.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Every story in this EPIC is merged with its automated tests passing in the 12 CI cells and its real-account checks recorded.
- [ ] Direct specialist use and Virgilio-mediated work retain attribution and work with inference concurrency one.
- [ ] Unknown usage, model and effort remain unknown everywhere they are shown or stored.
- [ ] Credentials stay in main: they never reach the data service, and never appear in the window after entry, in research records (including the SQLite database, its write-ahead log and the ADR-0010 checkpoints) or in diagnostics.
- [ ] Every amendment in SPEC-003-005 is merged in its owning story.
- [ ] Deterministic collaboration scenarios 1 to 12 and 15 to 20 of `agent-collaboration-evaluation.md` pass at the module boundary; scenarios 13 and 14 need a manuscript editor, which this EPIC does not deliver.
- [ ] Workspace scenarios V07, V08, V10, V13, V16, V17 and V18 pass for the delivered chat, and V14 passes except archiving, deleting and generated titles, which this EPIC does not have; V15's grouping of repeated reads has nothing to group because no agent has read tools in this EPIC.
- [ ] Waiting coordination consumes no inference slot, and duplicate ready events never create repeated synthesis.
